PDF WORKBENCH — CURRENT CONTINUATION UPDATE — 2026-09-24 — BASELINE 5.8.12-exp

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER OLDER STATUS/TEST PRIORITY TEXT BELOW.
Continue future work from the packaged 5.8.12-exp ZIP, not an older build.

CURRENT VALIDATED BASELINE
- 5.8.11 graph Select-resize/content-scaling behavior was used in class on 2026-09-24. User reports manipulating a graph with handwritten/labeled nodes went perfectly.
- Editable classroom backup inspection showed intact semantic graph relationships (nodes, attached edges, and node-owned ink) after real use. Treat 5.8.11 graph resizing/content behavior as classroom-validated unless a new regression appears.
- Intermittent iPad Pen nonresponse remains a separate watch item. Several 2026-09-24 incidents recovered immediately after tapping Diagnostics; saved snapshots showed no active stuck gesture/contact, no render anomaly, and no event-loop stall. Do not rewrite Pen or graph code from those incidents alone.

5.8.12-exp SEMANTIC HANDWRITTEN EDGE LABELS

GOAL / USER REQUIREMENTS
- Edge labels must remain attached to their edge when endpoint nodes move or resize.
- Labels must be repositionable along the edge and perpendicular/across the edge so graphs can be visually cleaned up.
- Reuse mature Pen/Highlighter/Eraser behavior and avoid reintroducing expensive exact Highlighter rendering during drag/resize.

SEMANTIC MODEL
- Adds persisted annotation type `graph-edge-label`.
- Each label owns one `edgeId`, normalized `position` along the current visible straight edge (0..1), signed perpendicular `offset`, and `contents: []` of owned Pen/Highlighter annotation IDs.
- Label handwriting remains ordinary top-level Workbench ink for rendering/export/eraser compatibility. Child strokes store `graphEdgeLabelId` and `graphLabelLocalPoints` relative to the current semantic label anchor.
- One label per edge is intentional in this first milestone.
- Label writing remains upright; changing edge direction moves the anchor but does not rotate the handwriting.

CREATE / EDIT
- Selecting exactly one edge in Graph shows an Add/Edit Label action. If no label exists, it creates one near the midpoint with a modest perpendicular offset and enters the existing attached-content editor.
- Selecting the label itself exposes the same Edit Label action.
- Label editing reuses Pen, Highlighter, restricted Eraser, Clear Contents, Move Contents, and Done.
- A new stroke must begin near the highlighted label region, then can extend normally.
- Move Contents changes handwriting placement inside the semantic label without moving the label's edge anchor.

POSITION / PRETTIFY
- Graph -> Move can hit the label handwriting/region as one semantic object.
- Dragging is projected into two stored values: position along the edge and signed perpendicular offset.
- Therefore the label can slide toward either endpoint and can be moved to either side of the edge without detaching.
- Position is clamped to the visible edge segment; perpendicular offset is free.

FOLLOW / TRANSFORM BEHAVIOR
- Moving either endpoint node with Graph Move makes attached edge labels follow the new edge geometry.
- Fixed Graph node-size changes and node auto-fit also reposition label handwriting to the new semantic anchor without unexpectedly scaling it.
- Select move/resize extends the 5.8.11 lightweight transform path with isolated per-label temporary canvases.
- If both endpoints of a labeled edge are in one proportional Select resize, the label's perpendicular offset and handwriting scale with the graph.
- If only one endpoint changes, the label follows the reshaped edge but retains its own handwriting size and perpendicular offset.
- Rotate Selection moves label anchors with rotated endpoint nodes while keeping handwriting upright.
- Page-fit/page-geometry transforms preserve label attachment and scale label offset/handwriting consistently.

PERFORMANCE
- Edge-label handwriting is isolated on `live-graph-label-canvas` layers during Graph Move and graph-containing Select transforms.
- Pointer movement CSS-transforms these lightweight canvases while semantic graph geometry redraws on the graph layer.
- Exact Pen/Highlighter point geometry commits once on release, following the same performance principle validated for node Graph Move and Select resize.
- Cancel and Diagnostics recovery remove transient label layers and restore the pre-gesture snapshot when necessary.

RELATIONSHIP CLEANUP / PERSISTENCE
- Deleting an edge deletes its attached edge label and label-owned ink.
- Deleting a node deletes incident edges plus their labels/label-owned ink.
- Ordinary Select deletion uses the same relationship-aware cleanup.
- Undo/Redo snapshots clone label contents and local metadata.
- Local Library/editable backup persistence rides the existing page annotation storage.
- PDF export needs no new text renderer: label-owned Pen/Highlighter exports through the existing ink path; semantic label selection chrome is not exported.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 5.8.12
1. Create two nodes + edge; select the edge -> Add Label; write a short Pen label and Done.
2. Graph -> Move the label along the edge, then drag it across to the opposite side. Confirm handwriting moves as one unit and remains upright.
3. Move either endpoint node. Confirm the label follows the edge while preserving its along-edge position and offset.
4. Resize both endpoint nodes together with Select. Confirm label offset + handwriting scale smoothly with the whole graph and do not jump on release.
5. Resize/move only one endpoint. Confirm label follows the reshaped edge without changing handwriting size.
6. Repeat with Highlighter and mixed Pen + Highlighter label contents; drag/resize should remain smooth.
7. Use Edit Label -> Eraser, Clear Contents, Move Contents, Undo/Redo.
8. Change a node's fixed size and exercise an auto-fit node; confirm the edge label follows.
9. Rotate a selected labeled subgraph; label location should rotate with the edge while handwriting stays upright.
10. Delete label only; recreate it. Delete its edge; label + owned ink should disappear. Delete an endpoint node; incident labels should disappear with their edges.
11. Save/reopen or editable-backup round trip and export PDF; label relationships and visible ink should persist.
12. Regression-check classroom-validated 5.8.11 node-content resize, 5.8.10 Graph Move, and 5.8.9 restricted Eraser.

NEXT GRAPH WORK AFTER EDGE-LABEL VALIDATION
- Relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste using the already-agreed self-contained-fragment semantics.
- Later: curved/parallel edges, loops, directed edges/arrowheads, then further graph conveniences.

5.8.11-exp SMOOTH GRAPH RESIZE + SCALE NODE-OWNED HANDWRITING

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
- User reported that Select-handle resizing of graphs was slow/jumpy and that handwritten node contents could overflow their frames while shrinking.
- 5.8.10 had already proven the lightweight temporary-layer approach for Graph -> Move, but `prepareSelectionGestureLayers()` still explicitly rejected graph-containing Select transforms.
- Existing graph resize semantics moved node-owned ink with the node center but deliberately did not scale the handwriting, leaving the content-transform question unresolved.

WHAT 5.8.11 CHANGES
- Graph-containing Select move/resize gestures now use the existing `live-selection-canvas` optimization instead of forcing a full exact annotation redraw on every pointer sample.
- The live layer contains ordinary selected ink/images plus Pen/Highlighter contents owned by selected graph nodes. Those drawable objects are excluded from the frozen persistent annotation/image layers during the gesture.
- During graph resize, the live layer is CSS-scaled around the original selection anchor. Semantic graph-node geometry is updated cheaply so node borders and incident edges stay live; selection handles are redrawn from the current semantic geometry rather than blindly CSS-scaling past the node minimum.
- At release, owned Pen/Highlighter point arrays and stroke widths are scaled exactly once around the same selection anchor. `graphLocalPoints` ownership metadata is then rebuilt by the existing finish path before persistence/Undo.
- If a lasso contains both a graph node and one of its child strokes, the child is transformed once through node ownership rather than being double-transformed as an independent selected stroke.
- The existing 16-unit node-frame minimum is preserved. Once a frame reaches 16, its center can continue moving inward with the graph and its handwriting continues scaling with the overall selection. This prevents overflow without allowing one handwritten label to establish a larger minimum size for the whole graph.
- The safety fallback used when the temporary preview cannot be prepared now scales owned contents too, so optimized and fallback paths have the same final semantics.
- Pointer cancel and Diagnostics recovery restore the pre-gesture snapshot when the graph-optimized preview has mutated temporary semantic geometry.

PRESERVED
- 5.8.10 lightweight Graph -> Move with attached ink.
- 5.8.9 isolated live restricted node-content Eraser preview.
- Exact static Pen/Highlighter rendering and Highlighter bead-prevention semantics.
- 5.8.8 customizable Presentation toolbar.
- 16-unit graph-node minimum, graph spacing compression after minimum, edge attachment, Undo/Redo, persistence, and export.

TEST PRIORITY
1. Make a node with Pen contents and Select-resize it smaller/larger. The writing and stroke width should scale with the node rather than overflow.
2. Repeat with Highlighter and mixed Pen + Highlighter contents; drag should remain smooth.
3. Shrink far enough for the node frame to hit the 16-unit minimum. The frame should stop shrinking, graph spacing should continue compacting, and the handwriting should keep scaling smaller.
4. Resize several connected nodes together and confirm edges stay attached/live during the drag and all owned contents land exactly after release.
5. Repeat with a mixed selection containing graph nodes plus ordinary ink and/or an image.
6. Undo/Redo the resize; save/reopen and confirm geometry/content ownership persists.
7. Cancel a resize if convenient and confirm the original graph/content geometry is restored.
8. Regression-check 5.8.10 Graph Move and 5.8.9 restricted node Eraser.

NEXT GRAPH WORK
- Edge labels remain the next major feature. Reuse the same semantic container/content transform approach so label-owned handwriting can later follow/scale with its container without another transform redesign.
- After edge labels, relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste remains the next larger semantic milestone.

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-23 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.10-exp

5.8.10-exp LIGHTWEIGHT GRAPH MOVE FOR NODES WITH ATTACHED INK

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
- User reported that moving a graph node became slow/jumpy once the node contained Highlighter.
- Diagnosis: Graph Move called a complete `redrawPageAnnotationOverlays()` on every pointer move. Exact Highlighter redraw allocates/clears a full-resolution scratch canvas and composites translucent strokes, so the drag path repeatedly paid full-page Highlighter rendering cost.

WHAT 5.8.10 CHANGES
- At Graph Move start, attached node contents are rendered once to the temporary live-selection canvas and excluded from the frozen ordinary annotation layer.
- During drag, the temporary content canvas moves using CSS translation. The semantic node x/y still updates continuously, but only `redrawPageGraphOverlays()` runs per move so node border and incident edges remain live.
- Attached ink point arrays are NOT regenerated on every move in the optimized path. They are translated exactly once at release from the saved original geometry.
- Release performs one exact full annotation redraw, then persists/records Undo normally.
- A node with no attached ink also avoids full annotation redraw during drag.
- If temporary preview setup fails on an unusual/unrendered stage, the old correct full-redraw path remains as a safety fallback.
- Cancel and diagnostic recovery remove any temporary content layer and restore authoritative state.

PRESERVED
- 5.8.9 isolated node-content Eraser live feedback.
- Highlighter exact static rendering and bead-prevention semantics.
- 5.8.8 customizable Presentation toolbar.
- Graph edge attachment semantics, node contents, Undo/Redo, persistence, and export.

TEST PRIORITY
1. Create/edit a node with Highlighter contents; Graph -> Move should track the Pencil smoothly and edges should stay attached.
2. Repeat with Pen-only contents and mixed Pen + Highlighter contents.
3. Move a content-free node; graph should remain smooth.
4. Confirm the contents land exactly with the node after release and remain attached after another move/restart.
5. Undo/Redo the move.
6. Recheck 5.8.9 live node Eraser feedback after this performance revision.

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-23 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.9-exp

5.8.9-exp LIVE RESTRICTED NODE-CONTENT ERASER PREVIEW

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
- User confirmed 5.8.7 node-content editing works, but restricted Eraser feedback appeared only after Pencil-up.
- Diagnosis: 5.8.7 intentionally disabled the ordinary shared-raster destructive preview during node editing because that raster also contains unrelated page ink. The exact vector edit was therefore deferred to release.

WHAT 5.8.9 CHANGES
- On restricted node erase, only the active node's owned Pen/Highlighter annotations are rendered onto a temporary `live-node-eraser-canvas`.
- The ordinary annotation canvas is frozen once with those owned annotations excluded.
- During Pencil movement, `destination-out` is applied only to the isolated temporary canvas, giving immediate visual feedback while making it impossible for the preview to erase unrelated page ink or another node's contents.
- On release, the preexisting exact vector eraser still filters candidate IDs to the active node and commits once. The temporary layer is removed and the normal deferred exact redraw restores authoritative rendering.
- Cancel and diagnostic recovery remove the temporary preview and redraw the unchanged vector state.

UNCHANGED
- Ordinary page Eraser live preview and vector splitting.
- Node ownership and auto-fit/manual-size semantics.
- 5.8.8 customizable Presentation toolbar.
- Highlighter-containing graph-node move performance remains diagnosed but intentionally unfixed here; 5.8.10 should address it with a lightweight drag preview rather than full exact annotation redraws on every pointer move.

TEST PRIORITY
1. Edit a node containing Pen ink; erase partially and confirm the disappearance follows the Pencil before lift.
2. Repeat with Highlighter node contents.
3. Place unrelated ordinary ink immediately beside/behind the node and erase across both; only node-owned ink should preview/disappear and commit.
4. Cancel/interruption test if convenient: transient preview must restore correctly.
5. Regression-check ordinary page Eraser outside Edit Contents.

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-23 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.8-exp

5.8.8-exp CUSTOMIZABLE PRESENTATION TOOLBAR

5.8.8 is a focused Presentation-toolbar revision built directly from 5.8.7-exp. It does NOT address the two graph-content issues diagnosed immediately before this build: restricted node erasing still shows its exact result only on pen-up, and moving a node containing Highlighter can still be slow/jumpy because Graph Move currently performs a full exact annotation redraw on each pointer move, including expensive full-resolution Highlighter compositing. Preserve those diagnoses for the next performance pass; no Eraser/Highlighter/Graph-move rendering code was intentionally changed here.

USER-CONFIRMED BASELINE BEFORE THIS REVISION
- 5.8.5 graph-object selection handoff works: lassoed graph objects remain live after Select -> Graph, and Graph size/border/color options affect the preserved selection.
- 5.8.6 Page View exclusive contextual-panel behavior works, including clicking Page View again to return to the previous tool's controls.
- 5.8.7 simple node-content editing works functionally: add/edit/clear/move content works. Two UX/performance observations remain queued as described above.

WHAT 5.8.8 ADDS

1. PERMANENT TOOLS BUTTON IN PRESENTATION
- Adds a permanent Tools button at the far left of the Presentation toolbar.
- The Tools button itself cannot be hidden.
- Exit Presentation remains permanently visible and is pinned at the far right.
- Diagnostics and the document/pane selectors remain system controls, outside the user-customizable tool registry.
- Ordinary non-Presentation View is intentionally unchanged by toolbar visibility preferences.

2. TOOLS MENU = ALTERNATE LAUNCHER + TOOLBAR CUSTOMIZER
- The menu expands downward from the Tools button.
- It contains: Hand, Laser, Pen, Highlighter, Eraser, Lasso, Graph, Image, Asset, Page view, Split view, Add page, Thumbnails, Undo, Redo.
- Each row contains the familiar tool icon/action plus a checkbox.
- Clicking the tool/icon area performs the same action as clicking the normal toolbar button and closes the Tools menu.
- IMPORTANT UX RULE: tool-specific options NEVER expand inside the Tools menu. Example: choosing Pen from Tools activates Pen and shows Pen color/width controls in the normal contextual toolbar strip. Choosing Graph shows Graph controls there. Choosing Page view shows the normal Page View subtoolbar there.
- Clicking a checkbox only changes whether that top-level tool is also visible in the main Presentation toolbar. Checkbox changes keep Tools open so several visibility choices can be adjusted in one visit.
- Unchecked tools remain fully available from Tools.

3. FIRST-RUN DEFAULT VISIBLE PRESENTATION TOOLS
Visible by default:
- Hand
- Pen
- Eraser
- Lasso
- Thumbnails
- Undo
- Redo

Hidden by default but available in Tools:
- Laser
- Highlighter
- Graph
- Image
- Asset
- Page view
- Split view
- Add page

4. PERSISTENCE / FUTURE EXTENSIBILITY
- Visibility is stored in localStorage under `pdfwb-presentation-tools-v1` and survives app close/reopen.
- The preference is included in full editable Library backup/restore.
- The implementation is registry-driven through `PRESENTATION_TOOL_REGISTRY`.
- A future top-level Presentation tool should need one registry entry: stable id, display label, toolbar element id, activation action, and default visibility.
- Saved layouts are merged against registry defaults at startup. Therefore a future newly registered tool automatically appears with its declared default rather than requiring a preference migration or corrupting an older user's saved layout.

5. ADD PAGE FROM A HIDDEN TOOL
- Add Page can be hidden from the main toolbar and still launched from Tools.
- The command still uses the real Presentation Add Page action/state, but its popover is positioned from the always-visible Tools button so hiding the ordinary Add Page button cannot produce a zero-size/invalid popover anchor.

PRESENTATION CUSTOMIZATION TESTS
1. Enter Presentation on iPad/Surface. Confirm Tools is fixed at far left and Close remains accessible at far right.
2. Confirm first-run/default toolbar shows Hand, Pen, Eraser, Lasso, Undo, Redo, Thumbnails (plus non-custom system controls such as document selector/Diagnostics/Close).
3. Open Tools; uncheck several visible tools. Confirm they disappear immediately from the main Presentation toolbar while remaining in Tools.
4. Check previously hidden Laser/Highlighter/Graph/Image/Asset/Page view/Split/Add page. Confirm they appear immediately in the main toolbar.
5. Choose Pen from Tools. Menu should close; Pen should become active; Pen color/width options should appear in the ordinary toolbar, NOT in the Tools menu.
6. Repeat with Eraser, Lasso, Graph, Highlighter, and Page view.
7. Hide Add page, then launch Add page from Tools. Confirm the insert-page chooser appears in a sensible position below the left side of the toolbar and all insert commands still work.
8. Hide Split view and Thumbnails, then invoke each from Tools.
9. Hide active Pen while Pen is active. Pen should remain the active annotation tool; only its top-level toolbar button disappears. Pen options may remain visible because the active tool has not changed.
10. Close/reopen Workbench and re-enter Presentation. Confirm checkbox/tool visibility choices persist.
11. Normal View should still show its ordinary unified annotation toolbar independent of the Presentation customization.
12. Full editable Library backup/restore should preserve the Presentation toolbar visibility preference.

KNOWN GRAPH ISSUES TO ADDRESS AFTER TOOLBAR WORK
A. NODE-CONTENT ERASER LIVE FEEDBACK
- 5.8.7 restricted node Eraser intentionally suppresses the ordinary destructive shared-canvas live preview, because that preview would temporarily erase unrelated nearby page ink even though only node-owned ink is eligible for the exact vector edit.
- Exact restricted erasing is therefore calculated/committed on release, explaining why the visual result appears only after Pencil-up.
- A proper future fix should use an isolated temporary preview of eligible node-owned ink rather than re-enabling the shared destructive raster preview.

B. HIGHIGHTER INSIDE A MOVING NODE IS SLOW/JUMPY
- The translation of node/content point coordinates is cheap.
- The dominant cost is Graph Move calling a complete page annotation redraw on every pointer move.
- Exact Highlighter redraw is expensive: it uses full-resolution scratch-canvas compositing to preserve translucent stroke appearance without dark overlap beads.
- Repeating that exact full-page Highlighter render throughout a node drag causes the observed jumpiness, especially on iPad.
- Preferred future architecture: follow the already-successful Select move/resize pattern — preview the moving node+contents cheaply on a temporary GPU/composited layer, update attached edges cheaply, then commit coordinates and perform one exact redraw at release. Do not broadly rewrite the validated Highlighter geometry merely to mask this Graph Move issue.

CURRENT GRAPH ROADMAP AFTER 5.8.8
1. Address the two node-content performance/feedback issues above before extending attached-ink semantics further.
2. Edge labels after movement/eraser behavior is satisfactory.
3. Then settle node-content transform/scaling behavior.
4. Then relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste using the agreed self-contained-fragment semantics.
5. Later curved/parallel edges, loops, directed edges/arrowheads, and graph conveniences.

Continue future work from the packaged 5.8.8-exp build, not an older branch.

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.7-exp

5.8.7-exp SIMPLE GRAPH NODE-CONTENT EDITING

5.8.7 is a focused graph-content revision built directly from the validated 5.8.6-exp Page View toolbar baseline. It deliberately avoids an internal lasso/recolor system for the small handwritten labels likely to live inside graph nodes.

WHAT 5.8.7 ADDS

1. EDIT CONTENTS FOR ONE NODE
- Graph toolbar now shows a compact Edit Contents button only when exactly one graph node is the current graph editing context.
- The existing Select/Lasso node-content button also enters the same editor when exactly one node (and no loose selected ink) is selected.
- Entering Edit Contents switches to Pen and stores a separate semantic edit target. The target survives switching among Pen, Highlighter, and Eraser.
- The active node stays ring-highlighted while its contents are being edited.
- Done returns to Graph with that node selected. Tapping Graph directly also exits Edit Contents and returns to that node. Hand/Select/Laser end the edit state without retaining the content editor.

2. ADDING INK
- Pen and Highlighter continue to use the mature ordinary Workbench ink renderer/modeler.
- A new stroke must begin on or near the active node; once begun, it may extend beyond the current node boundary and still belongs to the node.
- Manual nodes stay manual when contents are added.
- Auto-fit nodes continue to refit around contents after completed strokes.

3. RESTRICTED ERASER
- During Edit Contents, Eraser can alter only ink semantically owned by the active node.
- Nearby ordinary page ink and other nodes' contents are not eligible for the vector erase.
- To avoid misleading temporary erasure of unrelated rasterized ink, the normal destructive live eraser preview is suppressed during restricted node-content erasing; the exact vector result appears on release.
- Existing partial-stroke splitting and relationship rebuilding remain in use. Auto-fit nodes can shrink after erasing; manual nodes retain their frame.

4. CLEAR / REPLACE
- Clear Contents removes all ink owned by the active node in one Undoable operation.
- Replacement is intentionally simple: Clear, then write the new contents.
- Clearing the final content leaves the current node frame in place. An auto node can refit when new content is subsequently written.

5. MOVE ALL CONTENTS
- Move Contents is a one-shot action in the node-content editing strip.
- Arm it, then drag from the node or its handwriting. All owned ink translates together.
- Content is clamped to the page bounds.
- Once actual movement occurs, the node is switched to manual sizing so its frame stays fixed while its handwriting is repositioned.
- No per-stroke internal selection is introduced.

INTENTIONALLY NOT INCLUDED
- No lasso/select inside a node.
- No recoloring of existing node contents from the node editor.
- No automatic scaling of node handwriting when the node frame is resized.
- No edge labels yet.
- No relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste yet.

RECENTLY VALIDATED BEHAVIOR TO PRESERVE
- User reported 5.8.5 graph selection handoff working: lassoed graph objects remain live when switching Select -> Graph and Graph size/border/color controls affect that selection.
- User reported 5.8.6 Page View toolbar behavior working, including the useful toggle back to the previous annotation tool panel when Page View is clicked again.
- Pure ink/image Select behavior must remain independent of graph-only controls.

CURRENT GRAPH ROADMAP
1. Test/settle this simple node-content editor in real use.
2. Edge labels next. Prefer the same semantic-content philosophy: labels belong to edges and follow them.
3. Then settle content-transform behavior (including optional scaling of node handwriting if desired).
4. Then relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste, using the already agreed self-contained-fragment semantics: copied nodes bring owned contents; edges between copied nodes come along; explicitly selected edges bring needed endpoint nodes; external connections to unselected nodes stay behind; pasted IDs and relationships are remapped.
5. Later: curved/parallel edges, loops, directed edges/arrowheads, and other graph conveniences.

TOOLBAR / UI NOTES
- 5.8.6 Page View remains an exclusive contextual panel; do not revert it to additive popover behavior.
- Select remains generic and keeps Ink -> Node / Use as Node Content.
- Graph owns graph size/border/color and now Edit Contents.
- Selected-ink recolor remains the standard five-color palette.

TARGETED TESTS FOR 5.8.7
- Select one node -> Graph -> Edit Contents -> write Pen strokes; verify they move with the node afterward.
- Repeat with Highlighter.
- While editing, switch Pen <-> Highlighter <-> Eraser; verify the same node remains highlighted/targeted.
- Erase node ink while ordinary page ink sits nearby; only node ink should change.
- Clear Contents, Undo, Redo.
- Move Contents; frame should stay fixed and the node should become manual-sized after actual movement.
- On an auto-fit node, add/erase content and verify the frame grows/shrinks. On a manual node, verify the frame remains fixed.
- Click Graph or Done to leave Edit Contents and verify the node is selected in Graph.
- Page View should still temporarily replace the current tool subtoolbar and toggle back exactly as in 5.8.6.

Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.7-exp build, not an older branch.

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.6-exp

5.8.6-exp EXCLUSIVE PAGE VIEW TOOLBAR PANEL

5.8.6 changes Page View from an additive popover into a contextual toolbar panel that behaves like the annotation tools for display purposes. Opening Page View hides the active Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select/Graph option groups while preserving the underlying annotationTool, graph selection, and annotationSelection. Tapping Page View again restores the active tool's options; choosing an annotation tool while Page View is open closes Page View and restores that tool's panel. Arbitrary document clicks no longer dismiss Page View. Leaving View closes the Page View panel. Normal View and Presentation share the same transient pageViewPanelOpen state.

The actual Page View controls remain Scroll mode, Fit mode, Zoom -, and Zoom +. The percentage/reset readout remains removed.

Graph selection from 5.8.5 remains validated by the user: lassoed graph-object selection is preserved on Select -> Graph and Graph size/border/color controls act on the selection.

Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.6-exp build, not an older branch.

--- PRIOR 5.8.5 HANDOFF ---

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.5-exp
====================================================================================

5.8.5-exp FOCUSED FIX
---------------------
5.8.4 preserved the Select/Lasso highlight when switching to Graph, but setAnnotationTool() then cleared the underlying annotationSelection without redrawing. This left a visually selected graph whose Graph controls saw no selected objects and therefore changed defaults instead of the selection. 5.8.5 removes that state clear. The lasso selection remains live in Graph mode; Graph size/border/color controls operate on its graph subset. A direct Graph hit still replaces the preserved lasso through setGraphSelection(), and Graph -> Select preserves the lasso selection.


THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.5-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.8.5-exp.
- 5.8.4 is a focused toolbar/selection workflow refinement built on 5.8.3 edge-aware lasso selection and 5.8.2 semantic node contents.
- Pen/Highlighter modeling, partial Eraser, PDF.js rendering/caps, viewer canvas release, Library schema, and PDF export architecture are unchanged.

5.8.4 CHANGES
--------------
1. SELECT IS GENERIC AGAIN
   - Graph Border and graph-color controls no longer appear in Select/Lasso.
   - Graph node-size controls no longer appear while Select is active.
   - Select still owns ordinary move/resize/rotate/delete/copy-region/paste behavior and the context-sensitive Ink -> Node / Use as Node Content / Node Ink button.
   - Ordinary selected-ink recolor is reduced to the standard five-color palette: black, blue, red, green, orange.
   - Pure ink/image selections remain on the established ordinary-selection path; no graph controls appear.

2. SELECT -> GRAPH HANDOFF
   - A lasso selection containing graph nodes/edges persists when the user taps Graph.
   - While Graph is active, node size, node border style, and graph color operate on the graph objects contained in that preserved multi-selection.
   - Mixed selections are filtered: Graph styling affects graph geometry only; selected ordinary ink/images are not recolored or resized by Graph controls.
   - Switching Graph -> Select preserves the lasso selection so ordinary selection editing can resume.
   - Starting a direct Graph interaction on the page replaces the preserved lasso selection with Graph's ordinary single-object selection.
   - In Graph inspector mode the preserved graph objects remain highlighted, but ordinary resize handles/transform box are hidden so the UI does not imply that Select transforms are active.

3. GRAPH COLOR MOVED TO GRAPH TOOL
   - Graph toolbar now contains the standard five-color palette.
   - With selected graph objects, the chosen color changes only node borders and selected edges (`strokeColor`). Node-content handwriting is unchanged.
   - With no selected graph objects, the chosen color becomes the default for new graph nodes and edges.
   - The graph color preference is persisted and included in editable-library backup preferences.
   - Nodes created from selected ink also use the current graph color.

4. GRAPH BORDER / SIZE UNDER GRAPH TOOL
   - Border Clean / Hand / None acts on all selected nodes in a preserved lasso selection; the chosen border also remains the default for future nodes.
   - Common node size likewise acts on all selected nodes after Select -> Graph and continues to update the future-node default.
   - Edge-only preserved selections ignore node-only border/size actions and retain their own geometry.

5. COMPACT PAGE-VIEW CONTROLS
   - Normal View header now shows one Page View button instead of permanent Scroll / Fit / Zoom controls.
   - Opening Page View reveals a temporary inline subtoolbar with scrolling mode, fit mode, Zoom - and Zoom +.
   - The old 100% zoom/reset readout is removed because it did not communicate fit-mode scale meaningfully.
   - Presentation/annotation toolbar likewise collapses its permanent scroll/fit/zoom controls behind one Page View button, reducing horizontal pressure when contextual Select/Graph controls are visible.
   - Pinch zoom and keyboard zoom remain unchanged.

6. GRAPH COPY/PASTE STILL DEFERRED
   - Ordinary Copy/Duplicate remains disabled for semantic graph selections.
   - Agreed future semantics: copied graph material should become a self-contained fragment; copied nodes bring node contents, edges between copied nodes are preserved, and explicitly copied edges pull in required endpoint nodes. New IDs must be remapped on paste.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
- Lasso ordinary ink/images only: verify Select toolbar contains no graph styling/size controls and ordinary transforms still behave exactly as before.
- Verify selected-ink recolor offers only black/blue/red/green/orange.
- Lasso several nodes, tap Graph, verify the selection remains visibly identified and common node size + Border operate on all selected nodes.
- Lasso several edges, tap Graph, verify graph color recolors only those edges.
- Lasso nodes + edges + ordinary ink, tap Graph, verify graph color changes node borders/edges only and does not change the ink.
- With no graph selection, choose a Graph color and create nodes/edges; verify new objects use that color.
- Change selected-node border/size through Graph; create a new node and verify the chosen settings became defaults.
- After Select -> Graph, tap/move/create a graph object directly; verify the preserved lasso selection is replaced cleanly by the ordinary single Graph selection.
- Verify normal View Page View button opens/closes scroll/fit/zoom subtoolbar and click-away closes it.
- Verify Presentation Page View button expands/collapses the same four controls without reintroducing the zoom percentage label.
- Re-test Split mode page controls because scroll/fit/zoom actions continue to target the active pane.
- Undo/Redo graph color/border/size edits and verify persistence/export remain correct.

NEXT GRAPH MILESTONES / OPEN DESIGN
-----------------------------------
- Implement relationship-aware graph Copy/Paste using the agreed self-contained-fragment rules.
- Edit existing ink attached to a node while preserving graph ownership and auto/manual-fit semantics.
- Decide whether node resize should optionally scale handwritten contents as well as the frame.
- Edge labels should reuse the semantic container/content relationship; then curved/parallel edges, loops, and directed arrowheads.
- Revisit further toolbar organization only after these Graph controls reveal additional pressure; the current Page View consolidation is intended to buy room first.

KNOWN WATCH ITEMS — UNCHANGED
------------------------------
- Rare iPad/WebKit missing-input episodes remain upstream-looking; do not rewrite Pen without new evidence.
- Rare canceled-pinch jumps and self-recovering render-left-loading/scroll-to-render events remain watch items.
- Current explicit viewer canvas release and 6 MP ceiling continue to look healthy.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.8.3-exp / SEPT. 22) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.3-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.3-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.8.3-exp.
- 5.8.3 is a focused Select/Lasso graph-object selection and styling pass built on 5.8.2 semantic node contents.
- Pen/Highlighter modeling, partial Eraser, PDF.js rendering/caps, viewer canvas release, Library schema, and PDF export architecture are unchanged.

5.8.3 CHANGES
--------------
1. LASSO / TAP SELECTION OF GRAPH EDGES
   - Ordinary Select can now hit a graph edge directly with a tap.
   - Lasso selection includes an edge if the lasso contains an endpoint or midpoint, or if the lasso boundary intersects the edge segment.
   - Therefore several edges can be selected together, and an edge-only lasso can be made across the middle of edges without selecting their endpoint nodes.
   - Selected edges receive a graph-selection halo in ordinary Select.

2. EDGE GEOMETRY REMAINS DEPENDENT
   - Selected edges do not gain ordinary move/resize handles and are not transformed as free-floating lines. Edge-only, edge+ink, and edge+image selections do not expose the ordinary transform box unless at least one graph node is also selected.
   - If selected endpoint nodes move/resize/rotate, incident edges continue to recompute from node geometry as before.
   - Mixed selections containing edges skip those edge records in ordinary move/resize/rotate math, preventing edge selection from interfering with existing annotation transforms.

3. MULTI-NODE BORDER STYLE
   - When one or more graph nodes are selected with ordinary Select, a graph-only Border control appears.
   - Clean / Hand / None applies to all selected nodes.
   - Mixed border styles show the neutral Border placeholder until the user chooses a common style.
   - The chosen style also becomes the current default border preference for newly created nodes.

4. NODE / EDGE COLOR AFTER LASSO
   - A graph-only five-color palette (black, blue, red, green, orange) appears whenever selected graph nodes or edges are present.
   - The graph palette changes only semantic graph geometry (`strokeColor`): node borders and edges.
   - Node-content Pen/Highlighter ink does not change unless the ordinary selected-ink recolor palette is used separately.
   - Mixed node + edge selections can recolor all selected graph geometry together. None-border nodes retain the chosen stored color, visible again if a border is restored later.

5. PURE INK / IMAGE SELECTION ISOLATION
   - If the selection contains only ordinary ink and/or images, the new graph-style group is hidden.
   - Existing selection recolor, move, resize, rotate, copy, duplicate, delete, region-copy, and paste behavior remains on the pre-5.8.3 path.
   - Graph Copy/Duplicate remains disabled whenever any semantic graph node/edge is in the selection until relationship-aware copy/paste is implemented.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
- Lasso only ink/images and verify the toolbar and all ordinary selection behavior look/act exactly as before.
- Tap a single graph edge with Select; verify it highlights, can be recolored/deleted, and does not show resize handles.
- Lasso across the middle of several edges without enclosing nodes; verify only those edges are selected and graph color applies to them.
- Lasso several nodes; change Border among Clean / Hand / None and verify all selected node frames change together.
- Lasso nodes + edges; apply each graph color and verify node borders + selected edges change while node-content ink does not.
- Lasso graph objects + ordinary ink/images; verify graph color and ink color are separate controls and ordinary geometry transforms do not attempt to move selected edges independently.
- Undo/Redo each border/color/delete operation and verify persistence/export retain the graph styles.

NEXT GRAPH MILESTONES / OPEN DESIGN
-----------------------------------
- Edit existing ink attached to a node while preserving graph ownership and auto/manual fit semantics.
- Decide whether node resize should optionally scale handwritten contents as well as the frame.
- Edge labels should reuse the semantic container/content relationship; then curved/parallel edges, loops, and directed arrowheads.
- Toolbar consolidation remains deferred until Graph/Select needs settle. User is comfortable eventually hiding Zoom +/-/percentage plus Full Page/Continuous/Page Snap behind one compact Page/View control.

KNOWN WATCH ITEMS — UNCHANGED
------------------------------
- Rare iPad/WebKit missing-input episodes remain upstream-looking; do not rewrite Pen without new evidence.
- Rare canceled-pinch jumps and self-recovering render-left-loading/scroll-to-render events remain watch items.
- Current explicit viewer canvas release and 6 MP ceiling continue to look healthy.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.8.2-exp / SEPT. 22) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.2-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.2-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.8.2-exp.
- 5.8.2 is the first semantic node-content milestone built from the validated 5.8.1 graph sizing/selection baseline.
- Stable Pen modeling, Highlighter, partial Eraser, PDF.js rendering/caps, viewer canvas release, Library schema, and PDF export architecture are preserved.

5.8.2 CHANGES
--------------
1. LASSO INK -> GRAPH NODE
   - Ordinary Select gains one compact node-content action.
   - Selecting ink/highlighter only and invoking the action creates a semantic graph node around the selected ink.
   - The new node uses the current Clean / Hand-drawn / None border preference and auto-fits to ink bounds plus padding.
   - The original ink objects are not re-rendered into a new format. They remain mature Workbench ink objects and gain semantic ownership metadata.

2. ATTACH EXISTING INK TO AN EXISTING NODE
   - Selecting exactly one graph node together with ink/highlighter changes the same action to Use as Node Content.
   - The selected ink is attached to that node; moving/reparenting removes it from any former node relationship.
   - The target node auto-fits around all of its attached content.

3. NODE INK MODE
   - Selecting exactly one node with no ink and invoking the same action enters Node Ink mode and switches to Pen.
   - Pen/Highlighter strokes that begin inside that node become node contents.
   - Auto-fit runs when each attached stroke finishes. Choosing a tool other than Pen/Highlighter leaves Node Ink mode.

4. CONTENT RELATIONSHIP MODEL
   - `graph-node.contents` stores child annotation IDs.
   - Child ink remains top-level page annotation geometry for compatibility with existing rendering/export/eraser behavior, but records `graphNodeId` plus node-relative `graphLocalPoints`.
   - Moving a node with Graph Move or Select translates attached ink with the node.
   - Node/group resizing currently moves attached ink with the node center but deliberately does NOT scale handwriting. Optional scale-with-node behavior remains a future refinement.
   - Partial Eraser fragments preserve/rebuild node ownership; auto-fit nodes can shrink after erasing or independently moving/deleting contents.
   - Deleting a node removes attached ink plus incident edges; Undo restores the complete state.

5. AUTO VS MANUAL NODE SIZE
   - Nodes made from/attached to ink use auto-fit sizing.
   - Explicit common-size selection and freeform node-frame resize switch the affected node to manual sizing.
   - Manual sizing remains authoritative even if content is larger/smaller.
   - Common-size choices now always update the default size for new nodes; freeform handle scaling does not.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
- Lasso one or several Pen strokes -> Make Node; confirm border style, fit, Undo/Redo, persistence, and export.
- Lasso ink + one existing node -> Use as Node Content; move the node and confirm ink follows.
- Select one node -> node-content action -> write several Pen strokes inside; confirm node auto-grows around completed strokes and moving the node carries all ink.
- Erase part of attached ink and confirm an auto-fit node can shrink; Undo should restore ink and frame.
- Resize a content node manually and confirm its frame size remains manual while the handwriting stays unscaled.
- Apply a common size in Select, then create a new node in Graph and confirm the chosen size is now the new-node default.
- Delete a content-bearing node and confirm contents + incident edges are deleted/restored together.

NEXT GRAPH MILESTONES / OPEN DESIGN
-----------------------------------
- Decide whether node resize should optionally scale handwritten contents as well as the frame.
- Edge labels should reuse the same container/content relationship and allow labels to slide along their edge.
- Curved/parallel edges, loops, and directed arrowheads remain later milestones.
- Toolbar consolidation remains deferred until Graph/Select needs settle. User is comfortable eventually hiding/consolidating Zoom +/-/percentage and Full Page/Continuous/Page Snap behind one Page/View control.

KNOWN WATCH ITEMS — UNCHANGED
------------------------------
- Rare iPad/WebKit missing-input episodes remain upstream-looking; do not rewrite Pen based on them without new evidence.
- Rare canceled-pinch jumps and self-recovering render-left-loading/scroll-to-render events remain watch items.
- Current explicit viewer canvas release and 6 MP ceiling continue to look healthy.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.8.1-exp / SEPT. 22) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.1-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.1-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.8.1-exp.
- 5.8.1 is a narrow Graph sizing + Files/Split workflow correction built from 5.8.0-exp.
- It does NOT alter Pen/Highlighter modeling, PDF.js render scheduling/caps, pinch/scroll handling, viewer canvas-release logic, Library schema, backup format, or PDF export policy.

5.8.1 CHANGES
--------------
1. SMALLER GRAPH NODE MINIMUM
   - Lowers the semantic graph-node minimum width/height from 28 to 16.
   - Adds common-size choices 16, 20, and 24 in addition to 28, 36, 44, 56, 72, 96, and 128.
   - Default new-node size remains 44.
   - None-border nodes retain the same logical hit/attachment geometry at these smaller sizes.

2. CONTINUED GRAPH COMPRESSION AFTER NODE MINIMUM
   - 5.8.0 derived the minimum allowed whole-selection resize scale from the first graph node that would reach its 28-pixel minimum. That caused the entire shrink gesture to stop.
   - 5.8.1 removes that graph-derived whole-selection stop. The overall selection can continue shrinking to the ordinary selection-scale floor.
   - Each graph node still clamps its own width/height independently at 16.
   - After a node reaches 16, its center continues moving toward the selection anchor according to the overall selection scale, so graph spacing can continue to contract while node size stays fixed.
   - Mixed ink/image + node selections keep the existing unified transform: ordinary ink/images continue scaling; graph-node centers follow the group; graph-node frames stop shrinking at 16; attached edges recompute from the updated node geometry.

3. FILES -> USE WHILE SPLIT IS ACTIVE
   - Diagnosis confirmed: Files -> Use correctly changed currentDocumentId but Split panes have their own documentId assignments, so returning to View could leave the active pane showing its previous document.
   - 5.8.1 assigns the requested document to the currently active split pane before returning to View.
   - The other split pane is untouched. Existing per-pane view state remains independent.
   - Normal Files Open still opens a closed document while remaining in Files. Use/click on an already-open document still returns to View.

GRAPH STATUS / NEXT DIRECTION
-----------------------------
- 5.8.0 graph selection/sizing tested successfully, including mixed ink + graph-node selections and Rotate Selection.
- Selected-edge visibility uses the 5.8.0 broad temporary halo plus endpoint/midpoint markers and remains unchanged.
- Next graph milestone remains node-local handwriting/content. Planned behaviors include:
  * write/draw explicitly inside a node using existing Pen behavior;
  * store node child ink in node-local/transform-friendly coordinates;
  * auto-fit node bounds to child ink plus padding while still allowing explicit/manual node sizing;
  * later allow optional scaling of contained handwriting when a node is resized;
  * lasso existing ink -> Make Node, computing bounds + padding and reparenting the ink into node-local content;
  * attach/use lassoed ink as the content/label of an existing node;
  * later reuse the same container mechanism for handwritten edge labels.
- Node borders continue to support Clean, Hand-drawn, and None. None is important for ink-only visible nodes.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
1. Create/select a node and set sizes 16, 20, and 24; verify selection/hit-testing and attached edges remain usable.
2. Select several graph nodes and shrink them with a corner handle past the point where node frames reach 16. Verify node diameters stop shrinking while their centers continue moving inward and edges continue following them.
3. Repeat with a mixed ink + node selection and verify ink continues scaling while nodes clamp at 16 without stopping the gesture.
4. In Split, go to Files, choose Use on a document different from the active pane's document, and confirm View returns with that document in the active pane while the other pane remains unchanged.
5. Regression-check normal single-view Files Open/Use, graph node movement/sizing, edge selection highlighting, Undo/Redo, and PDF export.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.8.0-exp) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.8.0-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.8.0-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.8.0-exp.
- 5.8.0 promotes the semantic graph work from the 5.7.44 prototype into the new 5.8.x Graph Tools line.
- Existing Pen/Highlighter model, PDF.js render scheduling/caps, pinch/scroll handling, viewer canvas-release logic, Library schema, export policy, and diagnostics remain unchanged except where graph objects participate in the existing Select/Undo/export systems.
- Existing zoom controls remain visible; do not hide them unless toolbar space becomes a demonstrated problem.

5.8.0 GRAPH TOOL CHANGES
------------------------
1. COMPACT GRAPH CONTROLS
   - Graph remains one permanent main-toolbar button.
   - The Graph contextual Node / Edge / Move / Delete controls use compact icons rather than long text labels.
   - Border style remains available as Clean / Hand-drawn / None.

2. NODE SIZING — INDIVIDUAL OR COMMON SIZE
   - Node geometry is explicitly width/height based and remains meaningful even when border style is None.
   - One selected node or several lasso-selected graph nodes can be moved and proportionally resized with the ordinary Select handles.
   - A compact node-size selector provides standard sizes: 28, 36, 44, 56, 72, 96, 128.
   - In Graph mode it sets the default size for new nodes and applies to a selected node.
   - In Select mode it can set all selected graph nodes to one common size, allowing uniform graph vertices without preventing free resizing.
   - This geometry model is intentionally ready for a later option to scale node-local handwritten contents when a node is resized.

3. SELECT/LASSO INTEGRATION
   - Ordinary Select/Lasso recognizes semantic graph nodes, including None-border nodes through their logical ellipse.
   - Nodes can participate in mixed selections with ordinary Workbench ink/images.
   - Moving/resizing selected nodes updates node geometry while attached semantic edges recompute from node IDs.
   - Rotating a selection understands graph nodes: node centers rotate with the group and node width/height swap on a 90-degree turn.
   - Deleting selected graph nodes also removes their incident edges.
   - Graph edges remain dependent geometry rather than independently stretchable Lasso objects in this milestone; selecting/moving the endpoint nodes is the graph-preserving operation.
   - Semantic graph-node Copy/Duplicate remains disabled until edge-ID remapping semantics are implemented.

4. EDGE-SELECTION VISIBILITY
   - 5.7.44 made a selected straight edge too subtle on dense pages.
   - In Graph mode, a selected edge now gets a broad temporary accent halo plus endpoint and midpoint markers.
   - These indicators are selection chrome only. They do not change the stored edge style and do not export.

5. PRESERVED GRAPH FOUNDATION
   - Nodes remain semantic `graph-node` objects with Clean / Hand / None borders and reserved local `contents`.
   - Straight undirected edges remain semantic `graph-edge` objects storing `fromNodeId` / `toNodeId`; endpoints are recomputed from current node bounds.
   - Graph geometry remains on its dedicated canvas below normal Workbench ink.
   - Node/edge creation, movement, sizing, deletion, border changes, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup/restore, thumbnails/previews, and vector PDF export remain supported.

NEXT GRAPH MILESTONE
--------------------
- Handwritten/drawn node-local content.
- Explicitly activate a node for content editing; do not capture arbitrary page strokes just because they overlap a node.
- Store Pen strokes in node-local coordinates.
- Auto-fit node bounds to content plus padding while retaining independent manual node sizing.
- Plan for two sizing behaviors: auto-fit content and explicit/fixed node size.
- Later allow optional scaling of child writing/drawing when manually resizing a node.
- Preserve Clean / Hand-drawn / None border styles; None should allow the handwritten contents to be the only visible node artwork.

LATER GRAPH MILESTONES
----------------------
- Edge-label containers with node-like handwritten contents, position along edge, perpendicular offset, and dragging along the edge.
- Curved/parallel edges and loops.
- Directed edges/arrowheads.
- Additional node shapes/styles and graph-semantic conveniences only after the core editor proves useful.

PRESERVE / WATCH
----------------
- Preserve 5.7.43 selected-ink recolor and Library Name/Date sort.
- Preserve 5.7.42 Files Open/Close thumbnail-canvas preservation.
- Preserve 5.7.37 explicit viewer canvas backing-store release and 1x1 lazy canvases.
- Preserve exact Pencil replay filtering and current Google-modeled Pen architecture.
- Continue watching rare iPad/WebKit missing-input, canceled-pinch, scroll-to-render, and background/resume IndexedDB anomalies without broad rewrites absent stronger evidence.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
- Graph toolbar: confirm compact Node / Edge / Move / Delete icons are understandable with tooltip/aria labels.
- Graph mode: create nodes at several sizes and verify attached edges follow while nodes move.
- Select: tap/lasso one or several nodes, move them, resize them proportionally, rotate them, and set a common size.
- Border None: verify an invisible-frame node remains hit-testable/selectable/resizable and edges attach to its logical boundary.
- Edge selection: select a graph edge in Graph mode and verify the halo plus endpoint/midpoint markers make selection obvious.
- Delete selected nodes and verify incident edges disappear and Undo restores the full semantic structure.
- Regression-check Pen/Highlighter/Eraser, normal image selection, ordinary mixed selection, PDF export, Pages thumbnails, and Library backup/restore.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.7.44-exp / SEPT. 22) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.7.44-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.7.44-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.7.44-exp.
- 5.7.44 begins the dedicated semantic graph-drawing capability discussed after the 5.7.42/5.7.43 workflow refinements.
- This first graph milestone is intentionally structural: semantic nodes, straight attached edges, movement, deletion, persistence/Undo/Redo, and vector PDF export.
- It does NOT yet put Pen ink inside nodes, auto-fit node size, add edge labels, loops, curves/parallel edges, or directed arrowheads.
- Existing zoom controls remain visible. Do not hide them merely to make room for Graph unless toolbar space becomes a demonstrated problem.
- Pen/Highlighter/Select modeling, PDF.js viewer rendering/caps, pinch handling, explicit viewer canvas-release behavior, Library schema, and diagnostic architecture are otherwise unchanged.

5.7.44 GRAPH FOUNDATION
-----------------------
1. DEDICATED GRAPH TOOL
   - Adds one permanent Graph button to the main annotation toolbar.
   - Graph-specific Node / Edge / Move controls and node-border selector appear only while Graph is active.
   - This avoids turning ordinary Assets into graph objects and avoids spending multiple permanent toolbar slots.

2. SEMANTIC NODES
   - Graph nodes are persisted annotation objects of type `graph-node`, not disguised Pen strokes or Assets.
   - Current node geometry is a centered logical ellipse/circle with minimum/default size metadata.
   - Border style is part of the node object from the beginning: `clean`, `hand`, or `none`.
   - Clean draws a precise ellipse.
   - Hand-drawn draws a deterministic slightly irregular vector outline so the appearance is stable between redraws/export.
   - None draws/exports no node frame at all. The node still has a logical hit area and attachment boundary, so it remains selectable/movable and edges remain attached. This is specifically intended for the future case where only handwritten content inside the node is visible.
   - Each node already reserves a `contents: []` field for the next graph milestone. 5.7.44 does not yet capture Pen ink into it.

3. SEMANTIC STRAIGHT EDGES
   - Graph edges are persisted `graph-edge` objects that store `fromNodeId` / `toNodeId` rather than fixed endpoints.
   - Visible endpoints are recomputed from the current two node centers and their logical ellipse boundaries.
   - Moving either node therefore moves the appropriate edge endpoint automatically.
   - Graph 1 creates straight, undirected edges only. Loops are explicitly not yet allowed; curves, parallel-edge shaping, labels, and arrowheads remain later milestones.

4. INTERACTION
   - Node mode: tap/click empty page space to create a node; tapping an existing graph object selects it instead of stacking a new node there.
   - Edge mode: tap one node, then another node, to create an attached edge.
   - Move mode: drag a node; its incident edges update live. Tapping an edge selects it.
   - Delete removes the selected edge, or removes a selected node together with all incident graph edges.
   - Delete/Backspace and Escape work with Graph selection where appropriate.
   - Apple Pencil PointerEvent and existing stylus TouchEvent fallback routing both include Graph.

5. SEPARATE GRAPH OVERLAY
   - Graph geometry is rendered on its own canvas layer between inserted images and ordinary Workbench ink.
   - Moving a node redraws this graph layer rather than repainting dense existing Pen/Highlighter ink every pointer move.
   - Selection indication remains a separate temporary SVG overlay. A None-border node therefore gets a visible dashed selection ring only while selected; that ring is not document content and does not export.
   - Compact previews, Pages thumbnails, and Copy Region include graph geometry.

6. PERSISTENCE / UNDO / GEOMETRY / EXPORT
   - Graph objects live in the existing page annotation collection, so ordinary Local Library persistence and editable backups retain them without a schema migration.
   - Node/edge creation, node movement, deletion, and border changes use the existing snapshot-based Undo/Redo model.
   - Page geometry shift/fit paths transform node position/size and edge widths while edge endpoint relationships remain ID-based.
   - Graph objects count as page annotations, disabling untouched-byte passthrough when present.
   - PDF export draws graph edges and borders as vector geometry below ordinary Workbench ink; None-border nodes export no frame.

NEXT GRAPH MILESTONE — HANDWRITTEN NODE CONTENT + AUTO-FIT
---------------------------------------------------------
- Make node contents true node-local child annotations rather than page-global ink that happens to overlap a node.
- Allow Pen writing/drawing into an explicitly active node.
- Store child ink in node-local coordinates so moving/scaling/rotating a node or graph later does not require rewriting page-global points.
- Auto-fit node logical bounds to the child-content bounds plus padding, with a minimum size; adding ink grows the node and erasing child ink can shrink it.
- Preserve the three border styles while auto-fitting. In particular, None should remain fully functional so the child ink can be the only visible node content.
- Do not automatically capture arbitrary page Pen strokes merely because they cross a node; node-content editing should be explicit enough to avoid surprises.

LATER GRAPH MILESTONES
----------------------
- Edge labels as attached local containers with a position parameter along the edge plus perpendicular offset; eventual handwritten label contents and dragging along the edge.
- Curved and parallel edges, loops, directed arrowheads.
- Additional node shapes/styles and broader graph-semantic conveniences only after the core editing workflow proves useful.

PRESERVE / WATCH
----------------
- Preserve 5.7.43 selected-ink recolor and Library Name/Date sort.
- Preserve 5.7.42 Files Open/Close thumbnail-canvas preservation.
- Preserve 5.7.37 explicit viewer backing-store release and lazy 1x1 canvases.
- Preserve exact Pencil replay filtering and current Google-modeled Pen architecture.
- Continue to watch rare iPad/WebKit missing-input episodes, canceled-pinch jumps, self-recovering render-left-loading/scroll-to-render events, and background/resume IndexedDB failures without broad stability rewrites.
- Leave the 6 MP iPad render ceiling and 50-step session-only Undo policy unchanged absent new evidence.

5.7.44 FIELD TEST TARGETS
-------------------------
1. Graph -> Node: create several nodes with Clean, Hand-drawn, and None borders.
2. Verify a None-border node still shows a temporary selection ring while selected and can be moved normally.
3. Graph -> Edge: tap two nodes to connect them. Move either node and verify the edge stays attached to both node boundaries.
4. Delete an edge. Undo/Redo. Delete a node with multiple incident edges and verify those edges disappear/restored together.
5. Change a selected node among Clean / Hand-drawn / None and verify Undo/Redo.
6. Save/reload the Local Library and verify graph nodes/edges restore.
7. Export a page containing all three node-border styles and attached edges; verify vector geometry and that None exports no node frame.
8. Check Pages thumbnails, compact previews, and Copy Region for graph geometry.
9. Regression-check ordinary Pen/Highlighter/Select, pinch/scroll, Presentation, Files, and PDF export. Graph 1 is not intended to modify those paths.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.7.43-exp / SEPT. 22) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-22 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.7.43-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.7.43-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.7.43-exp.
- 5.7.43 completes the remaining pre-graph-editor roadmap items after the 5.7.42 Files thumbnail fix: selected-ink recoloring and Library Name/Date sorting.
- During review, Rotate Selection was confirmed to already exist in the current code (90° clockwise, Undo/Redo/export compatible) and was field-checked by the user, so it was not reimplemented.

5.7.43 CHANGES
--------------
1. CHANGE COLOR OF SELECTION
   - While Select is active, a color palette appears when the current selection contains Pen/Highlighter strokes.
   - The palette includes the existing five Pen colors plus the four existing Highlighter colors.
   - Choosing a color recolors all selected ink/highlighter objects in one Undoable edit.
   - Mixed selections are supported: image annotations remain unchanged.
   - Recoloring preserves geometry, width, opacity, tool type, modeled Pen data, selection membership, persistence, and PDF export behavior.
   - If all recolorable selected objects already share one palette color, that swatch is shown active.

2. LIBRARY SORT BY NAME OR DATE
   - Local Library adds a Sort control beside Refresh/Grid/List.
   - Name sorts documents alphabetically (existing/default behavior).
   - Date modified sorts documents newest first, with name as a deterministic tie-breaker.
   - Folders remain alphabetic in both modes.
   - Date mode displays the modified date/time in each document metadata line.
   - Sort preference is remembered locally as `pdfwb-library-sort` and included in editable Library backup/restore preferences.

3. ROTATE SELECTION ROADMAP CORRECTION
   - Rotate Selection was already implemented earlier and is present in 5.7.42/5.7.43.
   - It rotates selected ink/highlighter/image annotations 90° clockwise around the selection center and is Undoable.
   - User re-tested it on 2026-09-22 and reported that it works. Treat it as validated/preserve, not pending work.

PRESERVE / WATCH
----------------
- Preserve 5.7.42 Files Open/Close thumbnail-canvas preservation.
- Preserve 5.7.37 explicit viewer backing-store release and 1x1 lazy canvases.
- Preserve current Pen/Highlighter/Select pipeline, exact Pencil replay guard, existing rotation behavior, and PDF export behavior.
- Leave the 6 MP iPad render ceiling and 50-step session-only Undo policy unchanged absent new evidence.
- Continue watching rare iPad/WebKit missing-input episodes, canceled-pinch jumps, the self-recovering scroll-to-render/render-left-loading family, and background/resume IndexedDB failures without redesigning those systems.

NEXT FEATURE DIRECTION
----------------------
- The four-item cleanup list is now complete: Open/Close thumbnail preservation, Rotate Selection (already present/validated), Change Color of Selection, and Library Name/Date sorting.
- The user wants to begin exploring a graph-editor capability next: graph nodes as movable containers that can hold handwritten/drawn content, attached edges that follow node movement, and movable edge labels that remain associated with their edges. Design this as dedicated graph objects rather than converting ordinary Assets directly into nodes.

5.7.42 CHANGE — FILES OPEN/CLOSE NO LONGER RERENDERS THUMBNAILS
----------------------------------------------------------------
- 5.7.41 already stopped selection-only changes from rebuilding Local Library file cards.
- Real-use testing then showed that opening or closing a Library document still rebuilt the entire Library document list, disconnecting/recreating thumbnail canvases and requeueing PDF.js first-page previews.
- 5.7.42 preserves the existing Local Library DOM for Open/Close performed while Files remains visible.
- Library document-row preview/name handlers now read the document's current open/closed state at interaction time rather than capturing the state when the row was created.
- A new in-place row synchronizer updates open/closed styling, metadata, Open/Use/Active state, Close-button presence, ARIA labels, and the Library open-count summary without replacing thumbnail canvases.
- Open Documents, Selected Documents, Export/Compress/Combine state, and the main document selector still refresh normally.
- Structural Library changes (rename, duplicate, move, trash, folder navigation, Grid/List changes, etc.) continue to use the full Library rerender path.

5. Confirm selection checkboxes and PDF Tools state remain synchronized through Open/Close.

5.7.41 CHANGES
--------------
1. FILES NAVIGATION SIMPLIFICATION
   - Removes the 5.7.40 280 ms delayed single-tap / double-tap recognizer.
   - Closed Library document: tap preview/name or Open -> opens into the working set and remains in Files.
   - Already-open nonactive document: tap preview/name or Use -> makes it active and switches to View.
   - This keeps the workflow explicit for both finger and Apple Pencil without maintaining extra gesture timing state.

2. LIBRARY SELECTION NO LONGER RERENDERS THUMBNAILS
   - 5.7.40 selection changes called renderExportPane(), which rebuilt Local Library file cards and recreated thumbnail canvases.
   - Diagnostics from the Sept. 22 three-hour class showed some tiny 48x36 Library thumbnail jobs taking roughly three seconds because PDF.js still had to decode/render the underlying source page.
   - Selection-only changes now preserve the Local Library DOM and synchronize existing checkboxes/selection controls in place.
   - Open Documents, Selected Documents, Export/Compress/Combine and other PDF Tools selection state still refresh normally.
   - Structural Library operations (open/close/rename/move/trash/folder/view changes) continue to rebuild the list when appropriate.

3. PRESENTATION THUMBNAIL BUTTON POSITION
   - Moves Browse page thumbnails to the far-right end of the Presentation toolbar, after Save diagnostics.
   - Thumbnail drawer behavior, rendering, memory cleanup, and navigation semantics are unchanged.

- Several Files-side event-loop gaps aligned with unexpectedly expensive tiny Library thumbnail renders, motivating the selection-DOM preservation change above.

- Saved canvas backing remained moderate and viewer-release behavior continued to look healthy.

5.7.40 FILES / EXTRACT CORRECTIONS

================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.7.39-exp / SEPT. 20) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-20 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.7.39-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.7.39-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest experimental/code baseline is 5.7.39-exp.
- 5.7.39 is a narrow grading-workflow revision built from 5.7.38-exp.
- It intentionally does NOT change Pen/Highlighter/Select behavior, PDF.js render scheduling/caps, pinch handling, viewer canvas-release logic, or diagnostic instrumentation.
- The Sept. 20 diagnostic interpretation remains: rare freezes primarily resemble iPadOS/WebKit event-delivery/compositor behavior; mild scroll jumps include the known canceled-pinch edge case; one self-recovering render-left-loading event remains worth watching; current viewer backing-store release behavior looks healthy.

5.7.39 GRADING WORKFLOW CHANGES
-------------------------------
1. EXTRACT TO LOCAL LIBRARY
   - Pages -> Extract to Library creates a new editable Local Library document from the selected pages.
   - The source document remains active; Extract does not switch away from the paper being graded.
   - The new document is created CLOSED in Local Library, avoiding extra live viewer/canvas pressure.
   - Files -> Extract has a Destination selector: Local Library (default) or Export PDF.
   - Local extracted documents retain page order, annotations, page geometry/background metadata, and referenced source content through ordinary page cloning.
   - The local output is placed in the same Library folder as the source document when possible.

2. SPLIT TO LOCAL LIBRARY
   - Files -> Split now has a Destination selector: Local Library (default) or Export PDF / ZIP.
   - Local split output creates each group as a separate editable CLOSED Library document.
   - The source remains active and the local outputs are placed in the source document's Library folder when possible.
   - Existing PDF/ZIP split export remains available and unchanged when Export PDF / ZIP is selected.

3. OPEN SEVERAL DOCUMENTS FROM FILES
   - Opening a CLOSED Local Library document while in Files no longer jumps to View.
   - This allows several documents to be opened in succession while staying in Files.
   - If no document is active, the first reopened document becomes the internal active document so View has a sensible target later, but Files remains visible.
   - For an already-open nonactive document, the existing Use action makes it active while still remaining in Files.

4. RENAME / CLOSE FROM VIEW AND PAGES
   - Compact Rename and Close controls were added beside the document selector in the main header.
   - They are available in View and Pages and hidden in Files.
   - In Split, they act on the active pane's document.
   - Close persists the document and removes it only from the open working set; the document remains in Local Library.

5. PAGES CLEANUP ALREADY AGREED IN PRIOR HANDOFF
   - Delete Pages now refuses an operation that would remove every remaining page. Ordinary Pages deletion can no longer create a zero-page Workbench document.
   - The temporary legacy `Purge old graph image` button has been removed from the Pages UI.
   - The permanent structured `Add graph paper background` feature is unchanged.

6. ZIP NAME ENTRY CONSISTENCY
   - Multi-document PDF ZIP export now asks for the ZIP name with the same Workbench naming dialog used by Library rename operations before handing the named ZIP to the browser.
   - There are no other intentional direct `window.prompt()` naming paths in normal operation; `window.prompt()` remains only as a defensive fallback if the Workbench naming dialog is unavailable.
   - Note: any later iPadOS/Safari Save/Share sheet is system UI and is not controlled by Workbench. If the reported small floating keyboard belongs to that system sheet rather than Workbench's own filename entry, this change will not alter the system keyboard itself.

VALIDATION / PRESERVE
---------------------
- JavaScript syntax check and release-consistency check must pass before distribution.
- ZIP integrity must pass after packaging.
- Preserve all validated 5.7.38/5.7.37 stability behavior, especially explicit viewer backing-store release, lazy 1x1 canvases, Presentation selector hardening, delayed normal pinch-anchor settling, exact Pencil replay filtering, and current diagnostic instrumentation.
- Continue to watch the isolated `viewer-render-left-loading` / scroll-to-render behavior and canceled-pinch scroll jumps, but do not alter render or pinch code from 5.7.39 without new evidence.

NEXT TESTS
----------
- Grading workflow: Extract selected pages to Local Library from Pages; verify source stays active and result appears as a closed Library document.
- Split: test both Local Library and Export PDF / ZIP destinations.
- Files: open several closed Library documents in succession and verify Files remains visible; test Use on an already-open document.
- Rename and Close from View, Pages, and active Split pane.
