- Try multi-document ZIP export naming on iPad and determine whether the disliked small keyboard was Workbench filename entry or the later iPadOS Save/Share UI.


================ PRIOR AUTHORITATIVE HANDOFF (5.7.38-exp / SEPT. 20) ================

PDF WORKBENCH — CONTINUATION HANDOFF — 2026-09-20 — CURRENT BASELINE 5.7.38-exp
====================================================================================

THIS SECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE OVER ALL OLDER STATUS TEXT BELOW.
Continue future code work from the packaged 5.7.38-exp build, not an older branch.

CURRENT DISTRIBUTION / STATUS
-----------------------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Latest packaged experimental/code baseline is 5.7.38-exp.
- 5.7.38 differs from 5.7.37 only by expanding the temporary legacy PowerPoint graph-background purge to recognize a second exact JPEG fingerprint.
- The user has continued most classroom/grading stability testing on 5.7.37 because the remaining semester PowerPoints were cleaned at the source using a VBA macro that makes every slide background a solid fill with 100% transparency before PDF export. Runtime behavior under test is therefore the same as 5.7.38 except for the unused purge tool.
- No new code changes were made after 5.7.38 through this handoff. The user explicitly wants additional classroom/grading evidence before deciding on render/pinch/input modifications.

LATEST DISTRIBUTION INTEGRITY
-----------------------------
- `pdf-workbench-m5.7.38-exp.zip` passed ZIP integrity testing on 2026-09-20.
- SHA-256 of the packaged distribution: `9c235b4f5c146fe783885e0ac702242036304f1a81a34adaae9c4b7c33fa7d2c`.

SEPT. 17 CLASSROOM RESULT — 5.7.37
-----------------------------------
- Roughly three hours of class were mostly uneventful.
- One full PWA restart occurred during zoom/scroll.
- The first saved same-day diagnostic after the restart contained only about 7.7 seconds of runtime events, so the unsaved in-memory events immediately before the restart were lost. The restart is visible indirectly, but its exact trigger is not captured.
- One brief `Rendering...` indication lasted roughly a second.
- One temporary episode occurred where live ink seemed slower than usual and then recovered.
- A student reported one brief Pen nonresponse and was told to tap the diagnostic button; the episode apparently recovered.
- The 5.7.37 explicit canvas-release work is active and functioning. Viewer/document rebuilds recorded old backing-store releases on the order of roughly 73–149 MB in several cases.
- Around 10:00, immediately after a pinch/zoom, diagnostics recorded a single ~1.22-second event-loop gap while two pages of `14 Fractions.pdf` were rerendering at the existing 6,000,000-pixel per-page cap. It recovered normally, render anomalies remained zero, and subsequent Pen strokes were accepted.
- The brief Rendering/lag observation is plausibly explained by this transient high-resolution zoom rerender. Do not treat it as the older orphaned-Rendering bug unless future diagnostics show a page left in loading state with no active/queued render.
- A later diagnostic save had a several-second interval with no viewer events after a normal stroke/persist. If that corresponds to the student's Pen nonresponse, it again matches the established pattern: Workbench has no stuck Pen/Select/Laser/Eraser state and receives no attempted viewer contact during the failure.
- Do NOT rewrite Pen geometry/modeling based on this class.

SEPT. 18 MORNING CLASS — BUILT-IN GRAPH PAPER
-----------------------------------------------
- The morning class was spent writing primarily on Workbench's built-in graph paper.
- One brief Pencil issue occurred.
- One scroll jump typical of iPad/Safari behavior occurred.
- All saved snapshots belonged to the same uninterrupted runtime; no restart occurred.
- Render anomalies remained zero.
- The likely Pencil nonresponse again looked like a normal completed Pen stroke followed by several seconds with no viewer Pencil/touch delivery; a toolbar Pen-color interaction then arrived and normal writing resumed. No active gesture, render backlog, or JavaScript stall explained the gap.
- The scroll jump appears to have been captured more specifically: Safari canceled a touch sequence during a newly recognized pinch. In roughly 25 ms the reported zoom moved from about 1.000 to 0.679 and scrollTop from about 1461 to 813. Because the gesture ended by `pointercancel`, the normal delayed pinch-anchor correction was not applied. This is a credible mechanism for the observed jump.
- During ordinary Workbench use the significant event-loop gaps were absent. Gaps near the final diagnostic occurred only after Workbench lost visibility/focus, likely while switching to Teams/Call Health, and should not be treated as Workbench rendering evidence.
- Estimated canvas backing use declined across the saved snapshots from roughly 170 MB -> 151 MB -> 129 MB as views changed/released.
- Largest observed individual canvases in this graph-paper class were approximately 4.11 MP earlier and about 2.80 MP later.

SEPT. 18 LATER CLASS — POWERPOINT-DERIVED SLIDES
-------------------------------------------------
- Later the same day, the user taught from PowerPoint-derived PDFs rather than only built-in graph paper.
- One serious but self-recovering input freeze occurred while using Highlighter:
  * the final Highlighter stroke completed normally;
  * pointer-up, handler completion, and pointer-capture release were all logged;
  * the normal Library persist then completed successfully;
  * after that, approximately 12.7 seconds passed with no Pen or finger page/UI input delivered at all;
  * there was no JavaScript event-loop stall, render backlog, stuck Highlighter gesture, or unfinished pointer capture explaining the silence;
  * Pen AND finger button input were reportedly unresponsive;
  * pressing the iPad hardware/Home/app-switch control to leave Workbench and then returning restored input without a Workbench process restart.
- This is strong evidence for an iPadOS/WebKit input/compositing delivery stall above the Workbench Pen/Highlighter/Select handlers. The browser was still capable of JavaScript work; contacts simply were not delivered to the page.
- Leaving/re-entering triggered viewer/layout rebuild and explicit canvas-release activity; input resumed afterward. This does not prove memory pressure caused the input stall, but the compositor/view-state reset is a plausible recovery mechanism.
- PowerPoint-derived pages did NOT inherently create larger Workbench canvases than built-in graph paper. Observed slide-deck canvases were roughly 3.15–3.70 MP, comparable to or smaller than the graph-paper class.
- PDF content complexity DID affect render time: two PowerPoint pages at only ~3.26 MP took just over one second to render, whereas many other pages of similar canvas size rendered in roughly tens to low hundreds of milliseconds.
- Therefore Workbench canvas MP is mainly determined by viewer size/zoom/device pixel ratio/render cap, while PDF internal complexity can independently increase PDF.js render cost.

POWERPOINT / TEAMS PROJECTION CONTEXT
-------------------------------------
- The classroom display is currently driven through Microsoft Teams rather than AirServer/direct iPad mirroring.
- The Teams Call Health screen did not report a usable sent-screen resolution during the user's check; do not assume a measured classroom transmission resolution is available.
- The user has now cleaned the remaining semester PowerPoints at the source: slide backgrounds are set to solid fill with 100% transparency by VBA before PDF creation. This eliminates the old full-page graph-paper JPEG background from newly generated PDFs and should reduce unnecessary PDF image decode/compositing work.
- For normal PowerPoint-to-PDF creation, keep standard vector-oriented PDF export. Do not rasterize whole slides merely to control Workbench MP; Workbench's rendered canvas dimensions are controlled primarily by viewer zoom/size/DPR and the render cap, not by the source image DPI alone.

RENDER-CAP DECISION — DEFERRED
-------------------------------
- Current iPad/page render ceiling remains 6,000,000 pixels.
- We discussed a possible iPad-only 4.0–4.5 MP cap because classroom projection through Teams/display hardware probably does not need full Retina-detail rendering.
- DO NOT implement that change yet.
- Evidence is currently mixed:
  * a Sept. 17 post-pinch ~6 MP rerender coincided with a 1.22-second event-loop gap;
  * later ordinary graph-paper and PowerPoint classes often rendered below 4.5 MP anyway;
  * during Sept. 20 grading, canvases reached about 5.93 MP with no render anomalies or event-loop gaps.
- The user plans a much better memory/performance stress test: grade a full classroom set with many scanned student documents open simultaneously. Scanned/image-heavy PDFs should exercise source-image decode plus viewer canvas memory more aggressively than a few small submissions.
- Use that future full-class grading session to decide whether 6 MP is actually causing memory/restart trouble before lowering the cap.

SEPT. 20 GRADING SESSION
-------------------------
- Only a few small documents were open in this session; it was not a full memory stress test.
- An early upload/open "blip" was saved diagnostically. It occurred about seven seconds into that runtime while opening a 3-page submission alongside a 2-page submission.
- The renderer briefly queued an obsolete first-generation render during viewer rebuild and skipped it as designed; replacement renders completed normally. No render anomaly, error, or event-loop stall was found.
- During this grading session, canvases reached approximately 5.93 MP / roughly 92 MB total estimated backing storage without render anomalies or event-loop gaps. This is useful evidence that near-6-MP output is not inherently unstable by itself.

PAGES UX ISSUE FOUND DURING GRADING
-----------------------------------
- The user accidentally deleted all pages from a document in Pages.
- Diagnostics confirmed that the document remained open with 0 pages while Workbench itself was healthy.
- Because the Pages workflow then ejects/redirects the user, the structural Undo control is no longer conveniently available; recovery is awkward.
- Treat this as a product-flow/UX bug, NOT a runtime diagnostic failure.
- FUTURE PAGES REVISION: prevent any Delete Pages operation that would remove the last remaining page. A Workbench document should never be allowed to transition to 0 pages through the ordinary Pages delete command.
- A simple explanatory alert is sufficient; do not create a special zero-page editing state.

LEGACY GRAPH-PAPER PURGE — RETIRE IN NEXT PAGES REVISION
---------------------------------------------------------
- The temporary `Purge old graph image` migration button is no longer needed for the user's normal workflow.
- The remaining semester PowerPoint source files were cleaned using the VBA background-transparency macro and will be regenerated without the legacy full-page graph-paper JPEG.
- FUTURE PAGES REVISION: remove the purge button/tool from the normal Pages UI.
- Preserve the historical implementation notes below for reference, but do not treat the purge as a permanent product feature.
- Do not remove the permanent structured `Add graph paper background` feature; only remove the temporary legacy-image purge command.

INPUT / WEBKIT STATUS — CURRENT INTERPRETATION
-----------------------------------------------
- Several distinct real-use incidents now share the same pattern across Pen, Select, and Highlighter:
  * preceding gesture finishes normally;
  * pointer capture is released;
  * no Workbench transient gesture remains stuck;
  * JavaScript/event loop is often healthy;
  * attempted Pencil/finger contacts sometimes do not reach the Workbench viewer/UI at all;
  * an ordinary toolbar interaction sometimes restores delivery; in the strongest Sept. 18 case even finger/button input was absent until the PWA was backgrounded and foregrounded.
- Continue treating these rare episodes primarily as upstream Safari/iPadOS/WebKit input/compositor delivery stalls unless a future diagnostic shows Workbench actually receiving and rejecting/mishandling the missing contact.
- Do not rewrite the Pen/Highlighter geometry/model pipeline without new evidence.
- The diagnostic button remains useful when it can be pressed, but it cannot capture failed contacts that Safari never dispatches to the page.
- If the entire web page is not receiving Pen or finger events, leaving and returning to the PWA is a reasonable classroom recovery action; it has restored input without losing Workbench state in observed use.

PINCH / SCROLL STATUS — WATCH, DO NOT CHANGE YET
-------------------------------------------------
- Existing 5.7.33 delayed pinch-anchor settling remains useful for ordinary pinch completion.
- Sept. 18 evidence suggests a remaining edge case when Safari cancels a pinch almost immediately: a substantial partial zoom/scroll change may be left authoritative because the delayed settling path is not applied after `pointercancel`.
- A possible future fix is to restore the pre-pinch anchor/state or apply an equivalent anchor reconciliation on an early/canceled pinch.
- DO NOT implement yet. The user asked to wait through additional classes before deciding modifications.

NEXT PLANNED REAL-WORLD TEST
----------------------------
- Grade a full classroom set with many scanned/image-heavy student PDFs open.
- Save diagnostics periodically and especially after any restart, prolonged Rendering state, input freeze, large-document switch, Split-view use, or obvious slowdown.
- Important questions for that test:
  1. Does total canvas/backing memory grow materially with many scanned documents open even when most are not currently displayed?
  2. Do `viewer-dom-release` events keep hidden/replaced viewer memory bounded?
  3. Are restarts correlated with high simultaneous backing-store estimates, source-image-heavy PDFs, 5–6 MP renders, or large document switches?
  4. Are render times for scanned pages substantially higher than vector PowerPoint pages at comparable MP?
  5. Do input-delivery freezes become more common under high memory/render pressure, suggesting a relationship between WebKit memory/compositor state and input loss?
- Make the render-cap decision only after this stress test unless a clearly reproducible problem appears sooner.

NEXT CODE REVISION — WHEN USER DECIDES TO RESUME CODING
--------------------------------------------------------
Start from 5.7.38-exp. Do NOT implement everything merely because it is listed below.

Already agreed for the next Pages-oriented cleanup:
1. Prevent deletion of the last remaining page in a document.
2. Remove the temporary legacy `Purge old graph image` Pages button/tool.

Candidates that remain observational/deferred until more evidence:
3. Canceled-pinch recovery/anchor reconciliation.
4. iPad-only reduction of the 6 MP render ceiling (candidate range discussed: ~4.0–4.5 MP).
5. Any additional memory policy beyond the existing 5.7.37 explicit canvas release.
6. Any Pen/Highlighter/Select rewrite — strongly NOT indicated by current diagnostics.

PRESERVE AS VALIDATED / DO NOT REGRESS
---------------------------------------
- 5.7.37 explicit canvas backing-store release before viewer replacement and when leaving Split.
- 1×1 lazy initial canvases.
- Presentation document-selector input/change/reconcile hardening.
- 5.7.36 compact editable-backup manifest and whole-Library PDF export fix.
- 5.7.36 diagnostic snapshot-before-recovery behavior.

- Current Google-modeled Pen geometry/live rendering architecture.
- Current PDF link policy: untouched pass-through preserves source bytes; rebuilt PDFs preserve external URI links and strip internal/document-navigation links; outlines/bookmarks are not rebuilt.

WHY 5.7.38 EXISTS
-----------------
- The user reported that Pages -> Purge old graph image did not remove the legacy graph-paper background from two uploaded documents:
  1. `Logic Slides 4 - Equivalence.pdf`
  2. `15 FormsOfFractions.pdf`
- Inspection of the editable backup showed both PDFs contain a shared 2048×1536 JPEG graph-paper image, but its exact encoding differs from the single fingerprint supported by 5.7.27-5.7.37.
- The newly observed JPEG is 206,281 compressed bytes with SHA-256 `9a13ca9e7a370e5fd51914a19e185eb285055c3657d602f83a39e9afbfe27439`.
- Visual inspection confirms it is the same pale-blue full-page graph-paper artwork family targeted by the temporary migration tool.
- `15 FormsOfFractions.pdf` also contains an unrelated 909×233 JPEG on page 27; it must NOT be removed.

5.7.38 CHANGE
-------------
- Replace the single legacy graph-background signature object with an exact-signature list.
- Preserve the original verified signature:
  * 2048×1536 JPEG
  * 200,613 compressed bytes
  * SHA-256 `bb72bdfa722481c2bee58ff3d9d54a1646607d9f1aeccdd316404411fa9e8530`
- Add the second verified signature:
  * 2048×1536 JPEG
  * 206,281 compressed bytes
  * SHA-256 `9a13ca9e7a370e5fd51914a19e185eb285055c3657d602f83a39e9afbfe27439`
- Matching remains exact: image subtype, width, height, compressed byte length, and SHA-256 must match one of the verified signatures.
- Do NOT add perceptual hashing, color/pattern guessing, page-coverage heuristics, or generic large-image removal.
- Replacement semantics are unchanged: only the matching shared image XObject is replaced by the empty Form XObject, leaving page content streams intact.

VALIDATION
----------
- Uploaded backup integrity passed.
- Both uploaded PDFs contain the new exact 206,281-byte fingerprint.
- `Logic Slides 4 - Equivalence.pdf`: one unique image, the verified graph background.
- `15 FormsOfFractions.pdf`: the verified graph background plus one unrelated 909×233 JPEG; only the former matches the expanded purge list.
- JavaScript syntax, release consistency, targeted fingerprint assertions, and packaged ZIP integrity must pass before distribution.

CURRENT BASELINE
----------------
- Official release remains 5.7.21.
- Current experimental/code baseline is 5.7.37-exp.
- 5.7.37 is deliberately a narrow iPad viewer-memory + Presentation document-selector stability revision built from 5.7.36-exp.

2026-09-16 CLASSROOM DIAGNOSTIC RESULT
--------------------------------------
- A Pen nonresponse episode recovered after tapping a Pen color button; diagnostics were saved immediately afterward.
- A second nonresponse episode occurred after Select and recovered after tapping a toolbar button; diagnostics were then saved.
- In both episodes the preceding viewer gesture ended normally and known pointer capture was released. There was then a multi-second interval in which no attempted viewer pointer/touch contact reached Workbench before a toolbar Pencil event arrived.
- No event-loop stall or render backlog accompanied those pre-restart freezes. Keep treating at least this class of nonresponse as upstream browser/iPadOS input delivery rather than a Pen/Select algorithm stall.
- Removing the 5.7.35 CSS brightness filter in 5.7.36 did not eliminate the problem; do not pursue that as the root cause.
- In Presentation Split, the document selector visibly accepted a different document but one pane did not change. Leaving Split and returning restored normal switching. Diagnostics showed selector interactions without a corresponding `presentation-document-change`, followed by a normal change after the layout round trip.
- Switching to already-open 209-page `AnalysisText.pdf` caused the entire iPad PWA to restart. Saved snapshots from earlier in class survived; only the final unsaved in-memory ring immediately before termination was lost.
- The pre-restart 09:31 snapshot showed Split already OFF but 25 page stages / 75 canvases still attached, with an estimated ~263 MB RGBA backing footprint while the displayed document had only 9 pages. Code review confirmed leaving Split hid/disconnected split viewers but did not explicitly collapse/detach their canvas backing stores.
- The first post-restart snapshot (~10 seconds into the fresh runtime) showed AnalysisText active, 217 canvases, slow initial 6 MP page renders, an ~835 ms event-loop gap, and a ~1.3 s Library persist. This is consistent with WebKit memory pressure, though not proof of the exact termination mechanism.

5.7.37 CHANGES
--------------
1. VIEWER CANVAS BACKING-STORE RELEASE
   - Adds `releaseViewerDom(viewer, reason)`.
   - Before an outgoing single viewer or split-pane viewer DOM is replaced, every canvas backing store is explicitly collapsed to 1×1, then the old DOM is detached.
   - Entering Split releases the outgoing single-view canvases first.
   - Leaving Split releases BOTH hidden split-pane viewer DOM/canvas stores instead of merely hiding them.
   - Rebuilding a split pane for a document/zoom/layout change releases that pane's outgoing canvases first.
   - Rebuilding the single viewer for a document/zoom/layout change does the same.
   - A compact `viewer-dom-release` diagnostic records reason, canvas count, page-stage count, outgoing backing pixels, and an estimated RGBA MB footprint.

2. LAZY PAGE CANVASES START AT 1×1
   - Newly created single/split page canvases explicitly start at 1×1 rather than the HTML canvas default 300×150.
   - This avoids roughly 180 KB of unused RGBA backing memory per unrendered canvas; on a 209-page document the default backing stores alone would otherwise be roughly 38 MB before visible pages render.
   - Visible pages still resize to the same existing bounded raster targets when their render actually begins.

3. PRESENTATION DOCUMENT SELECTOR HARDENING
   - Presentation document switching now listens to BOTH native `input` and `change`.
   - A blur-time reconciliation compares the select's visible value with the document actually assigned to the active pane/single viewer and repairs a mismatch if needed.
   - Duplicate native events are harmless because the selected id is compared with the actual displayed document before switching.
   - New diagnostics: `presentation-document-select-event` includes event source/value/context; `presentation-document-select-reconcile` records visible select value vs actual document and whether the repair path is needed.
   - Keep the native selector for now. Replace it with a Workbench popover/list only if this hardening still fails in real iPad use.

UNCHANGED / PRESERVE
--------------------
- 5.7.36 pre-recovery diagnostic snapshot + conservative transient-input cleanup.
- Pen/Highlighter Google-modeled geometry, exact Pencil replay filtering, partial Eraser, Select semantics, one-stroke Undo granularity, and live Pen release architecture.
- 5.7.33 pinch-release settling and current finger/stylus routing.
- PDF render pixel caps and bounded render queue; this revision changes disposal/allocation timing, not raster fidelity.
- Backup schema/format, compact 5.7.36 backup manifest, Library bulk-safety changes, PDF rebuilt-link policy, Assets/Recent, graph paper, Laser, Presentation thumbnails.

5.7.37 FIELD TEST PRIORITIES
----------------------------
1. iPad classroom use: watch for any full PWA restart when switching to/from large already-open PDFs, especially after using Split.
2. Repeat Single -> Split -> Single several times, then switch to a large document. A saved diagnostic should now show `viewer-dom-release` events and substantially lower hidden-viewer canvas retention.
3. In Presentation Split, repeatedly change each pane's document from the single native document selector. If a visible selection again fails to switch the pane, save diagnostics immediately; the input/change/reconcile events should identify where delivery stopped.
4. Continue using the diagnostic button after any Pen/Select nonresponse. Do not rewrite the Pen/Select pipeline unless a future snapshot shows Workbench receiving and rejecting the missing contact.
5. Regression-check page zoom/pinch, Full Page/Continuous/Page Snap, same-document split, View <-> Presentation transitions, and document scroll-position preservation after the new release-before-rebuild path.
6. Re-test full editable Library backup separately; 5.7.37 does not change the 5.7.36 compact-manifest backup path.

NEXT DECISION
-------------
- If large-document restarts materially improve, keep explicit viewer canvas release as permanent iPad hardening.
- If restarts continue, next memory work should inspect PDF.js/source-page caches and simultaneous active render/source buffers rather than weakening annotation history blindly.
- If Presentation document switching still fails despite input/change/blur reconciliation, replace that native select with a controlled Workbench menu.
- Persistent Pen/Select nonresponse remains a separate upstream-input investigation unless evidence connects it to memory pressure.

5.7.36 CHANGES
--------------
1. WHOLE-LIBRARY PDF ZIP REGRESSION FIX
   - Removes the stray undefined `createFolderEntries` reference accidentally introduced into ordinary whole-Library PDF export in 5.7.35.
   - Normal Library folder entries are emitted unconditionally in this path, preserving empty folders.

2. EDITABLE-BACKUP MEMORY REDUCTION
   - Editable backup manifests now use compact `JSON.stringify(manifest)` rather than pretty-printed JSON.
   - Backup schema/format is unchanged. Restore/Import/Merge compatibility is unchanged.
   - This applies to full and selected-document editable backup manifests for consistency.
   - Re-test full editable backup on iPad and Surface. If iPad still reloads under pressure, investigate streaming/chunking next rather than changing the data model.

3. GLOBAL-SELECTION SAFETY
   - Existing total/current-folder selection count remains and is preserved.
   - Existing Move selected to... dialog already states the total selected count and is preserved.
   - Move selected to Trash now confirms before acting; when the selected documents span multiple folders the confirmation explicitly says so.
   - Cross-folder selection remains intentional and useful; do not weaken it.

4. SAFER PRESSED-BUTTON FEEDBACK
   - Removes the 5.7.35 global CSS `filter: brightness(.80)` from `button:active`.
   - Keeps visible pressed acknowledgement using an inset box shadow only.
   - Reason: current Safari/WebKit 26.x evidence makes CSS filter/compositing changes during active interactions a plausible contributor to broader gesture/input disruption. This is an A/B stability test, not proof of causation.

5. EXPANDED TRANSIENT INPUT DIAGNOSTICS + RECOVERY
   - Diagnostic version increases to 6.
   - Runtime snapshot now includes DOM focus, active Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select/Laser/region-copy state, region-copy armed state, annotation selection count, selected tracked stylus/pointer contact maps, and known pointer-capture state for active gestures.
   - A locally saved diagnostic is fully built and stored BEFORE recovery is attempted.
   - After successful local save, Workbench conservatively clears transient annotation/input bookkeeping and known captures. Incomplete live ink is discarded rather than committed; transient Eraser preview is restored; unfinished Select transforms are restored/cancelled as appropriate; region-copy and Laser transient state is cleared.
   - The diagnostic button therefore remains the preferred recovery button during a freeze because it preserves the evidence first.

UNCHANGED / DO NOT DISTURB WITHOUT EVIDENCE
-------------------------------------------
- 5.7.31 exact Pencil replay guard.
- One completed stroke per Undo step; 50-step session-only history.
- Google-modeled Pen geometry and live Pen O(current-stroke) release architecture.
- 5.7.33 delayed pinch-anchor settling.
- 5.7.32 lightweight render/anomaly diagnostics.

- The user plans additional real-world classroom testing on Surface as well as iPad.
- iPadOS is now normally used in full-screen-app mode. Workbench Split view replaced the user's prior need for iPad windowed multitasking.

WHAT WAS VALIDATED AFTER 5.7.35 WAS PACKAGED
---------------------------------------------
1. Global button-down visual feedback works well.
2. Bulk Move selected to... works in testing.
3. Bulk Move selected to Trash works in testing.
4. Global document selection across folders is useful and SHOULD BE PRESERVED. It allows selecting documents in several folders and moving them all to one destination in one operation.
5. Selected-document editable backup plus Merge was tested across different devices and worked.
6. Existing Grid/List Library view is fine; no change is needed there.
7. Bulk operations remain DOCUMENT-ONLY. Folders retain their existing individual Move/Trash behavior. This simplification is intentional.
8. Empty Trash may permanently remove everything already in Trash, including folder subtrees, subject to the existing export-first safety for unexported changes.

GLOBAL SELECTION SAFETY — IMPORTANT FOLLOW-UP
---------------------------------------------
The global cross-folder selection is valuable, but the UI currently makes it possible to forget that off-screen documents remain selected. The user accidentally bulk-trashed a document in another folder during testing.

Do NOT remove cross-folder selection. Instead, add modest safeguards in a later pass:
- Always show a visible total selection count in Local Library, e.g. "7 selected".
- Prefer also showing current-folder context when useful, e.g. "7 selected · 3 in this folder".
- "Select all" should continue to select documents in the CURRENTLY VIEWED folder only.
- "Clear" should continue to clear the entire shared/global selection.
- Move selected to... should clearly display the total number of globally selected documents being moved.
- Move selected to Trash should require a confirmation showing the total count and, when applicable, that the selection spans multiple folders.
- Keep the safeguards lightweight; do not weaken the useful cross-folder workflow.

HOMEWORK / TEST 1 INCIDENT — FORENSIC RESULT
--------------------------------------------
The user created Analysis/Homework with five files to test bulk delete. Later Homework appeared in Trash, and the user initially believed its five documents had moved into Analysis/Test 1.

Record-level comparison of three editable backups showed:
- The older 2026-09-11 09:59 backup was created by Workbench 5.7.30.
- The 2026-09-13 13:20 backup was created by 5.7.35.
- The 2026-09-13 13:31 backup was created by 5.7.34 after switching back.
- The two 2026-09-13 backups have the same Library structure; switching back to 5.7.34 did not repair or further alter folder assignments.
- The five Homework documents DID NOT move to Test 1. Their stored folderId still points to Homework.
- Homework and those five documents share the same Trash timestamp and the same trashBatchId equal to the Homework folder ID.
- That marker is written by the EXISTING folder-Trade/Trash subtree path, not by the new 5.7.35 document-only Move selected to Trash implementation (which does not assign that folder batch ID).
- Test 1 already contained a different set of files before Homework existed.

Conclusion: stored data shows the existing Homework-folder Trash operation ran and trashed the folder subtree. The backups do not prove whether the user tapped that folder Trash control accidentally or whether some UI event invoked the handler, but there is NO evidence of silent document reassignment/corruption into Test 1. Do not chase a folderId corruption theory unless it reproduces.

FULL EDITABLE LIBRARY BACKUP — iPAD MEMORY/RELOAD ISSUE
-------------------------------------------------------
This problem predates 5.7.35.

Observed behavior:
- On iPad PWA, full editable Library backup sometimes hangs, the PWA is killed/reloaded, and on relaunch the footer shows the normal startup message "Restoring local Library...".
- The user clarified that each failed attempt was a separate backup attempt followed by app reload/relaunch; this was NOT repeated tapping that started overlapping jobs.
- The same full editable backup behavior reproduced on 5.7.34, so it is not caused by the 5.7.35 bulk-management changes.
- On Surface, the same full editable backup completed on the first attempt, but with a noticeable delay.
- Successful full backups restore/merge correctly, so the backup data model itself appears sound.

Strong memory-pressure evidence from the successful current backup:
- Final ZIP is only about 45.8 MB.
- The uncompressed manifest.json is about 236.6 MB because backup code currently uses JSON.stringify(manifest, null, 2).
- The actual source PDF/image payloads total only about 29.8 MB uncompressed.
- A compact JSON.stringify(manifest) would be about 99.5 MB for the same data.
- The older 5.7.30 backup already had a manifest around 216.3 MB, proving the scalability weakness predates 5.7.35.

Likely mechanism:
- During backup creation the PWA may simultaneously hold the live Library object graph, a 200+ MB JSON string, source ArrayBuffers, JSZip structures, and compression/output buffers.
- Surface has enough memory headroom to finish, albeit slowly.
- iPad/WebKit sometimes kills/reloads the PWA under that peak memory pressure.

Recommended FIRST backup fix:
- Change full editable backup manifest serialization from JSON.stringify(manifest, null, 2) to compact JSON.stringify(manifest).
- This is low risk: JSON whitespace is semantically irrelevant to Restore/Import/Merge and requires no backup format/schema bump.
- Re-test full backup on iPad and Surface before attempting more invasive streaming/chunked ZIP work.
- If compact JSON is still insufficient, then investigate streaming/chunking and duplicate in-memory representations.

DEFINITE 5.7.35 REGRESSION — WHOLE-LIBRARY PDF EXPORT
------------------------------------------------------
A coding error was found in 5.7.35 while adding Empty Trash export support:
- An option/variable intended for the Empty Trash export path leaked into the ordinary whole-Library PDF export function as an undefined variable (`createFolderEntries`).
- Ordinary whole-Library PDF export in 5.7.35 can therefore throw.
- This is definitely a 5.7.35 bug and must be fixed in the next code revision.
- Do not conflate it with the full editable-backup iPad reload issue; they are separate problems.

BUTTON FEEDBACK
---------------
The user asked for better acknowledgement that a button was actually pressed. 5.7.35 added only the low-risk global CSS pressed/down state. It works well in testing.
Do NOT add busy spinners, success toasts, or per-command async feedback unless later requested. The user explicitly chose only the CSS button-down feedback for this pass.

CURRENT PEN / INPUT DIAGNOSTIC STATUS TO PRESERVE
-------------------------------------------------
- 5.7.31 exact replay guard is real and useful. Classroom diagnostics showed Safari can replay exact coalesced Pencil batches; the filter removes only exact immediately repeated batches before model/storage ingestion.
- Undo remains one completed stroke per Undo step. snapshotPages() timing was small enough that Undo granularity should NOT be changed based on current evidence.
- Earlier true Pen nonresponse episodes on 5.7.32 showed the last stroke ending normally, then attempted marks with no Pen-classified page events until Pencil later appeared on UI.
- 5.7.34 viewer-level all-pointer/touch-start diagnostics were added to distinguish misclassification from no browser-delivered contact.
- A later fast-writing "skip" on 5.7.34 looked like a very brief/light contact: every delivered stylus contact was accepted normally.
- A stronger later dropped-strokes case showed a roughly 1.9-second gap where the expected marks were absent and NO pointerdown/touchstart/contact of any type reached Workbench. Surrounding Pencil contacts were normal pen/stylus and accepted.
- Therefore at least some dropped-stroke incidents appear upstream of the Workbench Pen pipeline (Safari/iPadOS/hardware contact), not Workbench discarding valid Pencil input.
- Do not broadly rewrite Pen code without new evidence.
- A future very narrow diagnostic, only if needed, could record the anomalous case where a pen pointermove/rawupdate indicates tip-down pressure/buttons WITHOUT a preceding pointerdown. Do not add a general move log.

RENDER / PINCH STATUS TO PRESERVE
---------------------------------
- Intermittent orphaned page "Rendering..." state remains a real issue. Diagnostics previously caught a connected page still marked loading while render queue/active render sets were idle. Keep the lightweight render diagnostics until root cause is confirmed/fixed.
- A real post-pinch jump was captured on iPad: live pinch anchoring was correct at finger-up, then Safari applied a delayed layout/scroll correction. 5.7.33 added two-frame anchor settling after pinch release; keep it.
- Laser tool from 5.7.33 is transient screen-only and should remain non-persistent/non-exported.
- Presentation thumbnail navigation has tested well and should remain permanent.

5.7.35 NEXT-REVISION PRIORITY ORDER
-----------------------------------
Recommended order when coding resumes:
1. Start from 5.7.35-exp, not an older build.
2. Fix the definite whole-Library PDF export `createFolderEntries` regression.
3. Compact full editable-backup manifest JSON (remove pretty-print whitespace) and test iPad/Surface backup reliability and time.
4. Add lightweight global-selection safety cues/confirmations, preserving cross-folder selection.
5. Re-run quick regressions for bulk Move, bulk Trash, individual folder Move/Trash, Empty Trash, selected editable backup + Merge, whole-Library PDF export, and full editable backup.
6. Continue ordinary classroom testing on Surface and iPad; do not disturb proven Pen/render/pinch instrumentation unless evidence requires it.

IMPORTANT 5.7.35 TESTING INTERPRETATION
---------------------------------------
- The new bulk document Move/Trash mechanics themselves have tested successfully so far.
- The accidental off-screen delete is a UI-safety problem caused by persistent global selection, not evidence that the bulk implementation moved/trashes the wrong IDs.
- The Homework incident is consistent with the existing folder subtree Trash path, not the new bulk document Trash path.
- The full editable-backup PWA reload is a preexisting scalability/memory problem, not a 5.7.35 bulk-management regression.
- The whole-Library PDF export undefined-variable issue IS a genuine new 5.7.35 regression.

PDF WORKBENCH — MILESTONE 5.7.35 DEVELOPMENT HANDOFF
======================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE

5.7.35 LIBRARY BULK MANAGEMENT + GLOBAL BUTTON PRESS FEEDBACK
-------------------------------------------------------------
- Adds one global CSS `button:not(:disabled):active` treatment so enabled buttons visibly darken/inset while finger, Apple Pencil, or mouse is physically down. This is visual acknowledgement only; no command timing or async behavior changes.
- Local Library now has a compact selection row: Select all (documents in the currently viewed Library folder), Clear (the shared global document selection), Move selected to…, and Move selected to Trash. The move chooser reuses the existing Library folder hierarchy. Bulk document moves preserve selection and work for open or closed documents; bulk Trash closes any selected open documents and clears those IDs from the shared selection.
- Adds Trash → Empty Trash…. It permanently removes everything already in Trash, including complete trashed folder trees and individually trashed documents. If any trashed document has unexported changes, the user can export all trashed PDFs as a ZIP before deletion, permanently empty without export, or cancel.
- No List/Grid redesign was made; the existing remembered List/Grid Library view remains unchanged.
- 5.7.34 all-pointer viewer-contact diagnostics, View diagnostic button, 5.7.33 pinch-release stabilization/Laser, render diagnostics, Pencil replay filter, annotation model, PDF output, backup format, DB/schema versions, and diagnostics bounds are unchanged.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
1. Confirm every enabled button gives an obvious pressed appearance on iPad Pencil/finger and desktop mouse without disturbing layout.
2. In Local Library, select documents in one or multiple folders; Move selected to… should move all to the chosen folder and preserve their checked selection.
3. Move selected to Trash should close selected open documents, remove them from the shared selection, and place them in Trash.
4. Empty Trash with only exported documents should show a simple irreversible confirmation. With at least one changed document, verify Export PDFs ZIP & empty Trash / Empty Trash permanently / Cancel.
5. Regression-check individual Rename/Duplicate/Move/Trash, folder subtree Trash/Restore/Delete, List/Grid, selected editable backup, and normal PDF export.


PDF WORKBENCH — MILESTONE 5.7.34 DEVELOPMENT HANDOFF
======================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE
----------------
- 5.7.21 remains the official current release.
- 5.7.34 is the current development/diagnostic branch.

5.7.34 ALL-CLASSIFICATION VIEWER-CONTACT DIAGNOSTICS + VIEW SAVE BUTTON
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
- Two classroom Pen-nonresponse episodes on 5.7.32 showed the last accepted stroke completing normally, pointer capture releasing normally, no active gesture remaining, no render/event-loop/snapshot stall, and then no Pen-classified viewer contact at all during the user's attempted marks. The next Pencil input appeared on UI controls.
- The old global raw logger intentionally recorded `pointerType=pen` everywhere and touch contacts in the viewer, but did not retain an unexpected viewer `pointerType=mouse`/other classification. Therefore the evidence could not distinguish "Safari delivered nothing" from "Safari delivered the Pencil under a different identity."
- 5.7.34 adds a second, boundary-only viewer-contact diagnostic path while Laser/Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select is active. It records every viewer pointer down/up/cancel regardless of `pointerType`; it does NOT retain an extra move stream. Each boundary includes classification, viewer, event-target relationship, and the top hit-tested element with pointer-events/position/z-index so an intercepting overlay can be recognized.
- Adds one compact viewer `touchstart` summary containing only changed/active touch IDs, Safari `touchType`, coordinates, target, and viewer. Existing stylus TouchEvent fallback remains unchanged.
- Adds an icon-only Save diagnostics locally button in ordinary View immediately before Present. It calls the same local-save path as the Presentation diagnostic button, so the user no longer needs to change modes before capturing a Pen failure.
- Diagnostic bounds are unchanged: 2,400 general in-memory records, 96 recent render transitions, and 24 preserved render anomalies. The 5.7.33 pinch fix, Laser tool, render diagnostics, exact Pencil replay filtering, Undo granularity, Presentation thumbnail navigation, structured graph-paper handling, colored Highlighter/Eraser cues, and ZIP-DEFLATE backups are unchanged.

VALIDATION TARGET
-----------------
- Update to 5.7.34 and use normally. If Pen stops responding, tap the new View diagnostic button immediately, before switching modes or documents if practical. If failed Pencil contacts arrive as `touch`, `mouse`, or another classification, `viewer-contact-*` / `viewer-touchstart-summary` should expose it; if nothing is logged, the absence itself is stronger evidence that Safari/iPadOS did not deliver a viewer contact to the page.


PDF WORKBENCH — MILESTONE 5.7.33 DEVELOPMENT HANDOFF
======================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE
----------------
- 5.7.21 remains the official current release.
- 5.7.33 is the current development/diagnostic branch.

5.7.33 PINCH-RELEASE STABILIZATION + LASER POINTER
-------------------------------------------------
- 5.7.32 diagnostics captured a real split-view pinch jump: live pinch finalization correctly preserved the page-67 anchor, then Safari/iPad applied a deferred layout/scroll correction about one to two frames later and Workbench landed around pages 85–89.
- The live pinch calculation is therefore unchanged. At the end of the whole touch gesture, Workbench now retains the final pinch page anchor/midpoint and reasserts it on two consecutive animation frames using the existing proven `restoreViewerAnchorAfterLayout()` strategy. Scroll-state persistence is suppressed during those two frames, then the settled position becomes authoritative.
- If one finger intentionally continues panning after the pinch, more than 8 px of accumulated post-pinch pan disables the delayed re-anchor so the user's deliberate movement is preserved.
- 5.7.32 lightweight renderer/navigation diagnostics remain enabled, including the 96-event rolling render history and 24 preserved render anomalies. A compact `pinch-anchor-settled` event records whether the delayed anchor was applied and the resulting scroll position.
- Added a Laser pointer tool directly between Hand and Pen. Laser is transient screen chrome only: a bright red dot appears at the stylus/mouse contact while down and disappears immediately on release/cancel/tool change. It creates no document annotation, Undo/Redo entry, autosave state, Library content, or PDF output. Apple Pencil TouchEvent fallback is supported as well as normal PointerEvents.
- Finger navigation remains available in Laser mode under the existing stylus/palm-intent rules.
- Validated classroom features remain unchanged: Presentation thumbnail navigation, ZIP-DEFLATE Library compression/backups, colored Highlighter/Eraser icons, and structured Add graph paper background.
- Exact Pencil replay filtering and Undo snapshot timing from 5.7.31 remain unchanged. No render scheduling fix is included yet; keep diagnostics active for the intermittent orphaned `Rendering…` investigation.

VALIDATION TARGETS
------------------
1. In Presentation Split view, repeatedly pinch in/out in either pane and verify release stays on the same logical page/anchor instead of jumping many pages.
2. After pinching, deliberately keep one finger down and pan; verify the continued pan is preserved rather than snapped back.
3. Select Laser: Pencil-down shows a bright red dot following the tip; Pencil-up hides it. Verify no Undo entry is added and exported PDF/Library state is unchanged.
4. Continue collecting diagnostics if any page remains stuck at `Rendering…`; switch documents and back if needed, then save diagnostics as before.


PDF WORKBENCH — MILESTONE 5.7.32 DEVELOPMENT HANDOFF
======================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE
----------------
- 5.7.21 remains the official current release.
- 5.7.32 is the current development/diagnostic branch.


5.7.32 LIGHTWEIGHT RENDER / NAVIGATION DIAGNOSTICS
---------------------------------------------------
- The second 2026-09-11 classroom session with 5.7.31 produced no Pen stalls. The exact replay guard was active in ordinary writing, while `snapshotPages()` timing remained far below the prior multi-second stalls (1,105 calls, about 11.9 ms average, 60 ms maximum). Undo granularity therefore remains one completed stroke per Undo step.
- A late-class rendering incident was reproduced by ordinary scrolling in the Chapter 5.2 slides: the next page remained at `page-loading`; nearby pages appeared frozen while scrolling; switching documents and back restored normal display. An earlier report also caught a page still marked loading while the render queue and active-render set were idle.
- 5.7.32 intentionally does NOT change render scheduling yet. Instead it keeps a bounded rolling history of the last 96 viewer-render transitions (request/start/complete/release/retry/skip), plus a separate 24-entry anomaly buffer for renders that return while a connected stage is still marked loading or that error. This anomaly buffer survives a document round-trip long enough to be present in a report saved immediately afterward.
- Loading-stage diagnostics include page/document identity, page number, graph-background presence, request ID, loading age, `wantRender`, and queue state. Render request IDs tie the stage state to the rolling render history.
- Added a specific diagnostic for a page that leaves the observer margin while still marked loading. This is important because the current queue can legitimately skip stale/offscreen work; the next classroom occurrence should show whether a skipped queued render is leaving the stage in an orphaned loading state.
- Added compact pinch start/finish diagnostics: start/final zoom, finger separation, midpoint, anchor page, and viewer scroll offsets. Navigation behavior itself is unchanged.
- Presentation document changes now record exact from/to document IDs and names. This removes the ambiguity seen in the 5.7.31 report, where two selector taps were visible but the intermediate document (`rolls.pdf`) could only be inferred.
- Explicit diagnostic snapshots now count structured graph-paper-background pages per open document and identify whether any loading/rendering page has that background. The graph-paper renderer itself is unchanged.
- Positive classroom validation to preserve: Presentation thumbnail navigation works well; ZIP-DEFLATE Library backup compression works well; the colored Highlighter/Eraser icon cues are effective; and the structured Add graph paper background feature appears to be working well in real use.
- No changes to Pen/Highlighter geometry, Google Ink model parameters, replay filtering, Undo history, touch/pinch behavior, render scheduling, thumbnail navigation, backup compression, graph-paper rendering/export, or annotation icon behavior.


5.7.31 EXACT PENCIL-BATCH REPLAY GUARD + UNDO SNAPSHOT TIMING
----------------------------------------------------------------
- Classroom diagnostics from 2026-09-11 showed that Safari can replay an entire coalesced Apple Pencil batch verbatim. 5.7.31 rejects only an exact replay of the immediately previous coalesced move batch. Every raw `(timestamp, clientX, clientY, pressure)` value must match exactly; there is no rounding, tolerance, or path-similarity test.
- The replay decision occurs before `appendInkPoint()`, Google Ink modeler ingestion, live preview processing, or annotation storage. Counters record exact previous-batch replays, replay batches skipped, and replay samples skipped.
- Removed the 5.7.28 temporary per-batch summary arrays, recent raw-sample window/set, overlapping-batch bookkeeping, and per-stroke anomaly arrays. Completed/active stroke diagnostics now retain compact counters only.
- `snapshotPages()` is timed on every call. Runtime diagnostics retain calls, last/average/max milliseconds, >=16 ms and >=50 ms counts, and last page count. Pen/Highlighter begin and finish diagnostics include that stroke's snapshot time.
- Undo granularity is NOT changed: one completed stroke still creates one Undo step; history remains capped at 50 and still uses packed Float32Array point storage. Do not group multiple strokes merely to mask snapshot cost.
- Existing Library documents are NOT cleaned or rewritten in this build. The live replay fix should be tested first. A later one-time cleanup should be tested only on a copy/backup and visually compared before touching the real Library.
- Pen geometry/model parameters, Highlighter geometry, touch/pinch behavior, 5.7.30 Files/Pages scroll preservation, permanent Presentation thumbnails, structured graph-paper backgrounds, and ZIP-DEFLATE backups are unchanged.



5.7.29 PEN REGRESSION FIX + DIRECT FILES SCROLL PRESERVATION

- Fixed a 5.7.28-only typo in the new Pencil-batch diagnostic collector. `recordInkGestureSampleBatch()` referenced `exactRecentRawRepeats` instead of the local `exactRecentRepeats`, causing a ReferenceError on every Pen/Highlighter move event. Eraser was unaffected because it uses a separate gesture path. Diagnostics from 2026-09-11 confirmed thousands of this exact ReferenceError and an active stroke stuck at one accepted point.
- Hardened the temporary batch diagnostics so an instrumentation exception is caught/logged once and can never stop Pen/Highlighter drawing.
- Fixed the older direct View -> Files/Pages -> View scroll reset: `showWorkspaceMode()` now saves the visible single-view scroll state before the viewer is hidden, or both pane scroll states in Split view. This is separate from the already-fixed Assets/Templates transient Files round-trip path.
- Graph-paper backgrounds created by 5.7.26+ do not render in 5.7.21 because 5.7.21 predates that renderer. The older page cloning path spreads unknown page properties, so the structured background metadata is preserved, but old builds should not be used to judge/export this feature.
- Permanent post-5.7.21 features to carry forward: Presentation thumbnail navigator; structured Pages graph-paper background; Highlighter/Eraser icon color cues; ZIP-DEFLATE editable backups. Temporary/support items: exact legacy graph-image purge; classroom diagnostic controls. Experimental: document-only Merge with backup.


5.7.30 DIRECT FILES/PAGES ROUND-TRIP SCROLL PRESERVATION

- Fixed the remaining ordinary View → Files/Pages → View scroll-to-top regression.
- Root cause: 5.7.29 saved the current viewer position before hiding View, but Files rendering immediately ran a normal state save. The parent `viewerPane` was hidden while the child `viewer` itself was not marked hidden, so the later save read the hidden-layout scrollTop (typically 0) and overwrote the correct saved position.
- The single-view state saver now reads DOM scroll only while `workspaceMode === 'view'` and both `viewerPane` and `viewer` are actually visible.
- This is a narrow viewer-state fix. Pen/Highlighter, Pencil diagnostics, rendering, touch/pinch, graph-paper background, thumbnail navigation, and backup compression are unchanged from 5.7.29.

5.7.28 LIGHTWEIGHT PENCIL-BATCH DIAGNOSTICS + COMPRESSED EDITABLE BACKUPS

- Added lightweight per-stroke raw/coalesced Pencil diagnostics so ordinary class use can reveal whether Safari is replaying batches or Workbench is simply oversampling.
- Each stroke now records bounded counters for raw samples seen, accepted points, threshold rejections, exact recent raw-sample repeats, repeats from the immediately previous batch, exact whole-batch replays, and non-increasing raw timestamps. The active stroke summary is included in a saved runtime diagnostic snapshot; completed strokes also emit a summary in the normal diagnostic log.
- The instrumentation is intentionally lightweight: no extra persistence during drawing, no additional geometry/DOM reads, and only small bounded arrays kept in memory.
- Editable full-Library backups and selected-document editable backups now use ZIP DEFLATE compression (level 6) instead of STORE. The backup format is unchanged, so Restore / Import backup as folder / Merge continue to work normally while the files become much smaller.
- Pen/Pencil behavior itself, touch/pinch navigation, and annotation geometry remain unchanged in this revision.

5.7.27 TEMPORARY LEGACY POWERPOINT GRAPH-BACKGROUND PURGE
---------------------------------------------------------
- Pages now has a temporary **Purge old graph image** action for selected PDF pages.
- It is intentionally a migration aid, not a permanent feature.
- Matching is exact: 2048×1536 JPEG, 200,613 compressed bytes, SHA-256 bb72bdfa722481c2bee58ff3d9d54a1646607d9f1aeccdd316404411fa9e8530. This is the same shared graph-paper image verified in the user's older PowerPoint PDFs. No generic background guessing is performed.
- The cleanup uses pdf-lib low-level objects to replace only that exact image XObject with an empty Form XObject. It does not parse or rewrite the rest of the page drawing commands.
- A cleaned derived PDF source is created per affected source PDF and only selected Workbench pages are repointed to it. Unselected pages retain the original source. The original source remains present for Undo during the session.
- The permanent Presentation thumbnail drawer and permanent structured Add graph paper background feature must be retained going forward.
- Temporary/experimental items remain: classroom diagnostics, selected editable backup, document-only Merge with backup, and the legacy graph-image purge button.
- Do not change Pen/Pencil/touch/pinch behavior based on this migration feature.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 5.7.26 DEVELOPMENT / DIAGNOSTIC UPDATE
=======================================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE STATUS
-----------------------
- Official current release remains 5.7.21 until this development branch is promoted.
- The Presentation thumbnail navigator (introduced in 5.7.23) is now explicitly a PERMANENT feature to retain.
- The new Pages graph-paper background is also a PERMANENT feature to retain.
- Temporary diagnostics, selected-document editable backup, and Merge with backup remain support/experimental features and should not be confused with the permanent UI decisions above.

MILESTONE 5.7.26 — ADD GRAPH PAPER BACKGROUND
---------------------------------------------
- Pages toolbar adds **Add graph paper background**; it is enabled when one or more pages are selected and applies directly to those selected pages.
- Background is NOT an image annotation and NOT a raster asset. It is structured page metadata:
  `background: { type: 'graph-paper', style: 'workbench-graph-v1', version: 1, settings: {...} }`
- Settings currently store target spacing, minimum margin, line/edge RGB colors, opacities, and line widths even though 5.7.26 does not expose controls for them. This is deliberate so later Remove / Change / style editing can replace metadata instead of migrating to a new model.
- Existing generated graph-paper pages are unchanged. Applying Add to an intrinsic generated graph page is treated as already graph paper rather than adding a duplicate layer.
- Screen rendering reuses the current procedural graph-paper drawing primitive. Imported PDF content is rendered with a transparent PDF.js background only when a Workbench graph background is active, so the underlying grid shows through transparent source areas. No separate full-page graph bitmap is introduced.
- Page-size normalization and crop/margin geometry remain compatible: the background belongs to the final visible page while source content is painted over it.
- Export: background pages cannot use untouched-passthrough PDF export. For imported PDF pages, a compact vector grid content stream is prepended before copied source content. This keeps the graph behind source content without rasterizing the slide or flattening its URI annotations. Generated/image pages draw the same procedural vector grid before their normal content.
- Page duplication, copy-to, Undo/Redo, Local Library persistence, editable backup and restore naturally retain the background because page cloning/persistence carries the structured field.
- 5.7.26 exposes Add only. No Remove/Change/settings UI yet.

PRESENTATION THUMBNAIL MEMORY HYGIENE
-------------------------------------
- User reported the thumbnail navigator worked very well in real classroom use and asked whether it could add to iPad memory pressure.
- Yes, modestly: the original drawer created one `<canvas>` per document page; a newly-created canvas defaults to a 300×150 backing store even before lazy rendering, and the hidden drawer retained those backing stores after closing.
- 5.7.26 starts unrendered drawer canvases at 1×1 and explicitly zeros/removes every drawer canvas on close. Nearby thumbnails are still lazily rendered through the existing render queue when the drawer is opened.
- This is a targeted memory-hygiene change only. The thumbnail UI, navigation semantics, and page-jump behavior are unchanged.
- The drawer alone is not considered sufficient evidence for the observed 2–4 second iPad stalls; continue collecting 5.7.24+ diagnostics before changing Pen/PDF rendering algorithms.

PRESERVE
--------
- Do not remove the Presentation thumbnail navigator in a future cleanup; it is now a validated classroom feature.
- Do not collapse page backgrounds into image annotations. Keep the structured page-background layer.
- Do not change Pen/Pencil modeler, touch/pinch behavior, or annotation geometry in response to the current stall investigation without diagnostic evidence.
- DB version remains 4 and Library schema remains 8; optional page metadata requires no migration.


PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 5.7.25 EXPERIMENTAL UPDATE
======================================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE REMAINS 5.7.21.

5.7.25 adds one diagnostic/transfer convenience on top of experimental 5.7.24:
- Files → Library backup & export → Back up selected documents (editable).
- Uses the existing global selected-document set.
- Saves current editable state, packages only those document records and the source PDF/image binaries their pages/image annotations reference, plus needed ancestor folder records.
- Does not include unrelated documents/sources, Templates, Assets/Recent, Trash, preferences, or session state.
- Output is a partial `.pwbbackup.zip`. Import backup as folder and experimental Merge may consume it. Full Restore explicitly refuses it to prevent replacing the Library with a subset.
- Source payloads are fetched one at a time from IndexedDB to avoid cloning the entire (potentially 200 MB+) source store into memory.
- 5.7.24 diagnostics and colored Highlighter/Eraser icon cues remain unchanged.
- Presentation thumbnail drawer from 5.7.23 remains unchanged.
- Pen/touch/pinch/viewer/render behavior is unchanged.

USER CONTEXT FOR THIS CHANGE
----------------------------
The user's whole editable Local Library backup had grown beyond 200 MB and iPad upload to Drive was no longer practical. Three PDFs involved in classroom rendering/stall symptoms were available as conventional Workbench exports, but those exports cannot reveal the original source records and editable annotation/page state. This selected-document editable package exists so only the implicated documents can be captured and uploaded for diagnosis.

CURRENT EXPERIMENTAL BRANCH 5.7.24 — CLASSROOM DIAGNOSTICS / ICON COLOR CUES
============================================================================

OFFICIAL RELEASE BASELINE
-------------------------
- Official current PDF Workbench remains **5.7.21**.
- 5.7.24 is experimental and is based on 5.7.23. Keep the successful 5.7.23 Presentation page-thumbnail drawer and the dormant/tested 5.7.22 Merge with backup code.
- User reported many ~2 second Pen nonresponsive incidents in class, a couple whole-iPad/App freezes around 3–4 seconds, and three cases where a page remained stuck Rendering while three documents were in use/rendering. This makes temporary expanded diagnostics appropriate; do not change the Pen model based on these symptoms yet.

5.7.24 TOOL ICON COLOR CUES
----------------------------
- Keep the existing Highlighter and Eraser silhouettes.
- Highlighter: fill the formerly clear portion of the icon yellow (`#ffeb3b`).
- Eraser: fill the interior pink (`#ff80ab`).
- This is a student-requested distinction cue only; tool behavior and palettes are unchanged.

5.7.24 QUICK LOCAL DIAGNOSTIC SNAPSHOT
--------------------------------------
- Temporary compact diagnostics button is at the far right of the Presentation toolbar.
- Tapping it saves immediately into Workbench local IndexedDB; it does NOT invoke download/share UI and does NOT leave Presentation.
- Diagnostics are stored using the existing `meta` object store, one record per snapshot plus a small index; no DB/schema migration.
- Up to 12 snapshots are retained. Saving a new snapshot does not clone/rewrite the payloads of earlier snapshots.
- Files → Library backup & export shows the saved count and provides Export saved diagnostics / Clear saved diagnostics. One saved snapshot exports as TXT; multiple export as ZIP.
- Saved diagnostics are device-local and intentionally not part of the editable Library backup format.

EXPANDED DIAGNOSTIC COVERAGE
----------------------------
Existing Pencil boundary diagnostics remain. Added:
- event-loop heartbeat every 250 ms, logging only gaps >=700 ms;
- focus/blur and visibility changes;
- window errors and unhandled promise rejections;
- render wrapper for every `renderPageToCanvas` call, maintaining an in-memory active-render map;
- slow-render records >=750 ms and render-error records;
- snapshot-time renderQueue active/queued/max, active-render age/page/source/context, page-stage loading/rendered/error counts;
- open document names/IDs/page counts and loaded source counts/byte sizes;
- live DOM canvas count/pixels and approximate 4-byte-per-pixel RGBA backing estimate;
- decoded image approximate RGBA bytes where an image is decoded;
- viewport/DPR and navigator hardware concurrency/deviceMemory where exposed;
- `performance.memory` heap fields only where the browser exposes that nonstandard API;
- navigator.storage estimate for context (storage, not RAM).

MEMORY-DIAGNOSIS LIMITATION
---------------------------
- iPad Safari does not expose a reliable webpage API for total Safari/PWA process RAM, PDF.js worker RAM, decoded PDF image RAM, or GPU/canvas backing-store RAM. Direct heap fields will likely be null on iPad.
- Therefore these diagnostics can identify strong memory-pressure patterns (many/large live canvases, active/queued renders, long/stuck render promises, large loaded sources, event-loop stalls) but cannot prove a specific total-memory number or an iPadOS memory-pressure event.
- Do not describe approximate canvas/source byte counts as total memory usage.

HOW TO USE IN CLASS
-------------------
1. After a ~2 second Pen pause, tap the far-right diagnostics button as soon as practical. The save-request record acts as an incident marker immediately after the symptom.
2. If the whole app/iPad freezes for several seconds, tap after it recovers; the heartbeat should show whether the JavaScript event loop itself paused.
3. If a page remains on Rendering, tap before scrolling away or otherwise forcing a retry when possible. Active-render age and queue state are especially useful then.
4. After class, Files → Library backup & export → Export saved diagnostics. Upload the TXT/ZIP plus the PDFs involved in any stuck-render incident.

INTERPRETATION GUIDE
--------------------
- Pen gap + heartbeat continues normally + no raw Pencil events: points again toward WebKit/iPad Pencil event delivery rather than Workbench handler rejection.
- Pen/app freeze + matching event-loop-gap: main JavaScript/browser process was stalled or suspended for that interval.
- Page stuck Rendering + one/two active renders with large age and queued work behind them: render/PDF.js/resource pressure becomes a strong suspect.
- Many large live canvases / large DPR raster targets at the same time strengthen a memory/GPU-pressure hypothesis, but remain indirect evidence.
- A slow Library persist overlapping the incident will still be visible through the existing library-persist diagnostics.

PRESERVE
--------
- 5.7.23 page thumbnail drawer behavior and current button arrangement. User liked it on first test and wants more real-world use before rearranging controls.
- 5.7.22 merge code stays but sync-via-full-backup is on hold because transfer size is tedious; merge logic itself was validated iPad → Surface.
- Pen/Highlighter/eraser algorithms, Google Ink model, 5.6.9 touch/pinch behavior, viewer navigation, page geometry, and Library schema must not change for this diagnostic experiment.

TEST PRIORITIES
---------------
- Confirm yellow Highlighter cue and pink Eraser fill remain legible in compact iPad Presentation.
- Confirm diagnostics button is reachable at the far right in ordinary single-pane Pen Presentation at iPad landscape width.
- Tap diagnostics during normal use and confirm it returns immediately with a Saved diagnostics locally status and no iPad share sheet.
- Open Files → Library backup & export and confirm count appears; export one snapshot as TXT and several as ZIP; Clear deletes them after confirmation.
- Induce ordinary page rendering/navigation and confirm no new visible performance regression from the lightweight instrumentation.

PRIOR 5.7.22 EXPERIMENTAL HANDOFF FOLLOWS
=====================================================================

CURRENT EXPERIMENTAL BRANCH 5.7.22 — MERGE WITH BACKUP

OFFICIAL RELEASE STATUS
- Official current PDF Workbench release remains **5.7.21**.
- 5.7.22 is an experimental branch only. Do not use it as the release baseline unless the merge experiment is explicitly promoted later.

EXPERIMENTAL MERGE RULES
- Files → Library backup & export → **Merge with backup… β** uses the existing `.pwbbackup.zip` format.
- Documents are matched by their preserved stable Library document ID, never by filename.
- Compare `modifiedAt`; use `createdAt` only as a fallback when `modifiedAt` is unavailable.
- Backup newer: accept the backup record under the original stable document ID and copy the displaced older local version to Trash under a new internal document ID.
- Local newer: keep local and ignore the backup record.
- Equal timestamp: keep local.
- Backup-only active document: add it under its original stable document ID.
- Backup documents already in backup Trash are ignored. A same-ID document already in local Trash is left in Trash and blocks resurrection in this first experiment.
- Folder handling is deliberately conservative: if the backup document points to a folder ID that already exists locally, use it; otherwise a replacement keeps its existing local folder and a backup-only document goes to Library root.
- Templates, Assets, folder structure, and Trash/deletion reconciliation are not merged in this experiment.
- Replaced documents that are currently open remain open, but their in-memory state is swapped to the accepted newer backup state so a later autosave cannot overwrite the merge.
- Pen/touch/viewer/annotation machinery is unchanged.

TEST PRIORITIES
1. Make a backup, modify one document locally, then merge the older backup: local version should remain.
2. Make a backup, modify a document after the backup on another copy/device, then merge that newer backup: backup version should become active and the displaced local version should appear in Trash.
3. Add a document only to the backup-side Library: merge should add it locally without opening it.
4. Verify a replaced document that was already open remains open and editable after the merge.
5. Verify Restore from Trash can recover the displaced older local version.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.21 — SELECTED DOCUMENTS PANEL STYLING

- Selected Documents remains collapsible and uses the unified global document-selection state.
- Added the same bordered/rounded panel styling used by Open Documents, including the narrow-screen padding rule.
- No selection behavior or PDF Tools behavior changed.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.21 — UNIFIED SELECTED DOCUMENTS
=====================================================

- One canonical document-selection set now drives Open Documents, Local Library, Selected Documents, and the multi-document PDF Tools.
- Local Library checkboxes are no longer an export-only selection; they select documents for Export, Compress, and Combine just like Open Documents checkboxes.
- Added a Selected documents section that only mirrors the shared selection. It has no duplicate PDF Tool buttons. Unchecking there removes the document from the shared selection everywhere.
- A selected document remains selected when it is closed; the same stored Library record can then be used by PDF Tools without reopening it in the viewer.
- Export / Compress / Combine now accept closed Local Library documents. Stored source data is lazy-loaded for the operation.
- Open Documents Select all / Clear affect the open-document subset while preserving any closed Library documents already selected.
- PDF Tools order: Export, Compress, Combine, Extract, Split.
- Extract and Split now use parallel summary text: `Active: <name>` or `No active document`.
- Existing folder PDF export is unchanged.
- No changes to Pen/touch/pinch/viewer, page geometry, Assets/Templates, Copy Region, or annotation rendering.

TEST PRIORITIES FOR 5.7.21
--------------------------
- Synchronization of checkboxes among Local Library / Open Documents / Selected Documents.
- Export, Compress, Combine with mixtures of open and closed documents.
- Close a selected open document and verify it stays selected.
- Uncheck from Selected Documents and verify all corresponding check marks clear.
- Confirm Extract/Split remain active-document-only and show parallel active-document summaries.


## Milestone 5.7.21 — annotation anchoring during page geometry changes

- Fixed Pen strokes appearing to drift when Page Size or Crop/Margins changes page geometry.
- Root cause: those geometry helpers mutate editable stroke points in place, while the Google Ink modeled-path cache used object/array identity and could retain the pre-transform path.
- The shared shift/fit geometry helpers now invalidate modeled Pen geometry after changing points.
- Editable annotation data remains editable; image/highlighter behavior and page-geometry semantics are unchanged.
- Pen input, touch/pinch navigation, selection rotation, Asset placement, and Presentation round-trip behavior are otherwise unchanged.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.21 — TEMPLATE DROPDOWN CONSISTENCY

- Styling-only fix: Files → Templates → Automatic new last page now uses the same border/radius/background/padding/font/height treatment as the standard Files dropdowns.
- No behavior changes. 5.7.13 selection icons, Presentation Files round-trip behavior, paste placement, Copy Region, and Pen/touch/viewer behavior are unchanged.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.21 — LAYOUT-ONLY POLISH

- **Copy Region dialog:** Original size and Current zoom size now use two explicit equal columns; Cancel occupies a separate full-width row. The three actions no longer compete in one narrow flex row.
- **Presentation Select actions:** Delete / Duplicate / Copy / Region / Paste remain content-sized even under the tablet compact Presentation rule; the 34 px rule continues to apply only to ordinary icon-style toolbar buttons.
- This release changes layout/CSS only apart from version/About text. The validated 5.7.11 centered Paste behavior, Asset/Recent view-center capture, 5.7.10 Files return-state fix, 5.7.9 Copy Region capture logic, 5.7.8 naming keyboard path, and Pen/touch/pinch/viewer machinery are unchanged.

TEST PRIORITIES FOR 5.7.21

- Open Copy Region, draw a rectangle, and confirm Original size / Current zoom size are clearly separated and Cancel is visible below them without overlap.
- In Presentation → Select, confirm Delete / Duplicate / Copy / Region / Paste each contain their full labels and the right-side Presentation controls remain usable.
- Briefly confirm Paste and Assets/Recent placement still center in the visible view.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.11 — CENTERED PASTE / ASSET PLACEMENT

- **Paste semantics are now uniform:** an ordinary copied annotation snippet pastes at the center of the current visible page area, rather than preserving/offsetting the source location.
- **Assets/Recent insertion now freezes the visible-center target before entering Files.** The selected image or editable snippet pastes back at that exact page/view center after the Files round trip. This avoids depending on viewer-rebuild timing.
- Asset cards now use the single action label **Paste** for both images and editable snippets; the toolbar/help text also says Paste from Assets or Recent.
- **Duplicate is intentionally unchanged:** it remains an offset duplicate near the source selection.
- 5.7.10 page/scroll return-state protection remains in place. 5.7.9 Copy Region and 5.7.8 naming keyboard path remain intact.
- No Pen/Highlighter/touch/pinch/rendering algorithm changes in this milestone.

TEST PRIORITIES FOR 5.7.11

- On a later page and while scrolled/zoomed away from the page top, copy a selected annotation object and press Paste. It should appear centered in the currently visible portion of that page.
- From the same kind of view, open Assets/Recent from the annotation toolbar and Paste both (a) a Recent item and (b) a saved Asset. Both should return to the same page/view and place the object at the center of the pre-Assets visible area.
- Repeat once in Presentation and once in ordinary View. If convenient, repeat in split view on the active pane.
- Confirm all Asset cards say Paste, regardless of image vs editable snippet.
- Confirm Duplicate still creates an offset copy near the source rather than centering it.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.10 — PRESERVE POSITION WHEN RETURNING FROM FILES / ASSETS

- Fixes the regression observed after 5.7.9 testing: inserting a Recent image or a saved Asset could return the document to page 1.
- Root cause area: the generalized Files round-trip from the 5.7.7 cleanup relied on mutable live viewer state while the View DOM was hidden/rebuilt, and Presentation could be re-entered before renderViewer's normal two-frame scroll restoration had finished.
- The Files round-trip now captures an immutable copy of the current single/split view state, restores it before rebuilding View, and waits three animation frames before re-entering Presentation.
- Asset insertion also avoids a redundant second `showWorkspaceMode('export')` call after the shared round-trip has already entered Files.
- No Pen, touch, pinch, annotation-rendering, or viewer-navigation algorithm was changed.
- Copy Region from 5.7.9 is retained unchanged.

TEST PRIORITIES FOR 5.7.10
1. From a page well beyond page 1, Insert from Assets → insert a Recent image; confirm the same page/working position returns.
2. Repeat with a permanent saved image Asset.
3. Repeat both in Presentation mode.
4. Repeat at non-100% zoom and, if convenient, in Split view.
5. Confirm Copy Region still copies to Recent and pastes normally.

CURRENT MILESTONE 5.7.9 — COPY REGION

- Added **Copy Region** in **Select** mode as a new action button labeled **Region** beside Copy/Paste.
- Workflow: tap **Region** → drag a page rectangle → choose **Original size** or **Current zoom size** → Workbench stores the result in **Recent** and makes it the current paste image.
- The capture reuses the existing image-asset / image-annotation path. It does **not** create a new asset type. Paste, move/resize, export, backup, and Keep all continue to use the existing machinery.
- Captured content includes the underlying PDF/generated page plus visible Workbench image/ink/highlighter annotations. Transient selection UI, handles, and toolbar chrome are excluded.
- **Original size** pastes at the source rectangle's original page dimensions. **Current zoom size** converts the current viewer zoom into the pasted image's dimensions, so a zoomed-in capture pastes back enlarged on the page.
- Region copies go to **Recent**, not directly into the permanent Asset Library. Use **Keep** later if desired.
- Keyboard regression fix from 5.7.8 remains intact and unchanged.

- Both choices therefore have exactly the same keyboard/name-entry path; only `includeAnnotations` differs when the page is cloned into the saved template.
