RETURN HERE AFTER A BREAK — CURRENT STATUS
==========================================

AUTHORITATIVE WORKING BASELINE
------------------------------
- Use **Milestone 5.7.7** as the current source/build for the architecture-audit test.
- Visible About version: 5.7.7.
- Service-worker/cache version: `pdf-workbench-m5.7.7-v1`.
- IndexedDB remains version 4; Library schema remains version 8. This is a code/UI simplification release, not a persistence migration.
- Pen/Highlighter rendering, 5.6.9 pinch/scroll behavior, finger-scroll engagement, inertia, split/single viewer rendering, and annotation geometry are intentionally unchanged.

WHY 5.7.7 EXISTS
----------------
- 5.7.6 removed the separate Assets modal and made Assets parallel to Local Library in Files. During follow-up testing, the user confirmed that Template rename/save dialogs also lose keyboard focus on iPad; pen/Scribble input had hidden the problem earlier.
- The user explicitly set a design rule for Workbench: prefer simpler interaction models and code paths that already work, especially when the same task appears in more than one subsystem.
- A focused architecture audit found several places where Library/Assets/Templates or user-created documents had parallel implementations that could drift. 5.7.7 consolidates those without entering the stable input/viewer machinery.

MILESTONE 5.7.7 — ARCHITECTURE SIMPLIFICATION / CONSISTENCY AUDIT
==================================================================

TEMPLATES
---------
- Template management is no longer a modal Template Manager. It now renders directly inside **Files → Templates**, parallel to Local Library and Assets.
- **Insert Page → Manage templates…** switches to that exact same Files → Templates manager. When launched from the document, a **Back to document** bar returns to the prior View/Pages/Presentation context.
- There is now one small shared naming dialog for Local Library names, Asset names, and Template names.
- **Rename template** uses the ordinary shared Name field with no extra controls.
- **Save current page as template** uses that same shared dialog but conditionally shows the existing **Page content: With annotations / Clean page** choice. The option was preserved rather than creating a second naming dialog.
- Save Template temporarily performs text entry over the Files workspace and then returns to the prior document/Presentation context, so iPad software-keyboard viewport changes do not occur over the live viewer.
- The old dedicated `templateNameDialog` and modal Template Manager path are removed.

SHARED LIBRARY / ASSET FOLDER MODEL
-----------------------------------
- Local Library folders and Asset folders now share small primitives for sorted children, breadcrumb paths, descendants, sibling-name collision checks, breadcrumb rendering, and move-destination tree construction.
- This is deliberately a small set of helpers, not a generic folder framework.
- Library and Assets now use **one shared Move dialog**. Their actual persistence operations remain domain-specific where the data semantics genuinely differ.
- The old separate `libraryMoveDialog` and `assetMoveDialog` are removed.

USER-CREATED DOCUMENTS
----------------------
- Blank, Graph Paper, From Template, Images → PDF, and Combine now share one created-document activation path.
- That path applies the currently browsed Local Library folder, unique naming within that folder, page/document initialization, active selection, split-pane attachment when relevant, and the requested View/Pages destination.
- This removes the previous opportunity for one creation feature to forget the current-folder placement rule.
- PDF import/open behavior is intentionally unchanged; it already had its own correct destination semantics.

SMALL AUDIT CLEANUPS
--------------------
- Page Size now calls the same `invalidateCurrentDocumentGeometryScroll()` helper already used by Crop/Margins instead of carrying a second copy of that logic.
- Added `isFilesWorkspace()` in touched code to make the legacy internal workspace value `export` less misleading without doing a risky mass rename.
- Added `release-check.mjs`, a development-time consistency check that verifies APP_VERSION, resource query strings, the visible milestone label, service-worker cache name, and README release heading all agree. It is not part of the runtime/service-worker app shell.

TEST PRIORITY
-------------
1. **iPad keyboard focus:** Files → Templates → Rename; type a long name continuously with the onscreen keyboard.
2. **Save Template:** from View and, if convenient, Presentation, save once With annotations and once Clean page. Confirm continuous keyboard focus and return to the same document context.
3. **Insert Page → Manage templates:** confirm it opens Files → Templates (not a modal), Rename works there, and Back to document returns properly.
4. **Shared Move dialog:** move one Local Library folder/document and one Asset folder/asset, including a nested destination if convenient.
5. **Created-document destination:** browse into a Local Library folder and create Blank, Graph, From Template, Images → PDF, and/or Combine. Each should land in that folder; root should still be used when root is the current Library location.
6. **Presentation toolbar:** retain the 5.7.6 compact iPad-width behavior and confirm the Exit × remains fully onscreen in Pen mode.
7. Continue normal grading. Pen, Highlighter, Eraser, Select, pinch, finger scrolling, split view, and rendering should feel exactly like 5.7.6/5.6.9 because those paths were not intentionally changed.

ARCHITECTURE AUDIT RESULT
-------------------------
- No second subsystem as duplicated as the pre-5.7.6 Assets modal remains in the audited management UI.
- Remaining separate dialogs (page transfer, page geometry, crop/margins, close/delete, generic information/new-from-template chooser) represent genuinely different operations rather than duplicate naming/folder browsers.
- Single/split rendering and the annotation canvas stack still contain some maintainability duplication, but they are deliberately left alone while real-use behavior is stable. Refactor those only when a concrete bug or feature requires entering that code.

NEXT AGREED FEATURE WORK AFTER STABILIZATION
--------------------------------------------
1. Continue field-testing 5.7.7 until the simplified naming/management architecture is trustworthy.
2. Then implement **Copy Region → Image** as another Recent producer, supporting original-size and current-zoom-size behavior.
3. Then implement explicit/manual **Google Drive sync** for Workbench-managed documents/folders/templates/permanent Assets/Asset folders and required source binaries. Workbench remains local-first; Recent stays local-only by default.
4. Selection rotation remains on the explicit backlog.

RESUME RULES
------------
- Favor reuse of an existing proven Workbench interaction over creating a second platform-specific or subsystem-specific path.
- Do not retune or refactor Pen, touch, pinch, scrolling, or viewer rendering without a specific real-use regression or requirement.
- Keep Templates, Assets, and Local Library management structurally parallel where their operations are actually the same.
- Use later milestone text above older handoff history when statements conflict.

MILESTONE 5.7.6 — SIMPLER ASSETS WORKSPACE + TOOLBAR + NEW-DOCUMENT DESTINATION
===============================================================================

ASSETS — REMOVE THE EXTRA MODAL
-------------------------------
- Removed the large native `assetDialog` entirely.
- Assets now browse directly inside **Files → Assets**, structurally parallel to Local Library rather than opening another modal browser.
- The existing Asset Library/Recent tabs, nested Asset folders, breadcrumbs, Import images, New folder, Rename/Move/Delete, Keep, thumbnails, and insertion actions all live in that one Files-workspace browser.
- Asset New folder / Rename folder / Keep / Rename now call the **exact same `requestLibraryName()` / `libraryNameDialog` path** used by Local Library naming. There is no Asset-specific text-entry UI, no iPad-specific overlay, no focus-recapture guard, and no close/yield/reopen cycle.
- The annotation-strip **Insert Asset** button now switches to the same Files → Assets browser in insertion mode. A small insertion banner provides **Back to document**.
- Choosing an Asset returns to the document first and then inserts/pastes it, so the existing visible-view placement code sees the real viewer geometry. This preserves the 5.7.1 behavior that images/snippets land in the current visible working area rather than page center.
- If Insert Asset was invoked from Presentation, Back/Insert restores Presentation after returning to the document.
- Files → Assets remains usable as the ordinary management browser. When an open document exists, its Insert/Paste actions still return to View after insertion.

PRESENTATION TOOLBAR
--------------------
- Added a tablet-width compact Presentation rule for logical viewports up to 1100 CSS px (above the existing phone/narrow rule).
- In that range the existing controls are only modestly compressed: 34 px main buttons, 28 px color buttons, 30 px width/eraser buttons, slightly smaller gaps, 104 px document selector, and 42 px zoom label.
- Nothing is hidden or moved into another menu.
- Static Chromium layout check at a 1024 CSS-px viewport (matching the iPad screenshot's likely logical width): 5.7.5 required 1100 px of toolbar content and placed Exit at x=1094; 5.7.6 fits without horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth=clientWidth=1024`) and places Exit at x=983.

NEW WORKBENCH-CREATED DOCUMENT DESTINATION
------------------------------------------
- The currently browsed Local Library folder is now the destination for Workbench-created documents.
- Applied to **Blank**, **Graph Paper**, **From Template**, **Images → PDF**, and **Combine** output documents.
- Root is used only when Local Library is actually at root (or the remembered folder is no longer active).
- Existing PDF/image **import/open behavior is intentionally unchanged**; it was already placing imports correctly when invoked from Files/Library workflows.
- The low-level `createDocument()` default remains unchanged. A narrow helper assigns the current Library folder only in explicit user-created-document workflows, avoiding hidden side effects in import/restore/internal operations.

CHANGES
-------
- Removed the complete 5.7.4 dynamically-created `assetNameOverlay` path, including iPad-only branching, delayed focus reinforcement, blur-refocus guard, and overlay CSS.

- IndexedDB version remains 4; Library schema remains 8. No persistence migration in this interaction-only fix.
- 5.6.9 pinch/scroll fixes and 5.6.8 unified modeled Pen remain the performance/input baseline and are intentionally unchanged.

WHERE THE PROJECT IS RIGHT NOW
------------------------------
- The user is actively grading with Workbench. Core functionality and the 5.6.9 navigation polish are working well.
- Reusable Assets have persistent nested folders modeled after the Local Library; Quick Image goes directly to Recent; inserted images/snippets center in the visible working area.
- Real iPad testing showed the 5.7.3 Asset naming fix was insufficient: Rename Asset folder and Rename Asset still lost the text-field focus after each onscreen-keyboard keypress.
- 5.7.3 had already eliminated the obvious nested-native-dialog case by closing/yielding Assets before opening the shared naming dialog. The continuing symptom indicates iPad Safari can retain native-dialog focus restoration even after the first dialog is closed.
- 5.7.4 therefore removes the second native dialog from the iPad Asset naming path entirely. Assets closes, then iPad immediately shows a blocking non-native naming overlay while the initiating tap is still active; other platforms continue using the shared Local Library naming dialog.
- The iPad overlay also has a narrow focus guard: if Safari unexpectedly blurs the name input back to the page during keyboard typing, focus is immediately restored. Save/Cancel pointer actions disable the guard so they remain ordinary taps.

NEXT AGREED STEPS
-----------------
1. Test 5.7.4 on the actual iPad: continuously type several characters in Rename Asset folder and Rename Asset without retapping the field. Also test New folder and Keep from Recent.
2. Confirm Save/Cancel and return-to-Assets restore the same Asset manage/insert mode, Library/Recent tab, and nested folder.
3. Once stable, implement **Copy Region → Image** as another Recent producer, with both original-size and current-zoom-size capture/paste behavior. Current-zoom capture must intentionally preserve the user's desire to paste an enlarged image when the source was zoomed in.
4. Then implement explicit/manual **Google Drive sync** for Workbench-managed documents/folders/templates/permanent Assets/Asset folders and required source binaries. Workbench remains local-first; Recent stays local-only by default.
5. Selection rotation remains on the explicit backlog.

------------
- Do not retune Pen, pinch, finger-scroll engagement, or 0.95 momentum during Asset work without a specific real-use regression.

- Asset folders may be nested; do not simplify them back to one level.

- Local Library naming and desktop/Surface/Chromebook Asset naming should continue using the established shared naming dialog unless a separate regression appears.
- Use the latest milestone text above older history when statements conflict.

================ PRIOR HANDOFF HISTORY ================

RETURN HERE AFTER A BREAK — CURRENT STATUS
==========================================

AUTHORITATIVE WORKING BASELINE
------------------------------
- Use **Milestone 5.7.0** as the current source/build.
- Visible About version: 5.7.0.
- Service-worker/cache version: `pdf-workbench-m5.7.0-v1`.
- IndexedDB version: 3; Library schema version: 7. The upgrade only adds the `assets` object store / Asset records; existing documents remain compatible.
- 5.6.9 navigation fixes are the interaction baseline. During real grading use the user reported that 5.6.9 **made a big difference**; do not casually revert the in-place pinch finalization, early deliberate-scroll engagement, or 0.95 inertia.

WHERE THE PROJECT IS RIGHT NOW
------------------------------
- The user is actively grading/doing normal daily work in Workbench and considers the current core feature set close to adequate unless real testing exposes weaknesses.
- 5.7.0 begins the next agreed feature family: **Reusable Assets**. This deliberately unifies the planned image library and browsable clipboard rather than building separate storage systems.
- Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select rendering/input geometry is unchanged from 5.6.9. The Google-modeled Pen remains the stable baseline for all widths.

5.7.0 REUSABLE ASSETS + RECENT CLIPBOARD
-----------------------------------------
- New persistent IndexedDB `assets` store. Asset records have stable IDs and are either `image` or `snippet`.
- **Permanent Asset Library:** Files → Assets → Open Assets…; the annotation-strip picture button now opens the same Assets browser in insertion mode.
- **Image assets:** Import images… stores the original image source once, shows it as a reusable Asset, and can insert it on any page as the existing editable image-annotation type.
- **Editable snippet assets:** ordinary Lasso/Select Copy still fills the normal clipboard, but also adds a true-copy `snippet` to **Recent**. The snippet stores the selected Workbench objects, not a rasterization. Pen/Highlighter remain editable strokes; inserted images retain source references.
- **Recent clipboard history:** capped at 30 entries, newest first, stored locally so recent copies remain browsable across restarts. Normal Ctrl/Cmd+C and the Copy button feed Recent. Ctrl/Cmd+V / Paste still use the current clipboard.
- **Promote without flattening:** Recent → Keep asks for a name and promotes the same snippet record to the permanent Asset Library. No rasterization occurs for normal selection copies.
- Asset cards provide Paste/Insert, Keep or Rename, and Delete. Permanent deletion of an Asset never deletes a shared image binary if any document/template/other Asset still references it.
- Mixed snippets can include Workbench ink/highlighter and inserted images. Thumbnail rendering preserves image-below-ink layering.
- Assets participate in editable Library backup/restore. Backup-as-folder import remaps source IDs and imports saved Assets safely with new IDs/names.
- The existing whole-Library conventional-PDF ZIP remains document/template oriented; Assets are preserved by the editable backup, not converted into arbitrary PDF pages.

NEXT AGREED STEPS AFTER 5.7.0 TESTING
-------------------------------------
1. **Region → image capture** using this Asset system as the destination. Captures should enter Recent as raster image assets.
   - Support an original-size mode and a **current-zoom-size** mode, because the user often deliberately zooms a small source region, screen-captures/crops it, and pastes it back enlarged.
   - Prefer an offscreen page-coordinate render over literal screen pixels so the captured result can be clean while still honoring the chosen zoom-derived output/paste scale.
   - A region capture is intentionally raster; an ordinary copied selection remains a true editable snippet.
2. **Manual Google Drive synchronization** after Assets/capture are settled. Explicit `Sync with Drive`, local-first, no background service requirement. Workbench itself remains the file/asset manager; Drive transports Workbench state between machines. Synchronize documents, folders, templates, permanent Assets and required sources, preserving both copies on conflicts. Recent clipboard history should be local-only by default for Drive sync unless later use suggests otherwise.
3. Selection rotation remains a separate explicit backlog item.

CURRENT INPUT / PERFORMANCE BASELINE — DO NOT REGRESS
-----------------------------------------------------
- 5.6.9 post-pinch finalization keeps the live-scaled page visible while a crisp replacement renders offscreen, then swaps pixels in place. Do not return to a full viewer DOM rebuild at pinch-up.
- Annotation-mode finger navigation retains the stationary 120 ms palm-intent window but promotes clear deliberate movement early and replays pending movement. iPad can override the softer recent-Pencil guard with stronger deliberate motion; Surface/ChromeOS keep the strict pen-proximity guard.
- Continuous touch inertia decay is 0.95.
- Pen release remains O(current stroke) through the dedicated live-Pen layer; do not reintroduce whole-page repaint on every Pencil-up.
- All Pen widths use `google-ink-v1`; raw timed Pencil points remain authoritative for editing while modeled positions are rendering geometry.
- Removed smoothing experiments (5.6.2 cardinal stabilization, 5.6.3 filled outline, 5.6.4/5.6.5 custom trajectory fitter) remain dead and should not be revived without specific evidence.

OTHER SETTLED ROADMAP / BEHAVIOR
--------------------------------
- Workbench is intentionally PDF/teaching-workstation centered rather than a mandate to clone every Goodnotes/Notability feature.
- Backlog possibilities include straight-line/shapes, Annotated-pages filtering, Library search, selection recoloring/rotation, image crop/rotation/system-clipboard paste/layer order, adjustable Pen width/full color picker, and other conveniences.
- Black generated pages, templates, automatic end-page append, split viewing, Images → PDF, page geometry/crop/margins, PDF split/combine/extract/compress, Local Library folders/backups/trash, session restoration, and Presentation behavior are established features.
- Rebuilt-PDF link policy: preserve standard external URI links; deliberately strip internal/document-navigation links; outlines/bookmarks are not rebuilt. Untouched pass-through preserves source bytes exactly.
- Attributions/licenses remain under ⋯ → Attributions & licenses; PDF.js/pdf-lib/JSZip are still externally pinned/cached rather than fully vendored.

RESUME PROCEDURE AFTER AN EXTENDED BREAK
----------------------------------------
1. Start from **5.7.0** and read this block first.
2. Ask for the user's real-use results with Assets/Recent before expanding the subsystem.
3. If 5.7.0 is sound, implement region-to-image capture into Recent (including current-zoom enlargement behavior), then manual Google Drive sync unless the user reprioritizes.
4. Preserve the validated 5.6.9 pinch/scroll behavior and unified Pen architecture while doing Asset work.
5. Treat older milestone sections below as history/context; later sections supersede older status statements when they conflict.

MILESTONE 5.6.9 — TOUCH NAVIGATION / PINCH POLISH
=================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.9
Visible About version: 5.6.9
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.9-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
During real grading use of 5.6.8, the user reported that most functionality was working well but three navigation details were distracting: a flash/blip after pinch zoom, occasional finger-scroll engagement delay, and insufficient release momentum. Inspection found concrete causes for the first two rather than treating them as vague performance complaints.

IMPLEMENTATION
--------------
- Replace the post-pinch full viewer rebuild with in-place crisp raster refresh using temporary canvases; keep the live-scaled page visible until the replacement is ready.
- Keep pinch anchoring and final zoom values unchanged.
- Add stale-refresh protection so another pinch/view change cannot commit an obsolete raster.
- Preserve the 120 ms palm-intent window for stationary/ambiguous touches, but promote clear deliberate motion early and replay pending movement.
- On iPad, permit stronger deliberate finger motion to override the soft recent/hover Pencil guard; preserve stricter Surface/ChromeOS guarded behavior.
- Increase continuous-scroll inertia decay from 0.94 to 0.95.
- Add touch-intent timing/guard telemetry.
- No Pen/Highlighter/eraser/selection geometry changes.

TEST PRIORITY
-------------
1. iPad grading use: pinch repeatedly at different zoom levels and confirm there is no blank/Rendering flash at release.
2. With Pen, Highlighter, Eraser, and Select left active, begin ordinary one-finger scrolls and check that the page engages promptly. Try this both after the Pencil has been away and immediately after writing/while the Pencil is still nearby.
3. Judge whether 0.95 momentum is a modest improvement rather than excessive coast.
4. When convenient, regression-test Surface and Chromebook palm rejection because the touch-intent code changed; their strict pen-proximity guard is intentionally retained.
5. Confirm split-view pinch finalization also remains anchored and flash-free.

================ PRIOR HANDOFF HISTORY ================

MILESTONE 5.6.8 — UNIFIED MODELED PEN RENDERER

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.8
Visible About version: 5.6.8
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.8-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
The 5.5 pt Thick Pen already looked acceptable, but keeping it on a separate cardinal-spline renderer meant Pen behavior still had two live/render/commit/export pipelines. The user explicitly preferred one algorithm for all widths, both to simplify the present code and to make future additional or continuously adjustable widths easier.

5.6.8 UNIFICATION
-----------------
- ALL Pen widths now use `google-ink-v1` modeled input. Thin, Medium, and Thick differ only by their numeric `width` value.
- REMOVED the remaining Thick-only cardinal renderer: `THICK_PEN_CURVE_FACTOR`, `THICK_PEN_HANDLE_CAP`, `thickPenStrokeControls`, `traceThickPenCanvas`, the raw/incremental Thick live helpers, and the Thick PDF-export Bezier branch.
- REMOVED renderer-selection-by-width. New Pen input always creates a Google modeler and stores relative input timestamp `t` on raw Pen points.
- Screen redraw and PDF export now route every Pen annotation through `buildGoogleInkRender`; Highlighter remains the separate raw/translucent path.
- Pen-up still commits only the just-finished modeled stroke to the persistent overlay, preserving the 5.6.1 O(current-stroke) dense-page performance rule.
- Diagnostics now report `penRenderer: "google-ink-v1"` for every Pen width; the retired `thick-cardinal` identity no longer exists.
- Raw Pencil points remain authoritative for Eraser, Lasso/Select, hit testing, transforms, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup, and page operations.
- There is no compatibility target for experimental pre-5.6.6 renderer appearance; those test documents can be deleted.

WHY THIS HELPS FUTURE WIDTHS
----------------------------
Width no longer selects an algorithm. Adding another fixed width, changing the current three widths, or later replacing the three choices with a slider can operate on the same modeled trajectory/rendering code and only change the numeric line width. Width-specific model tuning should be introduced only if real testing demonstrates a need.

ATTRIBUTIONS / LICENSES
-----------------------
The 5.6.7 legal/UI work remains intact:
- **⋯ → Attributions & licenses**;
- `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`;
- `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt`;
- `APACHE-2.0-Google-Ink-Stroke-Modeler.txt`.
The notice now describes Google-style positional modeling/prediction as applying to all Pen widths. PDF.js 6.2.108, pdf-lib 1.17.1, JSZip 3.10.1, and Google Ink Stroke Modeler remain the listed third-party projects/licenses.

DEPLOYMENT NOTE
---------------
External PDF.js/pdf-lib/JSZip bundling remains POSTPONED. 5.6.8 keeps the same pinned external versions and service-worker caching behavior as 5.6.7. True vendoring can resume after the unified Pen path has had a short real-use regression test.

Write normally with Thin, Medium, and Thick. Thick is the only behavior newly changed in this revision. MILESTONE 5.6.6 — GOOGLE-STYLE MODELED-INPUT THIN/MEDIUM PEN EXPERIMENT

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.6
Visible About version: 5.6.6
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.6-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Real iPad testing of 5.6.5 showed visible improvement and a more responsive feel, but the custom distance/corner/Bezier pipeline still did not approach GoodNotes closely enough. The full 5.6.5 diagnostic set also showed that the heuristic preprocessor was not doing what its synthetic tests suggested on real handwriting: across 122 completed Thin/Medium strokes the median stroke had 46 raw points, 47 retained/resampled points, 9.5 detected corners, and 10.5 fitted cubics. A representative 107-point stroke retained 105 points and reported 25 corners. Ordinary curvature/noise was therefore being classified as a succession of explicit corners, creating an increasingly hand-tuned heuristic problem.

Open-source research found Google's Ink Stroke Modeler, which was designed specifically for handwriting/stylus smoothing and treats raw positions as noisy estimates of an idealized physical pen. The published/default approach combines velocity-aware wobble filtering, spring/mass/drag position modeling, minimum-rate resampling, live prediction to hide model lag, and end-of-stroke catch-up. Rnote uses a Rust reimplementation of this family of modeling ideas. 5.6.6 tests that modeling approach instead of adding more custom point/corner heuristics.

5.6.6 EXPERIMENT
----------------
- NEW Thin and Medium Pen strokes are tagged `renderer: "google-ink-v1"` and use the modeled-input path.
- Existing Thin/Medium annotations created before 5.6.6 are NOT retagged and continue to render/export through the 5.6.5 trajectory fitter. This prevents a new experiment from changing previously written documents merely by opening them.
- Thick Pen remains on the established cardinal-spline renderer and is still the in-app control.
- Raw Pencil points remain the authoritative editable geometry for Eraser, Lasso/Select, hit testing, transforms, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup, and page operations.
- New modeled strokes additionally store a RELATIVE INPUT TIMESTAMP `t` with each raw point. This makes redraw/export after reload deterministic from the raw input stream rather than requiring derived modeled points to be persisted.
- Undo-history packing stores x/y/t triples only for modeled/timed strokes; ordinary Pen/Highlighter snapshots keep the existing compact x/y-pair packing so this experiment does not increase history memory for all legacy ink. Old in-memory x/y-pair snapshots remain readable during the same runtime.
- Move/resize, copy/paste, duplication, and partial-stroke erasing preserve/interpolate `t` so edited modeled strokes can be reconstructed later.
- Renderer identity is sticky after creation: resizing a modeled stroke changes its width/geometry but does not silently switch it to the legacy renderer.

MODELER / RENDERING
-------------------
The local `google-ink-modeler.js` module is an independent JavaScript implementation based on the mathematical algorithms and tuning guidance published by Google's Apache-2.0 Ink Stroke Modeler project. It does not vendor the full Google C++ library or the third-party TypeScript port.

5.6.6 models directly in native PDF points and seconds. Google's model is explicitly unit-agnostic and says its wobble parameters should be tuned to the client's input rate/speed. The latest 5.6.5 classroom/iPad sample gives us an unusually good calibration set: the 122 new blue strokes have a median stored-input rate of about 409 points/s and an approximate median rendered path speed of 287 PDF pt/s. Following Google's starting guidance (wobble timeout about 2.5 / expected input rate; speed floor/ceiling about 2% / 3% of expected speed), the first Workbench-specific parameters are:
- wobble timeout: 0.006 s;
- wobble speed floor / ceiling: 6 / 9 PDF pt/s;
- spring mass constant: 11 / 32400 (upstream starting value);
- drag constant: 72 (upstream starting value);
- minimum modeled output rate: 180 Hz;
- end-of-stroke stopping distance: 0.001 PDF pt;
- end-of-stroke max iterations: 20;
- live predictor: stroke-end prediction.

These are deliberately starting values, not presumed final tuning. The core 5.6.6 experiment implements the pieces relevant to constant-width positional ink: wobble smoothing, the spring/mass/drag position model, minimum-rate resampling, stroke-end catch-up, and StrokeEnd prediction. It does NOT yet implement pressure/tilt stylus-state modeling, the optional loop-contraction mitigation, or Google's optional Kalman predictor.

The rendered Thin/Medium stroke is deliberately SIMPLE for this experiment:
- constant width;
- ordinary round caps and joins;
- no pressure/tapering;
- no filled-outline construction;
- no supersampling;
- no extra Bezier fitter after the modeler.
The modeled 180 Hz positions are connected directly. This isolates whether the modeled trajectory itself improves handwriting.

LIVE INK / LATENCY
------------------
- Preserve the 5.6.1 O(current stroke) release architecture: no whole-page annotation redraw on each Pen lift.
- Coalesced accepted input samples are fed incrementally into one live modeler instance.
- Stable modeled output is accumulated. On each pointer event, Workbench requests a temporary stroke-end prediction and redraws only the current live stroke (stable modeled output + replaceable prediction) on the isolated live-Pen canvas.
- When the next real input arrives, the old prediction is discarded; previously produced stable model output is never recomputed.
- Pencil-up feeds one final Up sample, runs the model's end-of-stroke catch-up, and commits only that completed modeled stroke to the persistent annotation overlay.
- The live canvas still clears only its previous dirty footprint at high zoom.

DIAGNOSTICS
-----------
For new `google-ink-v1` strokes, completed diagnostics add:
- `penRenderer: "google-ink-v1"`;
- `googleLiveModelMs`;
- `googlePredictionMs`;
- `googleStablePoints`;
- `googlePredictionPointsLast`;
- `googleWobbleSpeedLast`;
- `googleWobbleBlendLast`;
- `googleLagPointsLast`;
- `googleOutputPoints`;
- `googleEndLagPoints`;
plus the existing live render, commit, page-annotation-count, and history-depth fields. Legacy trajectory-fit diagnostic fields remain for old strokes.

PRE-FLIGHT CHECKS
-----------------
- `app.js` and `google-ink-modeler.js` pass JavaScript syntax checks.
- Offline reconstruction and incremental live processing were compared on identical timed input and produced identical stable modeled samples.
- A randomized 1,000-stroke model stress test produced no NaN/invalid modeled points. Typical ~99-input-point strokes produced ~155 modeled samples at the 180 Hz target rate; model computation was very small in the Node test (about 0.06 ms/stroke average, max ~2.2 ms in that run). Browser/iPad Canvas behavior remains the authoritative performance test.
- Live stroke-end prediction was stress-tested independently and remained finite.
- The end-of-stroke state/lag metric was verified against the final modeled tip (including early stop exits); a 1,000-stroke randomized stress run stayed finite with maximum residual end lag about 0.15 PDF pt in that synthetic set.

TEST REQUEST
------------
Use normal small mathematical handwriting in Thin and Medium and compare it to the blue 5.6.5 sample and GoodNotes. Pay attention to:
1. long-edge smoothness rather than just centerline shape;
2. whether small loops/corners (x, z, k, superscripts, radicals, fractions) retain intent;
3. whether the live stroke appears to trail the Pencil or prediction keeps it visually caught up;
4. whether the final stroke visibly snaps/settles at Pencil-up;
5. whether stroke endings appear shortened (watch `googleEndLagPoints` if so);
6. any latency as the page grows dense.
Export diagnostics after a representative test.

SOURCE / ATTRIBUTION NOTE
-------------------------
Google Ink Stroke Modeler: https://github.com/google/ink-stroke-modeler (Apache License 2.0; Copyright 2022 Google LLC). The Workbench module is an independent implementation from the published mathematical description/defaults. The WhiteboardCX TypeScript port was used as a behavioral/reference cross-check during research but is not bundled as a dependency.

DEPLOYMENT NOTE
---------------
External PDF.js/pdf-lib/JSZip bundling remains POSTPONED while Pen rendering is being tuned. 5.6.6 keeps the same CDN versions and service-worker caching behavior as 5.6.5. The new `google-ink-modeler.js` is local Workbench code and is precached in the app shell.

================ PRIOR HANDOFF HISTORY ================

MILESTONE 5.6.5 — DISTANCE-FILTERED THIN/MEDIUM TRAJECTORY FITTING

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.5
Visible About version: 5.6.5
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.5-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Real iPad testing of 5.6.4 showed two useful things: the new live-fitting architecture FELT more responsive, and the orange trajectory-fit handwriting was somewhat smoother, but still not close enough to GoodNotes. Diagnostics showed ordinary strokes still feeding 39–107 raw points into the fitter and often producing 6–14 Bezier curves. The user proposed averaging/filtering consecutive points and, especially, retaining only points that are meaningful by distance or as corners. This revision tests that idea directly.

5.6.5 EXPERIMENT
----------------
Thin and Medium rendering/export now use this pipeline:
RAW PENCIL POINTS -> DISTANCE RESAMPLING -> EXPLICIT CORNER RETENTION -> MILD AVERAGING -> BEZIER FIT.

- Raw stored annotation points are NOT changed. They remain authoritative for Eraser, Select/Lasso, hit testing, transforms, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup, and page operations.
- Thin resample spacing: 0.85 PDF point. Medium: 1.05 PDF point. This makes trajectory representation depend on geometric distance rather than Pencil sample frequency.
- Endpoints are always retained.
- Corner detection is distance-based rather than adjacent-sample based. It compares turn angle over a short window with the turn over a broader window. A true cusp retains most of its turn at both scales; a smooth U/arc spreads its turn over distance. This is intended to preserve x/k/z-style corners without making rounded handwriting angular.
- Nearby corner candidates are merged so one physical corner does not create several retained anchors.
- Regular distance anchors too close to a retained corner are omitted in favor of the corner itself.
- Between retained corners, apply only a mild 3-point averaging pass (strength 0.38 Thin / 0.30 Medium). This REPLACES the very strong 5.6.2/5.6.4 five-point stabilization in the Thin/Medium fit path.
- Fit tolerances are 0.72 pt Thin / 0.95 pt Medium. Because sub-point sampling chatter has already been filtered, the fitter can be looser without relying on low tolerance to preserve corners.
- Each explicitly retained corner starts a new fitted span with independent incoming/outgoing tangents. The recursive fitter retains its sharp-turn fallback as a safety net.
- 5.5 pt Thick Pen remains the unchanged control.
- No pressure/tapering, velocity width, filled outlines, or supersampling in 5.6.5.

LIVE INK / LATENCY
------------------
- Preserve the 5.6.1 O(current stroke) commit architecture and 5.6.4 once-per-pointer-event fit/redraw.
- The live layer remains isolated from persistent page ink and clears only its previous dirty footprint at high zoom.
- The new preprocessing reduces the point set before recursive fitting. Synthetic repeated-growing-stroke benchmarking was faster than the 5.6.4 fitting block in Node (about 0.24 ms vs 0.38 ms per fit in that test), despite corner detection and resampling. This is not a substitute for iPad testing.

DIAGNOSTICS
-----------
Completed Thin/Medium Pen diagnostics now include:
- raw input: `penFitInputPoints`;
- reduced trajectory: `penFitRetainedPoints`;
- explicit corners: `penFitCornerCount`;
- resample spacing: `penFitSpacing`;
- fitted cubic count: `penFitCurves`;
- fit/commit timing: `penFitMs`, `penCommitMs`;
- corresponding latest-live values: `liveFitInputPointsLast`, `liveFitRetainedPointsLast`, `liveFitCornerCountLast`, `liveFitSpacingLast`, `liveFitCurvesLast`, plus accumulated live fit/render timing.

PRE-FLIGHT CHECKS
-----------------
- JavaScript syntax passes.
- Synthetic noisy straight line: no false corners; approximately 2 fitted cubics.
- Synthetic smooth U: no false corner; one fitted cubic in the test geometry.
- Synthetic noisy Z: both deliberate corners retained; three fitted spans.
- Distance reduction materially lowers points reaching the fitter.

TEST REQUEST
------------
Use ordinary small mathematical handwriting, especially U/u, y, k, z, x, parentheses, superscripts, fraction bars, and short equals/minus strokes. Compare smooth long contours, preservation of intentional corners, whether the live stroke visibly settles at Pencil-up, and responsiveness. Export diagnostics after a representative sample so raw -> retained -> curves can be inspected.

DEPLOYMENT NOTE
---------------
External-library bundling remains POSTPONED while Pen rendering is being tuned. 5.6.5 keeps the same external dependency/caching setup.

================ PRIOR HANDOFF HISTORY ================

MILESTONE 5.6.4 — EXPERIMENTAL THIN/MEDIUM TRAJECTORY FITTING

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.4
Visible About version: 5.6.4
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.4-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Real iPad comparison of 5.6.3 showed that constant-width filled outlines were not markedly smoother than the 5.6.2 centerline renderer and occasionally produced tiny white holes/pinholes where a closed offset contour folded across itself. The exported 5.6.3 PDF showed the same holes, so this was geometry rather than merely screenshot antialiasing. The user judged the added outline complexity not worthwhile and suspected a more fundamental trajectory issue. The next experiment therefore attacks the sampled centerline rather than stroke-edge construction.

5.6.4 EXPERIMENT
----------------
- REMOVE the 5.6.3 filled-outline renderer from the active Thin/Medium Pen path. Thin and Medium are again ordinary constant-width, round-capped/round-joined CENTERLINE strokes on screen and in PDF export.
- Preserve the 5.6.2 strong anchor-stabilization prepass for this experiment, but STOP forcing the rendered path through nearly every stabilized Pencil sample.
- Thin and Medium now use recursive cubic Bezier trajectory fitting. All stabilized samples contribute to the fit, but a curve is accepted when it stays within a width-aware error tolerance rather than creating one cubic segment per sample interval.
- Initial tolerances: Thin 0.62 PDF point; Medium 0.82 PDF point. These are experimental and intentionally easy to tune.
- At recursive split points, strongly localized sharp turns can use independent incoming/outgoing tangents so deliberate corners are not forced into C1-smooth rounding. Ordinary smooth curvature remains smooth.
- 5.5 pt Thick Pen remains EXACTLY on the prior cardinal-spline renderer and serves as the unchanged in-app control.
- Raw sampled points remain authoritative and unchanged for Eraser, Select/Lasso, hit testing, transforms, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup, and page operations.
- No pressure/tapering, velocity width, filled outlines, or supersampling are introduced in 5.6.4.

LIVE INK / LATENCY ARCHITECTURE
--------------------------------
- Preserve the 5.6.1 O(current stroke) Pen-up rule. No dense-page full-overlay repaint is reintroduced.
- Thin/Medium coalesced Pencil samples are appended as one event batch, then the isolated live stroke is fit and redrawn ONCE PER POINTER EVENT, not once per raw/coalesced sample. This also permits one cached event geometry/layout read for the whole batch.
- The live layer contains only the current Pen stroke, so clearing/refitting it never touches persistent page ink.
- Pencil-up builds the final fitted centerline once and reuses it for all visible instances of the page, including same-document split view.
- Thick retains the prior incremental live-segment renderer as the control.
- High-zoom safety: the fitted live preview clears only its previous dirty bounding rectangle before redrawing, not the entire page-sized live canvas on every pointer event. A full live-layer clear still occurs once at final commit.

PRE-FLIGHT VALIDATION
---------------------
- JavaScript syntax check passes.
- Synthetic 100–140-sample noisy line/S/V trajectories fit to about 3–4 cubic segments with the initial tolerances instead of roughly one cubic per sample interval.
- A randomized 1,000-stroke geometry stress test produced no NaN/invalid control points; typical fitted curve counts remained far below raw point counts.
- Isolated Node geometry timing is sub-millisecond for ordinary 100–140-point strokes, but real iPad Canvas/live latency remains the authoritative test.

DIAGNOSTICS
-----------
Completed Pen diagnostics now report:
- `liveRenderMs` and `liveRenderCalls`;
- `liveFitMs`;
- `liveFitCurvesLast` and `liveFitInputPointsLast`;
- `penRenderer` (`trajectory-fit` or `centerline-stroke`);
- `penFitMs`, `penFitCurves`, and `penFitInputPoints`;
- existing `penCommitMs`, `penCommitStages`, page annotation count, and history depth.
The 5.6.3 outline-specific diagnostic fields are retired.

REAL-WORLD TEST REQUEST
-----------------------
Compare ordinary small mathematical handwriting in Thin and Medium with 5.6.2/5.6.3 and GoodNotes. Look especially for (1) smoother long edges without micro-wobble, (2) faithful x/k/z/fraction-bar corners, (3) whether a live stroke visibly changes at Pencil-up, and (4) any live latency. The key diagnostic comparison is raw input-point count versus fitted cubic count. A successful fit should normally use substantially fewer cubics than input points.

5.6.3 OUTLINE EXPERIMENT RESULT
-------------------------------
The 5.6.3 filled-outline approach should be considered REJECTED for now: little visible gain, substantially greater path complexity, and occasional white pinholes/self-intersection artifacts. Do not revive it unless later evidence gives a specific reason.

DEPLOYMENT NOTE
---------------
External-library bundling remains POSTPONED while Pen rendering is being tuned. 5.6.4 keeps the same external library versions/caching behavior as 5.6.3.

MILESTONE 5.6.3 — EXPERIMENTAL FILLED-OUTLINE THIN/MEDIUM PEN

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.3
Visible About version: 5.6.3
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.3-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Direct comparison of the same handwriting in Workbench 5.6.2 and GoodNotes showed that the aggressive 5.6.2 centerline smoothing materially improved Workbench, but GoodNotes still had visibly smoother stroke EDGES even apart from its tapering/width variation. Inspection of the extracted GoodNotes PDF also showed its ink represented as filled closed vector outlines rather than simple constant-width stroked centerlines. The user asked to isolate that edge-rendering difference before experimenting with pressure/tapering or higher-resolution canvases.

5.6.3 EXPERIMENT
----------------
- Keep ALL 5.6.2 thin/medium centerline smoothing and completed-stroke anchor stabilization values unchanged. This revision does not turn the smoothing knob further.
- 1.5 pt Thin and 3 pt Medium Pen now render as CONSTANT-WIDTH CLOSED FILLED OUTLINES. The left and right offset contours are smoothed independently around the existing smoothed centerline.
- No pressure-based width variation, no velocity-based width variation, no tapering, and no annotation-canvas supersampling are introduced. This intentionally isolates edge construction from those other possible GoodNotes differences.
- 5.5 pt Thick Pen is unchanged and continues to use the existing centerline-stroked renderer as an in-app control.
- Raw sampled centerline points remain the authoritative editable data. Eraser, Select/Lasso, hit testing, transforms, persistence, Undo/Redo, backup, and page operations are unchanged.
- PDF export mirrors screen geometry: Thin/Medium are emitted as filled closed vector paths; Thick remains a stroked centerline.

LATENCY / DENSE-PAGE ARCHITECTURE
----------------------------------
- Preserve the 5.6.1 rule that Pen release is O(current stroke), never O(all page ink).
- Thin/Medium live rendering constructs only the newly finalized cubic-segment outline on each incremental live update; it does NOT redraw the full in-progress stroke or persistent page ink.
- On Pencil-up the final Thin/Medium outline is built once and reused across every visible instance of the page, including same-document split view.
- Canvas internal resolution is unchanged in 5.6.3. Supersampling is explicitly deferred until the filled-outline experiment shows whether it is needed.

DIAGNOSTICS ADDED
-----------------
For completed Pen strokes, `handler-finish-accepted` now additionally records:
- `liveRenderMs` and `liveRenderCalls`;
- `liveOutlineBuildMs`;
- `penRenderer` (`filled-outline` or `centerline-stroke`);
- `penOutlineBuildMs`;
- `penOutlineCommands`;
- existing `penCommitMs` / `penCommitStages`.
These fields are intended to determine whether smoother filled edges materially affect Apple Pencil latency in real use.

TEST REQUEST
------------
Compare ordinary small mathematical handwriting in Thin and Medium against both 5.6.2 and GoodNotes. Judge (1) visible edge smoothness, (2) whether corners/letterforms remain faithful, (3) whether the live stroke feels delayed, and (4) whether Pencil-up causes any visible settling. Thick remains the unchanged control. If latency or geometry is unacceptable, back off/revert the outline experiment before considering supersampling or pressure/tapering.

---------------

MILESTONE 5.6.2 — EXPERIMENTAL STRONG THIN/MEDIUM PEN SMOOTHING

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.2
Visible About version: 5.6.2
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.2-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
During side-by-side real handwriting use, the user reported that Goodnotes inking still looks noticeably smoother than PDF Workbench. The user explicitly requested a MAJOR thin/medium Pen tuning experiment with the expectation that the final settings may later be backed off. This revision is therefore intentionally more aggressive than the conservative 5.5.x smoothing work.

5.6.2 EXPERIMENT
----------------
- 1.5 pt Thin Pen curve factor / handle cap changes from 0.205 / 0.72 to 0.27 / 0.90.
- 1.5 pt completed-stroke anchor stabilization changes from 0.58 to 0.86.
- 3 pt Medium Pen curve factor / handle cap changes from 0.155 / 0.62 to 0.225 / 0.82.
- 3 pt completed-stroke anchor stabilization changes from 0.36 to 0.68.
- 5.5 pt Thick Pen remains EXACTLY at 0.135 / 0.58 with zero anchor stabilization. Use it as an in-app control when comparing the experiment.
- Because the thin/medium stabilization is much stronger, deliberate-turn protection now begins around a 35-degree change in direction instead of roughly 45 degrees and can taper to a slightly lower minimum contribution. The goal is to suppress shallow edge wobble while still allowing mathematical symbols and handwriting corners to stay recognizable.
- Raw sampled points remain authoritative and unchanged. Eraser, Select/Lasso, persistence, session-only Undo/Redo, hit testing, and object transforms still operate on the same raw geometry.
- Live Pen drawing uses the stronger width-aware spline settings. Completed screen rendering and PDF export additionally use the stronger anchor stabilization, so a stroke may settle slightly smoother when the Pencil lifts.
- This is explicitly NOT presumed final tuning. After real handwriting comparison, expect to keep, reduce, or partly revert the thin and medium changes independently.

UNCHANGED / REGRESSION-SENSITIVE
--------------------------------
- Milestone 5.6.1 dedicated reusable live-Pen layer and O(current-stroke) Pen-up commit remain intact. Do not reintroduce dense-page full-overlay repaint on each Pen release.
- Highlighter behavior is unchanged.
- Thick Pen is unchanged and serves as a control.
- 5.6.0 White/Black blank pages remain unchanged.
- 5.5.9 rebuilt-PDF link policy remains unchanged.
- External-library bundling remains POSTPONED while Pen behavior is being field-tested.

TEST REQUEST
------------
Compare 1.5 pt and 3 pt handwriting directly with Goodnotes using ordinary lecture/scratch-work writing, especially small letters, fractions, parentheses, long algebraic strokes, and tight corners. Pay attention to both (a) whether shallow wobble/rough edges are visibly improved and (b) whether the smoothing begins to distort intentional corners or makes the stroke feel as if it changes too much on Pencil-up. The 5.5 pt Pen provides an unchanged Workbench reference.

MILESTONE 5.6.1 — DENSE-PAGE PEN RELEASE OPTIMIZATION

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.1
Visible About version: 5.6.1
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.1-v1
Library schema version: unchanged

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
A real scratch-work session in 5.5.8 exposed progressively sluggish/non-responsive Pen behavior on page 2 after ordinary mathematical handwriting/highlighting had accumulated. The exported page was not pathologically dense for the intended classroom use. Diagnostics showed healthy Apple Pencil pointer delivery (no characteristic dropped-contact/page-stage failure signatures) but increasing synchronous Pen-up cost. The root cause was the opaque Pen finalization path: live Pen geometry was drawn directly onto the persistent annotation canvas, so every Pencil-up had to clear provisional tail geometry by repainting every annotation on the page.

5.6.1 FIX
---------
- Pen now has a dedicated reusable `live-pen-canvas`, parallel in principle to the already-optimized live Highlighter layer.
- During contact, all immediate/raw and finalized smoothed Pen progress is drawn only onto this temporary live layer. The persistent annotation canvas remains frozen with previously completed annotations.
- On Pencil-up/cancel, Workbench draws only the just-finished Pen stroke's exact completed/stabilized geometry onto the persistent annotation canvas, then clears the live Pen canvas for reuse.
- Pen release therefore changes from roughly O(all existing page annotations) to O(the just-finished stroke). A page with hundreds of prior strokes should not become progressively more expensive merely because another short stroke is completed.
- Same-page split instances receive the live Pen stroke and one-stroke commit independently, preserving the established shared-document/independent-view behavior.
- If an unrelated asynchronous exact annotation repaint occurs during an active Pen or Highlighter gesture, that active stroke is excluded from the persistent repaint so it cannot be accidentally committed twice.
- Existing Highlighter live-layer behavior is retained. Eraser deferred exact redraw, selection optimization, Pen smoothing/stabilization, raw editable points, export geometry, and annotation ordering are otherwise unchanged.

DIAGNOSTICS ADDED
-----------------
`handler-begin-accepted` and `handler-finish-accepted` now identify the live layer and include the current page annotation count and history depth. Pen completion additionally reports `liveCommitMs`, `penCommitMs`, and `penCommitStages`. These are intended to verify during classroom use that Pen release remains nearly independent of page density.

PRE-RELEASE VALIDATION
----------------------
- Browser smoke testing created a black generated page, drew Pen ink, verified the reusable live Pen canvas is empty after release, and confirmed annotation Undo -> Redo restores the exact same annotation-canvas pixels. Highlighter still removes its temporary live layer normally.
- A synthetic dense-page comparison used 220 opaque Pen strokes with about 30 sampled points per stroke. On the same headless Chromium run, 5.6.0 average synchronous pointer-up processing rose from about 0.87 ms early to 2.62 ms late; 5.6.1 measured about 0.30 ms early and 0.29 ms late. This is not an iPad performance guarantee, but it confirms that the old page-density growth is removed from the Pen-release drawing path.
- An identical 30-stroke synthetic drawing produced byte-for-byte identical PNG data from the completed persistent annotation canvas in 5.6.0 and 5.6.1. Completed Pen appearance therefore remains unchanged by the live-layer architecture in this test.

FIELD-TEST / DISTRIBUTION DECISION
----------------------------------
- External-library/CDN bundling was considered for the classroom test but is POSTPONED at the user's request until this newly discovered Pen performance defect is addressed and field-tested. Do not treat 5.6.1 as the bundled/self-contained build.
- Continue using the existing pinned external library versions/caching behavior from 5.6.0 for this revision.
- The user plans a multi-day real-world classroom test after the Pen fix is validated.

UNCHANGED
---------
- 5.6.0 White/Black blank-page creation/insertion.
- 5.5.9 rebuilt-PDF link policy: preserve external URI links, strip internal/document-navigation links.
- 50-step session-only Undo stack; no memory cap has been added preemptively.
- Apple Pencil/Surface/Chromebook input architecture, partial Eraser, Select/Lasso, Highlighter, images, persistence/export, 0.94 touch-scroll inertia, Presentation ratio, graph paper, and end-of-document behavior.

CURRENT ROADMAP NOTE
--------------------
The roadmap list below remains authoritative. Its first item (performance/stability checkpoint) is now actively being exercised by this 5.6.1 Pen fix. After real-world validation, return to the remaining possibilities rather than treating external-library bundling as feature work. Bundling remains a deployment hardening task to revisit before/after the classroom field test as appropriate.

MILESTONE 5.6.0 — BLACK BLANK-PAGE BACKGROUNDS

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.6.0
Visible About version: 5.6.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.6.0-v1
Library schema version: unchanged (generated-page records tolerate the new optional field without a schema migration)

- New blank documents now offer a compact Blank background selector: White or Black. White is the default. After a blank document is created, the New selector resets to White so Black never becomes an accidental persistent default.
- Insert Page keeps the existing four built-in page-choice cards and adds only one short Blank background selector row beneath them. Each freshly opened Insert Page popover resets the blank-page choice to White.
- Black is not a viewer/theme effect. Generated blank pages now carry `generatedBackground: 'white' | 'black'`, and black pages render/export as real black page content.
- Black generated pages therefore survive Local Library persistence/restoration, page duplication, saved templates, page reordering/transfers, ordinary PDF export, and rasterized compression. Older generated-page records without `generatedBackground` naturally continue to render as White.
- Graph-paper pages remain white; this change applies only to blank pages. Automatic end-of-document blank-page append also remains White because Black is intentionally not remembered as a global/default page preference.
- The existing Pen and Highlighter palettes are unchanged. User testing established that the current non-black Pen colors and current Highlighter colors are satisfactory on black pages. Black Pen remains available and is naturally invisible on a black page; Workbench does not silently change annotation colors.
- A future White Pen color remains an optional convenience, not a prerequisite for black pages.
- History structure comparison now includes `generatedBackground`, so any future background-changing edit would correctly trigger a full base-page repaint rather than annotation-overlay-only Undo/Redo.
- Milestone 5.5.9 PDF-link policy is unchanged: untouched pass-through preserves everything; rebuilt PDFs preserve external URI links and strip internal/document-navigation Link annotations.

CURRENT ROADMAP / FUTURE POSSIBILITIES — PRESERVE EXPLICITLY

User priority note (2026-09-03): existing PDF hyperlinks and document outlines/bookmarks are NOT important to the user's normal PDF Workbench use. Do not make hyperlink/outline preservation a major milestone unless requirements change. Current rebuilt-export policy is intentionally simple: preserve standard external URI links, strip internal/document-navigation links, and accept that source outlines/bookmarks are not rebuilt.

Current Milestone 5/5.6 status:
- The original core annotation roadmap is essentially complete: Pen, Highlighter, true partial-stroke vector Eraser, Lasso/Select with transforms and clipboard operations, Undo/Redo, smoothing/stabilization, image annotations, persistence/export, and the major Apple Pencil/Surface/Chromebook input/performance work are in place.
- Session/workspace restoration, clean vs annotated templates, automatic end-of-document page creation, export-name suggestions after Library renames, More-menu layering, Presentation-ratio page creation, refined graph paper, deterministic rebuilt-PDF link handling, and White/Black blank-page creation/insertion are implemented and should not be treated as pending roadmap work.
- The old combined configurable-pen-preset idea has been superseded by the accepted direct color/width controls.

Future possibilities, roughly in current consideration order:
1. Performance/stability checkpoint after ordinary dense-document use. Reassess whether the 50-step session-only Undo history needs a memory-based cap in addition to the count limit; do not change preemptively without evidence.
2. Annotated-pages navigation/filter in Pages, initially something simple such as All | Annotated for pages containing any Workbench annotation.
3. Selection refinement: allow recoloring selected Workbench annotations. Selected-stroke width/opacity remain lower priority unless actual use calls for them.
4. Image annotations phase 2: independent rotation, system-clipboard image paste, crop, opacity/image editing, and layer ordering. Rotation and clipboard paste are the strongest candidates among these.
5. Annotation-toolbar convenience options: Always Visible/Auto-hide and possibly Top/Left/Right placement. Continue watching Full Page bottom navigation for palm interference, but do not change unless it becomes a real problem.
6. Small workflow conveniences: optional auto-return from Eraser, optional multi-finger Undo/Redo gestures, optional White/light Pen colors or a fuller color picker for dark/black pages and dark PDFs, and Draw with Finger. Pressure-sensitive Pen width remains low priority because it adds rendering/editing complexity.
7. Library enhancements: Search, user-selectable sorting, Favorites, and final Library UI cleanup. Schema/recovery hardening remains an ongoing responsibility rather than a single feature.
8. Minor end-of-document tuning: automatic page append is implemented; revisit trackpad threshold/prompt-linger behavior only if normal use shows it is annoying.
9. Optional PDF fidelity work only if a future use case demands it. Current policy is settled for normal use: external URI links are preserved on rebuilt exports; internal links are deliberately stripped; outlines/bookmarks are not rebuilt.
10. Laser Pointer remains intentionally deferred by user choice.

Superseded/settled sizing note:

MILESTONE 5.5.9 — DETERMINISTIC EXISTING-LINK EXPORT POLICY

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.5.9
Visible About version: 5.5.9
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.5.9-v1

- Completely untouched full single-source PDF export still uses the original byte stream unchanged. In that pass-through case, all original links, destinations, outlines/bookmarks, forms, metadata, and other PDF structures remain exactly as supplied.
- Whenever Workbench rebuilds a PDF, copied source-page Link annotations are now filtered deliberately. Standard external URI (`/A << /S /URI ... >>`) links are preserved. Internal/document-navigation links (`/Dest`, `/GoTo`, and any other non-URI Link action) are removed.
- Rationale: an internal link that silently points to the wrong page after reordering/deleting/extracting/combining/duplicating pages is worse than no link. Workbench does not maintain or remap PDF destination structures, so rebuilt PDFs should not contain navigation links whose validity is accidental.
- Non-Link PDF annotations are left untouched by this filter.
- Existing document outlines/bookmarks remain absent from rebuilt exports because the current pdf-lib copyPages-based export path does not reconstruct catalog-level outlines.
- Rasterize compression continues to remove all live links and other interactive structures by converting each page to an image.
- The viewer still does not create a PDF.js annotation layer or outline UI, so existing source links are not clickable inside Workbench and source outlines/bookmarks are not shown while editing.
- Milestone 5.5.8 annotation-only Undo/Redo overlay repaint optimization is unchanged.

CURRENT ROADMAP / FUTURE POSSIBILITIES — PRESERVE EXPLICITLY

User priority note (2026-09-03): existing PDF hyperlinks and document outlines/bookmarks are NOT important to the user's normal PDF Workbench use. Do not make hyperlink/outline preservation a major milestone unless requirements change. Link handling is intentionally simple: preserve standard external URI links on rebuilt exports, strip internal/document-navigation links, and accept that source outlines/bookmarks are not rebuilt.

- Session/workspace restoration, clean vs annotated templates, automatic end-of-document page creation, export-name suggestions after Library renames, and More-menu layering are already implemented/fixed and should not be treated as pending roadmap work.
- The old combined configurable-pen-preset idea has been superseded by the accepted direct color/width controls.

Future possibilities, roughly in current consideration order:

2. Annotated-pages navigation/filter in Pages, initially something simple such as All | Annotated for pages containing any Workbench annotation.

4. Image annotations phase 2: independent rotation, system-clipboard image paste, crop, opacity/image editing, and layer ordering. Rotation and clipboard paste are the strongest candidates among these.

CURRENT EXISTING-PDF LINK / OUTLINE BEHAVIOR (5.5.9)
- Viewer: Workbench renders PDF pages to canvas but does not construct a PDF.js annotation layer or outline/bookmark UI. Existing link annotations are therefore not exposed as clickable overlays in Workbench, and document outlines/bookmarks are not shown or used while editing. Visible link text that is part of the page artwork still renders like ordinary page content.
- Completely untouched full single-source PDF export: Workbench returns the original source byte stream unchanged, preserving all original PDF structures exactly.
- Rebuilt export: source page annotations are copied, then every copied Link annotation is inspected. Standard external URI actions are retained. Internal destinations, GoTo actions, and other non-URI Link annotations are removed before the page is added to the rebuilt document.
- Outlines/bookmarks on rebuilt export: the source document's catalog-level outline tree is not reconstructed and is therefore expected to be absent.
- Rasterize compression: intentionally converts every page to an image and removes live links/forms and other interactive PDF structures.

MILESTONE 5.5.8 — UNDO/REDO REPAINT CLEANUP

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.5.8
Visible About version: 5.5.8
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.5.8-v1

- Annotation-only Undo/Redo now detects whether page viewer structure is unchanged. If IDs/order/source/page geometry are identical, it restores the history snapshot and redraws only Workbench annotation overlays, preserving the already-rendered PDF/generated-page canvases and eliminating the visible whole-viewer flash.
- Page-structural Undo/Redo continues to call the full render path.
- Any pending deferred exact annotation redraw is cancelled before history restoration so stale pre-Undo page objects cannot repaint later.
- Diagnostic event `history-restore-finish` reports whether the fast overlay-only path was used.
- No changes to the 50-step session-only Undo stack, Presentation-ratio sizing, graph paper, Pen stabilization, image annotations, PDF export, or 0.94 touch-scroll inertia.

2. Annotated-pages navigation/filter in Pages, initially something simple such as All | Annotated for pages containing any Workbench annotation.
3. Selection refinement: allow recoloring selected Workbench annotations. Selected-stroke width/opacity remain lower priority unless actual use calls for them.

Superseded/settled sizing note:
- The older proposed Presentation canvas / smaller Presentation canvas / Letter choices evolved into the current Presentation Ratio · long side 11 in design. Treat the older three-choice proposal as superseded unless a separate smaller presentation size later proves useful.

CURRENT EXISTING-PDF LINK / OUTLINE BEHAVIOR (5.5.8)
- Viewer: Workbench renders PDF pages to canvas but does not construct a PDF.js annotation layer or outline/bookmark UI. Existing link annotations are therefore not exposed as clickable overlays in Workbench, and document outlines/bookmarks are not shown or used while editing. Visible link text that is part of the page artwork still renders like ordinary page content.
- Completely untouched full single-source PDF export: Workbench returns the original source byte stream unchanged. In this special pass-through case, links, outlines/bookmarks, forms, destinations, metadata, and other PDF structures remain exactly as in the source file.
- Rewritten export: if Workbench must rebuild the PDF because of annotations or other edits, it creates a new PDF and copies source pages with pdf-lib copyPages(). Page annotations are generally copied, so ordinary external URI hyperlink annotations may survive. Workbench does not explicitly inspect, remap, repair, or validate link destinations. Internal page links and named destinations therefore must NOT be assumed reliable after reordering/deleting/combining/subsetting/duplicating pages or other structural edits.
- Outlines/bookmarks on rewritten export: pdf-lib copyPages() does not copy the source document's catalog-level outlines/bookmarks into the newly created output PDF. Therefore existing outlines/bookmarks are expected to be lost whenever Workbench takes the rewrite path.
- Rasterize compression: intentionally converts every page to an image and therefore removes live links/forms and other interactive PDF structures.
- Because links/outlines are not needed for the user's normal workflow, preserving or repairing these structures is not currently an active roadmap priority.

MILESTONE 5.5.7 — PRESENTATION SIZING + GRAPH-PAPER REFINEMENT

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.5.7
Visible About version: 5.5.7
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.5.7-v1

- Presentation-ratio page geometry is predicted from the current device/window outside Presentation mode. The usable target subtracts the 46 px unified annotation toolbar plus top safe-area inset. iPad uses the current visual viewport; Surface/Chromebook use screen dimensions in the current orientation to predict native-fullscreen Presentation.
- Pages → Page size… now offers Presentation Ratio · long side 11 in using the same predictor as New documents. Existing Preserve/Portrait/Landscape controls remain available.
- Generated graph paper now targets 17.25 pt cells, chooses integer rows/columns with one common adjusted spacing, centers the small leftover margin, and draws a subtly darker outer grid boundary. This prevents partial edge cells while keeping cells square. Screen/PDF paths share the layout helper.
- Fit Page, annotation input, Pen stabilization, images, session-only Undo, and 0.94 touch-scroll inertia remain unchanged.

MILESTONE 5.5.6 — PRESENTATION-RATIO NEW DOCUMENTS

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.5.6

MILESTONE 5.5.6 — PRESENTATION-RATIO PAGE SIZE
- New Blank and Graph-paper documents can now be created as either US Letter landscape or Presentation Ratio.
- Presentation Ratio is calculated from the current device/orientation's predicted single-view Presentation canvas, not from physical viewport inches. iPad uses the current visual viewport because Presentation is app-level there; Surface/Chromebook use screen geometry because Presentation requests native fullscreen.
- The unified annotation-toolbar height and measured top safe-area inset are removed from the target Presentation height before calculating the ratio.
- The page long edge is normalized to exactly 11 in = 792 pt; the short edge follows the target aspect ratio. Current orientation determines landscape vs portrait.
- The New panel previews the resulting dimensions and refreshes on resize/orientation changes.
- Existing-document inserted blank/graph pages still match the current page geometry. No Fit Page behavior changed.
- Do not dynamically resize an existing Presentation-ratio page when the device later rotates; the ratio is captured at document creation time.

MILESTONE 5.5.3 — WIDTH-AWARE PEN SMOOTHING
- Pen smoothing is now width-aware rather than using one curve setting for every Pen width.
- 1.5 pt Pen: factor 0.18, handle cap 0.68 (largest smoothing increase).
- 3 pt Pen: factor 0.155, handle cap 0.62 (moderate increase).
- 5.5 pt Pen: factor 0.135, handle cap 0.58 (kept close to previous 0.13/0.58 behavior).
- Raw sampled points remain unchanged and authoritative for Eraser, lasso/selection, persistence, and session-only Undo/Redo.
- Live Pen rendering, final canvas redraws, and PDF export all pass the stroke width through the same smoothing-control calculation.
- Highlighter behavior is unchanged and remains unsmoothed.

- Laser Pointer is deferred by user choice for now.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 5.5.0 CURRENT UPDATE

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.5.0
Visible About version: 5.5.0
Library schema version: 6
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.5.0-v1
Baseline: 5.4.8, validated under heavy annotation load on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook.

MILESTONE 5.5.0 — INSERTED IMAGE ANNOTATIONS
- Added a picture action button to the annotation strip plus a dedicated image picker input (`accept=image/*`).
- Inserted images are annotation objects: `{ type:'image', sourceId, x, y, width, height, opacity }`, with geometry in unrotated page coordinates.
- New images are centered/scaled to fit, selected immediately, and Select mode becomes active.
- Selection/lasso infrastructure is image-aware: hit testing, polygon selection, group bounds, optimized move preview, proportional resize, Delete, Duplicate, Copy, Paste, Undo/Redo, and mixed ink+image groups.
- Screen rendering uses a dedicated `.annotation-image-canvas` at z-index 1, below the existing ink/highlighter canvas at z-index 2. This preserves the 5.4 eraser architecture: live partial erasing only modifies ink, not inserted image pixels.
- Inserted image binaries reuse the existing Local Library `sources` store. Annotation objects hold source ids, so duplicate/copy/paste/page duplication does not duplicate binary data.
- All source-reference plumbing now includes annotation-image sources: current persistence, reopen, previews, templates, backup validation/restore, backup-as-folder source-id remapping, Library PDF export, source-release checks, and permanent folder/document deletion cleanup.
- PDF export is image-aware. Inserted images are embedded below Workbench ink/highlighter and follow inherited PDF rotation plus Workbench page rotation. Preserve-mode image compression can downsample inserted image annotations too.
- `hasPageAnnotations()` now treats inserted images as Workbench annotations, so untouched-byte passthrough is correctly disabled.
- Page geometry transformations (canvas resize/crop/margins) now shift/fit image rectangles along with ink.
- The 5.4.8 stable Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/input/performance behavior is intentionally unchanged.
- Undo/Redo remains session-local and starts fresh after a true restart.
- Keep the reminder to ask about dense-page performance later after normal use.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 5.5.0
1. iPad first: insert from Photos/Files; move/resize with Pencil; draw/highlight over the image; erase ink across it; export.
2. Surface + Chromebook: same operations with mouse/pen/touch; regression-test finger navigation and palm behavior.
3. Copy/paste and duplicate images within page, across pages, and across open documents.
4. Mixed lasso: select image plus ink, move and resize together.
5. Rotate page 90/180/270, then move/resize image and export; compare screen to PDF.
6. Save page with inserted image as template, duplicate the page, close/reopen app, and confirm persistence.
7. Editable backup/restore and backup-as-folder import must preserve image binaries and source references.
8. Re-run dense-page stress briefly to ensure the added image layer did not disturb 5.4.8 performance.

DEFERRED IMAGE FEATURES
- Crop.
- Independent image rotation.
- System clipboard image paste.
- Image opacity/editing controls.
- Layer-order controls; images are below ink/highlighter in 5.5.0.
