PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 5.4.8 CURRENT UPDATE

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.4.8
Visible About version: 5.4.8
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m5.4.8-v1
Baseline: 5.4.7, whose dense-page iPad stress test was reported immensely better; Surface and Chromebook regression testing is next.


MILESTONE 5.4.8 — POST-PERFORMANCE CLEANUP
- Cleanup/performance-polish release; no change to Pen/Highlighter geometry, partial-erasure semantics, Select semantics, input routing, PDF export, finger navigation, split view, session restoration, templates, or automatic page append.
- Durable Library document records now omit history/future entirely. Undo/Redo remains session-local and resets after a true app restart.
- Library refresh performs a one-time prune of legacy persisted history/future fields from closed records, so old dense histories do not remain in IndexedDB simply because those documents were never reopened under 5.4.7.
- Restore backup and Import backup as folder both strip legacy persisted Undo/Redo arrays before writing document records. New 5.4.8 backups therefore contain current editable state but no cross-restart Undo stack.
- Removed superseded 5.4.1 smooth:false eraser-redraw plumbing and a dead preview flag. Current live Eraser remains the 5.4.4 architecture: transient raster destination-out feedback during contact and compacted/candidate-filtered vector surgery once on release. The exact full annotation repaint is now deferred until browser idle so pointerup/UI response is not blocked by a dense-page redraw; cancelled gestures still repaint immediately.
- Retain the useful optimizations discovered during 5.4: dedicated live Highlighter layer, raw Highlighter geometry, batched coalesced samples, optimized selection preview, deferred Eraser vector commit, Highlighter PDF centerline simplification, and gesture-aware autosave scheduling.
- 5.4.7 diagnostics showed a scheduled Library save could begin about 850 ms after one Eraser release and still be finishing when the next swipe began. Annotation autosave is now debounced to about 1.4 seconds after the last edit; lifecycle persistence remains immediate.
- Diagnostics now also report the deferred exact annotation redraw time after an Eraser release.
- Retain detailed diagnostics through Surface/Chromebook regression testing.
- FOLLOW-UP: after additional normal use and later feature work, explicitly ask about dense-page performance again. If degradation returns, consider a 20-step or memory-aware in-session Undo cap; do not change the current 50-step limit yet.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 5.4.8
1. Surface: regression-test Pen hover/cursor suppression, Pen/Highlighter responsiveness, Eraser, lasso manipulation, finger navigation, split view, export, and restart/session restoration.
2. Chromebook: regression-test palm rejection, finger pan/scroll/pinch, Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select responsiveness, split view, export, and restart/session restoration. Specifically check the small post-Eraser-release pause reported there as well as on iPad.
3. No special iPad retest is required beyond ordinary use unless a regression appears; iPad remains the primary target and 5.4.7 dense-page testing was a major improvement.
4. Later, ask again about dense-page performance under normal real work before deciding whether to limit Undo by count or memory.


MILESTONE 5.4.7 — DENSE-PAGE HISTORY / AUTOSAVE MEMORY FIX
- iPad stress test plus GoodNotes comparison confirmed that hundreds of page marks are a reasonable workload and that remaining slowdown was Workbench-specific, not a device limit.
- Root cause found in Undo/Redo + persistence architecture: snapshotPages() cloned every page/annotation point into ordinary JS objects for each undo step (up to 50), and serializeDocumentForLibrary() then cloned/persisted the entire history and future arrays on every autosave. This created large memory/GC pressure and multi-second post-gesture UI stalls on dense pages.
- History snapshots now pack annotation point coordinates into Float32Array x/y pairs. restorePages() transparently inflates them only when Undo/Redo is actually used.
- Durable Local Library persistence stores only the current editable document state; 5.4.7 wrote history/future empty and deliberately did not hydrate legacy history. 5.4.8 subsequently removes those durable fields entirely and prunes old records. Undo/Redo remains fully available during the current app session, but starts fresh after restart.
- Added library-persist-start/library-persist-finish diagnostics including serializeMs and totalMs to verify that autosave no longer causes UI stalls.
- 5.4.6 live Highlighter batching, direct composite commit, geometry reuse, autosave gesture deferral, 5.4.4 optimized selection/eraser previews, and 5.4.5 Highlighter PDF cleanup are retained.

MILESTONE 5.4.6 — DENSE-PAGE LIVE GESTURE SCHEDULING
- Scheduled Local Library persistence is deferred while any Pen/Highlighter/Eraser/Select gesture is active. This prevents dense document cloning/serialization from starting midway through a stylus drag. Explicit lifecycle persistence remains available.
- Highlighter start no longer performs a redundant full-page annotation redraw.
- Highlighter coalesced samples are drawn as one batch path per PointerEvent rather than one Canvas/DOM setup per raw sample.
- Finished Highlighter strokes are composited directly from the live temporary layer into the persistent annotation canvas rather than rebuilding all existing page annotations.
- Highlighter and Eraser reuse one stage geometry measurement for an entire coalesced PointerEvent batch, avoiding repeated layout reads.
- 5.4.5 PDF Highlighter centerline simplification, 5.4.4 selection/eraser acceleration, 5.4.3 duplicate-open repair, Pen smoothing, and authoritative raw annotation geometry are retained.

MILESTONE 5.4.3 — LIVE HIGHLIGHTER + DUPLICATE-OPEN REPAIR
- Live Highlighter uses a dedicated temporary overlay canvas and incremental opaque drawing with canvas-element opacity. It no longer redraws all existing annotations on every Pencil move.
- Completed Highlighter strokes remain raw polylines but are isolated/composited once per stroke on the persistent Canvas overlay to avoid darker sample beads caused by raw-path self-overlap.
- PDF export remains unchanged from 5.4.2.
- Same-id open-document duplicates are prevented by a post-await reopen recheck and repaired before open-document UI rendering/Library persistence.
- Duplicate repair prefers the actual live active document object, then newer/richer state, so annotations are not discarded in favor of a stale clean copy.
- Pen smoothing, eraser fast preview, selection, palm rejection, session restoration, templates, and automatic new-page behavior are unchanged.


MILESTONE 5.4.2 — HIGHLIGHTER PERFORMANCE REFINEMENT
- Pen retains the 5.4.x restrained cardinal-spline screen/PDF smoothing.
- Highlighter now uses the authoritative raw sampled points as one continuous round-joined/round-capped polyline on screen and in PDF export.
- Rationale: wide translucent highlighter ink gains little from cubic smoothing, while full-path cubic reconstruction on every live pointer update caused noticeable iPad lag.
- Highlighter still redraws its complete current path once per pointer event to avoid opacity buildup/dark joints.
- 5.4.1 raw-overlay eraser optimization remains. Input routing and annotation data model are unchanged.

MILESTONE 5.4.1 — IPAD ERASER PERFORMANCE FIX

- 5.4.0 smoothing remains the normal on-screen and PDF-export rendering.
- During an active partial-eraser gesture, overlay redraws temporarily use the authoritative raw polylines instead of recomputing every smoothed cubic path on every move.
- On eraser release, surviving annotations are immediately redrawn smoothed.
- No Pen/Highlighter input routing or stored annotation geometry changed.
- Motivation: user testing found erasing on iPad became unusably slow on highlighted pages after 5.4.0, while Surface remained acceptable.

MILESTONE 5.4.0 — FIRST HANDWRITING SMOOTHING PASS
- Raw/coalesced Pen and Highlighter samples remain the authoritative editable object data; no storage/schema migration.
- Completed strokes render/export with a restrained cardinal spline converted to continuous cubic Bézier segments. The curve passes through source samples.
- Control handles are capped relative to each raw segment to reduce overshoot/loops around tight handwriting corners and keep the displayed curve close to raw eraser/lasso geometry.
- Live Pen stays incremental rather than full-page-redrawing on every sample. A newly arrived sample finalizes the preceding cubic segment; final pen-up redraw clears the tiny provisional first segment/tail.
- Highlighter retains full-current-path redraw per pointer event for uniform transparency, now using the same smooth curve.
- PDF export uses cubic `C` path commands corresponding to the screen smoothing; round caps/joins and opacity are retained.
- Partial Eraser, Lasso/Select manipulation, Undo/Redo, copy/paste, persistence, and backups continue to operate on unchanged raw points.
- No intentional changes to Apple Pencil fallback, Surface/ChromeOS Pointer Events, palm rejection, finger navigation, session restoration, templates, automatic appended pages, live pinch scaling, or PWA asset-cache behavior.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 5.4.0:
1. iPad and Surface: rapid handwriting and short multi-stroke symbols; compare latency with 5.3.0 and inspect smoothing on pen-up.
2. Tight loops/sharp corners/fast strokes/dots: check for spline overshoot or end-of-stroke snapping.
3. Partial erase and lasso move/resize/copy/paste smoothed-looking strokes; raw edit geometry should remain robust.
4. Export mixed Pen + Highlighter annotations and verify Acrobat matches the smoothed Workbench rendering and erased regions stay gone.
5. Split view, pinch zoom, PWA restart/session restore, and installed-iPad update regression smoke tests.
6. Chromebook compatibility can be tested later; it is not the primary target.

FUTURE NEW-DOCUMENT SIZE REQUEST (2026-09-02):
- Blank/Graph document creation should eventually offer US Letter plus a Presentation-ratio option.
- The Presentation option only needs to match the current usable Presentation canvas aspect ratio; it does not need to represent the device's physical screen size.
- Normalize the generated Presentation-ratio page so its long edge is 11 inches, then derive the short edge from the measured canvas aspect ratio.
- A slightly-smaller presentation option is optional and can be discussed later.

NEXT MAJOR FEATURE AFTER THIS PASS:
- Image insertion as annotation objects integrated with the existing selection/manipulation system.

MILESTONE 5.2.2 — LIVE PINCH ANNOTATION SCALING FIX
- Root cause: Milestone 5.1.0 separated annotations onto a transparent overlay canvas. Live pinch zoom resized only the first/base PDF canvas, leaving the annotation overlay at the old CSS dimensions until the final rerender.
- Fix: live single-view and split-pane zoom now resize every canvas in each page stage, so PDF raster and annotation overlay scale together throughout the gesture.
- Final crisp rerender remains unchanged.
- 5.2.1 PWA cache consistency/versioned asset behavior retained.
- No input pipeline, eraser geometry, lasso manipulation, export, template, automatic-page, or session-restoration behavior changed.

MILESTONE 5.2.1 — INSTALLED-PWA CACHE CONSISTENCY HOTFIX
- iPad Safari tab testing of 5.2.0 worked, but the installed PWA showed the new Select button with no lasso icon and clicking it did nothing. That combination is diagnostic of mixed release assets: new HTML plus older cached CSS/JavaScript.
- The lasso/selection implementation is unchanged.
- `index.html` now fingerprints first-party `styles.css` and `app.js` with the release version.
- Service-worker install explicitly fetches the app shell with `cache: reload`, then caches those exact versioned requests, preventing stale browser HTTP-cache entries from contaminating a new PWA release cache.
- Existing controller-change reload/update behavior remains in place.

MILESTONE 5.2.0 — LASSO / OBJECT SELECTION AND MANIPULATION
====================================================================

Confirmed baseline before this build:
- 5.1.1 works on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook.
- Pen input remains stable on all three; Chromebook inking was reported more responsive than GoodNotes Web before smoothing.
- Partial-stroke eraser works in single/split view and Presentation/non-Presentation on all three; exported PDFs contain only surviving vector ink and no deleted stroke remnants.
- Contextual annotation toolbar works on all three.
- Template creation, automatic new page, and session restoration are all confirmed working on all three.
- Automatic end-page append has minor trackpad sensitivity / prompt-linger tuning issues, but these are deliberately deferred until normal use shows whether they are bothersome.

5.2.0 implementation:
- Adds Lasso / Select as a fourth annotation tool. Stylus/mouse performs selection; finger navigation continues through the existing stylus-aware pan/pinch/palm-rejection path.
- Lasso selection is object-grained: if a stroke/object is intersected or enclosed, the whole object is selected. Partial editing remains the job of the 5.1.0 eraser.
- Selected objects get a visible SVG bounding box and four corner resize handles. Dragging inside the box moves the group; dragging a handle resizes the group proportionally in page coordinates and scales stroke widths with the geometry.
- Contextual Select actions: Delete, Duplicate, Copy, Paste.
- Copy/Paste uses an internal annotation clipboard. It supports same-page, other-page, and other-open-document workflows. Pasted/duplicated annotations receive independent IDs and become the new selection.
- Keyboard shortcuts while Select is active: Delete/Backspace, Ctrl/Cmd+C, Ctrl/Cmd+V, Ctrl/Cmd+D.
- Move, resize, delete, duplicate, and paste are normal Undo/Redo operations and use the same persisted/exported vector annotation model.
- Same-page split-view instances redraw live during move/resize. Selection UI is a separate overlay and is never exported.
- Existing 5.1.1 pen/eraser input, ChromeOS palm rejection, Surface hover suppression, session restore, templates, automatic page append, and PDF export paths are intentionally unchanged.

TEST PRIORITY:
1. Lasso one stroke and a multi-stroke group on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook; verify selection is whole-object and finger pan/pinch remains usable.
2. Move and resize selected groups, including each corner handle; verify Undo/Redo and same-page split-view live synchronization.
3. Delete and Duplicate selected objects; verify Undo/Redo and export.
4. Copy/Paste on the same page, another page, and another open document; verify independent objects and reasonable paste offset/placement.
5. Export after every manipulation type and confirm no lasso/bounding-box UI is present in Adobe Acrobat.
6. Quick pen/eraser/palm/session regression only; those subsystems were not intentionally changed.

MILESTONE 5.1.0 — PARTIAL-STROKE VECTOR ERASER
====================================================================

Confirmed baseline before this build:
- 5.0.10 stable pen/Pencil input works on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook.
- Surface hover '+' cursor suppression and ChromeOS palm rejection work; deliberate finger scrolling/panning/zooming still work on all three.
- 5.0.11 template creation (with annotations / clean) and automatic new-last-page creation work on all three.
- 5.0.12 session restoration works on all three.
- User specifically requires erasing portions of strokes/objects; selection may remain whole-stroke/object based.

5.1.0 implementation:
- New Eraser tool with remembered 12/24/40 pt circular sizes.
- Partial erasure edits vector stroke geometry: an eraser pass cuts intersected polylines and stores surviving portions as ordinary stroke fragments. It does not paint white and does not delete the whole stroke unless all of it is erased.
- Eraser hit radius includes half of each stroke's width so the visible erased swath matches the requested eraser footprint after round end caps are drawn.
- One eraser contact commits one Undo/Redo history step. Fragments persist/export through the existing annotation model.
- Viewer annotations are now drawn on a transparent overlay canvas above the unchanged page raster. This permits low-latency eraser redraw without PDF/image rerendering on every move and prepares the viewer for selection/highlighter overlays.
- Same page in split panes redraws live in both panes.
- Existing Apple Pencil stylus TouchEvent fallback is generalized to Eraser; Pointer Events remain primary. Existing 5.0.9 palm suppression applies to Pen and Eraser.
- Circular custom eraser cursor added for mouse/stylus hover; native Surface pen cursor remains hidden.
- Pen sampling/storage/export behavior was not otherwise changed. Smoothing is still deferred.

TEST PRIORITY:
1. On iPad, Surface, and Chromebook: draw crossing lines and handwriting, erase only middle portions using all three sizes, and verify no whole-stroke deletion unless fully covered.
2. Undo immediately after a multi-cut eraser gesture; the entire gesture should restore in one step. Redo should reapply it.
3. Same-document split view: erase while the same page is visible in both panes and confirm live shared update.
4. Export after erasing and verify the PDF contains the surviving vector fragments/gaps.
5. Confirm palm rejection and deliberate finger pan/pinch remain correct in Eraser mode.
6. Quick regression: restart with multiple open documents/split view and confirm session restoration still works.

MILESTONE 5.0.12 — SESSION RESTORATION STARTUP-RACE FIX
====================================================================

User test result from 5.0.11:
- Template creation with/without annotations works on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook.
- Automatic last-page append and configured page source work on all three devices.
- Session restoration failed on all three devices.

5.0.12 restoration correction:
- Keep the stable 5.0.10 input/palm/cursor path completely unchanged.
- Keep the working 5.0.11 template/end-of-document features unchanged.
- Root architectural weakness addressed: event handlers are bound before persistent Library restoration, so a startup visibility/page-lifecycle event could synchronously checkpoint the transient empty workspace and make it newer than the real saved session. 5.0.12 blocks session writes until startup has read/restored the previous session.
- Synchronous checkpoint key advanced to `pdfwb-session-checkpoint-v2` so an empty/stale v1 checkpoint from 5.0.11 cannot dominate the corrected logic.
- Session records now carry `explicitEmpty`. A newer empty record may override a non-empty record only when the user deliberately closed the last/all documents.
- Once hydrated, open/close/current-document/workspace-mode/split changes checkpoint immediately, while IndexedDB continues to receive the full durable session through normal persistence.
- Restoration attempts saved document IDs directly via Library/IndexedDB; stale deleted/trashed IDs are ignored.
- A startup Library reconnection re-enters restoration instead of persisting an empty workspace.

TEST PRIORITY:
1. In 5.0.12, manually open at least two Library documents, leave one active, close/restart the app, and confirm both reopen with the same active document.
2. Repeat with Split view and different documents/pages in the two panes.
3. Use Close all files, restart, and confirm the workspace intentionally stays empty while the documents remain in Local Library.
4. Reconfirm one quick inking/palm test only as a regression check; the input code was not intentionally changed.

MILESTONE 5.0.11 — SESSION RESTORE / TEMPLATES / AUTOMATIC LAST PAGE
====================================================================

Cross-device input baseline:
- 5.0.10 tested successfully on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook. Keep its PointerEvent/Apple Pencil TouchEvent path, ChromeOS palm rejection, finger navigation, selection suppression, and pen-hover cursor handling unchanged unless a new regression appears.
- User reports Chromebook raw inking is currently more responsive than GoodNotes Web; smoothing is deliberately deferred so later smoothing must preserve this low-latency feel.

Session restoration hardening:
- Existing IndexedDB session metadata remains authoritative durable storage.
- Added a small throttled synchronous localStorage checkpoint (`pdfwb-session-checkpoint-v1`) containing the same workspace/session metadata. pagehide and visibility hidden force an immediate checkpoint before the asynchronous IndexedDB save.
- On startup, choose the newer valid IndexedDB session or localStorage checkpoint. This targets the installed-PWA shutdown race that could leave Library documents present but reopen with an empty active workspace.
- Session includes open documents, current document, workspace mode, split state, active pane, single-source pane, pane document assignments, and pane view maps.
- Close-one and Close-all still persist the intentionally closed workspace. Backup restore clears the transient localStorage checkpoint before reload so restored backup session metadata is not overridden by a stale checkpoint.

Template changes:
- Save-current-page-as-template now offers **With annotations** (default/current behavior) or **Clean page (no annotations)**.
- Template Manager now includes **Automatic new last page** preference. Factory default is Graph paper. Choices: Graph paper, Blank, or a saved template.
- Graph/Blank automatic pages use the display dimensions of the preceding last page; saved templates keep their own dimensions.
- If the selected automatic template is deleted/missing, preference falls back to Graph paper. Preference is stored with template metadata and therefore travels in editable Library backups.

End-of-document append:
- Continuous/Page Snap viewers receive an end-of-document pull strip after the final page. Pull/scroll until it reaches the ready threshold and says Release to add, then release to append one page.
- Wheel/trackpad downward scrolling can trigger the same threshold.
- Full Page mode uses one additional forward swipe/wheel action from the final page to append. Ordinary Next buttons/keyboard page navigation do not silently create pages.
- Automatic append uses the existing normal insertion/history/persistence path and focuses the new page. In split view it edits the shared document in the pane that initiated the gesture.
- A short append lock prevents momentum scrolling from rapidly creating multiple pages.

NEXT PRIORITIES AFTER 5.0.11 TESTING:
- Confirm session restart behavior, including Split→restart and intentional Close all files→restart.
- Confirm automatic append on iPad, Surface, and Chromebook in Continuous, Page Snap, and Full Page modes and with Graph/Blank/template defaults.
- Then proceed to deliberate stroke smoothing/interpolation while preserving raw sample points and current latency.
- Fine-grained partial-stroke eraser, then whole-stroke/object lasso/select with move/resize/delete/copy/duplicate, then Highlighter.

MILESTONE 5.0.10 — STABLE CROSS-PLATFORM INPUT CLEANUP
====================================================================

Cross-platform result before this cleanup:
- iPad: Apple Pencil is stable without skipped strokes; native Copy/Look Up selection leakage remains suppressed; deliberate finger navigation still works.
- Surface: pen writes reliably; 5.0.9 removed the distracting hover crosshair/plus cursor; finger navigation remains usable.
- Chromebook: 5.0.9 palm suppression stopped the canvas jumping while preserving deliberate finger scroll/pan/pinch.

5.0.10 cleanup decisions:
- KEEP Safari Apple Pencil stylus TouchEvent fallback plus PointerEvent/TouchEvent deduplication.
- KEEP ChromeOS palm suppression / short touch-intent gate and extra-contact filtering.
- KEEP Pen-mode native selection/callout suppression.
- KEEP geometric page hit-testing for a retargeted pen start.
- KEEP pen-hover cursor suppression.
- REMOVE the speculative 5.0.6 move-start recovery. An unowned contact-bearing pen `pointermove` is suppressed but never starts ink.
- REMOVE the speculative 5.0.6 pointerup-only-dot recovery. An unmatched pen `pointerup` never invents ink.
- RESTORE tip-only pen starts: pen pointerdown must have `button === 0`; normal iPad, Surface, and Chromebook tip contacts have all been observed with button 0. Non-tip/barrel-button starts are suppressed and logged.
- KEEP diagnostics available while eraser/lasso work begins.
- No changes to stored stroke geometry, raw/coalesced sample storage, Canvas/PDF rendering, export, split-pane synchronization, persistence formats, or palm-rejection timing.

Annotation design requirement clarified after 5.0.9 testing:
- Eraser must support fine-grained deletion of portions of strokes/objects; whole-stroke-only erasing is not sufficient.
- Lasso/selection may operate at whole-stroke/object granularity; sub-stroke selection is not required.

NEXT PRIORITIES AFTER 5.0.10:
- Session-restoration regression.
- Template creation option: with annotations vs clean/no annotations.
- End-of-document upward-swipe/new-page behavior design.
- Deliberate stroke smoothing/interpolation while preserving raw sample points.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 5.0.9 CURRENT UPDATE

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 5.0.9

MILESTONE 5.0.9 — CHROMEOS PALM SUPPRESSION / PEN-HOVER CURSOR
====================================================================

- Cross-platform testing of 5.0.8: Apple Pencil on iPad is stable; Surface Pen writes without skipping; Chromebook pen writing is accepted, but a resting palm can make the canvas jump badly.
- The Chromebook diagnostic shows why: ChromeOS reports the palm as a rapid burst of many ordinary `pointerType: "touch"` contacts while Pen mode is active. The existing manual viewer navigation immediately interpreted those as alternating one-finger pan and two-finger pinch gestures.
- 5.0.9 leaves the successful 5.0.8 pen/stylus ink path unchanged. In Pen mode only, touch navigation now waits 120 ms before committing to pan/pinch. Three or more contacts during the pending interval classify the gesture as palm and suppress it until the contacts lift.
- Pen hover/contact proximity, when exposed by the platform, also suppresses new touch contacts for palm rejection. Once a deliberate two-finger navigation gesture has been accepted, additional contacts are ignored rather than changing the gesture. Hand mode retains immediate finger navigation.
- Surface test also showed a distracting plus/crosshair while the pen hovered. A real pen pointer over the viewer now hides the browser cursor; normal mouse behavior is unchanged.
- Diagnostics remain available and include palm-suppression / deliberate-touch-navigation markers.
- GoodNotes Web on the same Chromebook did not show the palm-jump problem, supporting the conclusion that browser-side palm filtering is feasible on this hardware rather than an unavoidable ChromeOS limitation.

====================================================================
MILESTONE 5.0.8 — APPLE PENCIL TOUCH-EVENT FALLBACK / MORE-MENU STACKING FIX

- 5.0.7 diagnostic result: all 80 page Pencil contacts that produced a raw `pointerdown` were accepted and finished by the ink handler; no stage-miss, move-recovery, up-only-recovery, or pointer-cancel records appeared. Missing handwritten strokes therefore occurred before Workbench received a usable PointerEvent contact.
- 5.0.8 keeps Pointer Events as the normal path but also listens for Safari Touch Events explicitly identified by `Touch.touchType === "stylus"`. A stylus TouchEvent may recover a contact when Safari omits its PointerEvent. When both streams exist they are deduplicated so only one stroke is created.
- Diagnostic download remains available and records the stylus-touch path.
- The recurring More-menu-behind-toolbar bug was a stacking-context problem: `#moreMenu` is a child of `.app-bar`, whose z-index was below the annotation toolbar. 5.0.8 raises the app-bar stacking context above the annotation bar so its popover can render in front.

MILESTONE 5.0.7 — APPLE PENCIL DROPPED-STROKE DIAGNOSTIC
- Controlled iPad handwriting showed that multi-contact symbols lose whole contacts: many +, i, =, and Q marks lose one component, while one-stroke s characters are mostly preserved.
- This is distinct from stroke smoothing/interpolation and from the native Copy/Look Up selection issue (which 5.0.5/5.0.6 stopped).
- 5.0.7 adds lightweight contact-boundary and ink-handler diagnostics under More > Download Pencil diagnostics. It deliberately avoids another speculative input-routing change until the actual WebKit event stream is captured.
- Diagnostic output is text/JSONL and contains event metadata only, not document content.

====================================================================
MILESTONE 5.0.7 — IPAD / APPLE PENCIL PEN-MODE NATIVE-SELECTION FIX
====================================================================

New iPad result after 5.0.4:
- 5.0.4 successfully made the annotation toolbar itself non-selectable, but while writing with Apple Pencil iPadOS then occasionally selected text in the footer/status area instead and showed the native Copy / Look Up / … callout.
- This confirms the failure is a WebKit/Pencil native-selection leak/retargeting problem, not a specific Hand-glyph problem and not a real tool activation.
- The user also noted that GoodNotes can occasionally select underlying PDF text while inking; PDF Workbench should NOT accept that behavior as normal. Ink-tool input should win over native text selection.

5.0.7 fix:
- Native selection suppression now follows the input mode instead of a single DOM element.
- While Pen is active in the document viewer, a document-level capture listener prevents `selectstart` and `contextmenu`/touch-callout activation, and any selection range WebKit nevertheless creates is immediately cleared on `selectionchange`.
- CSS also marks the viewer pane, annotation/presentation bar, app bar, and status/footer bar non-selectable/non-callout while Pen is active, so WebKit does not simply move the selection to the next available piece of UI text.
- The document-level JavaScript guard is active only for Pen while the viewer is the current workspace (or Presentation is active). Hand/View mode deliberately remains outside the guard so intentional text selection can be supported there later.
- Intentional Hand/Pen/color/width taps remain ordinary button actions.
- No changes were made to stored stroke points, coalesced-event sampling, stroke geometry, Canvas rendering, PDF export, split-pane synchronization, or finger pan/pinch.

IMMEDIATE IPAD RETEST:
1. Select Pen and write several lines continuously in ordinary View and Presentation.
2. Confirm no native Copy / Look Up / … menu appears on the toolbar, footer/status text, or any selectable PDF/page text.
3. Repeat with the writing area near the top and far down the page, with and without the palm resting.
4. Intentionally tap Hand, Pen, colors, and widths with Apple Pencil; normal toolbar actions must still work.
5. Confirm one-finger pan and two-finger pinch remain navigation-only.
6. In same-document split view, confirm live ink still appears in both panes.

KNOWN FOLLOW-UP ITEMS AFTER CURRENT TEST ROUND:
- Session-restoration regression: closing/restarting the app currently appears to leave previously open documents closed. Intended behavior is to restore the open workspace/session.
- Template creation currently includes annotations. Add an explicit clean/no-annotations template option, analogous to Duplicate + notes vs Duplicate clean.
- End-of-document behavior: discuss whether an upward swipe at the bottom should append a new page and decide what page/template/background should be used.
- Continue Pen testing, including Surface Pen when available.
- After input correctness is stable, add deliberate stroke smoothing/interpolation while preserving raw sample points, then a fine-grained eraser that can remove portions of strokes/objects, lasso/select at whole-stroke/object granularity (sub-stroke selection is not required), and Highlighter.

====================================================================
MILESTONE 5.0.2 — PDF INK EXPORT JOIN/CAP FIX
====================================================================

User-reported export defect after initial 5.0.1 pen testing:
- Wider curved strokes showed repeated white wedges/slits on the inside of turns in the exported PDF. The effect was especially visible in the thick black and green sample curves.
- This was distinct from the planned later interpolation/smoothing work. The stored stroke was a sampled polyline, but the immediate white-gap defect came from how that polyline was emitted to PDF.

Root cause and fix:
- 5.0.0/5.0.1 exported every adjacent sampled point pair as a separate `drawLine` operation, then added circles only at the two overall stroke ends. Separate PDF line segments do not form a joined stroke at their interior endpoints, so wider turns could expose triangular white gaps.
- 5.0.2 maps the same stored points to PDF coordinates but emits the complete stroke as ONE `drawSvgPath` polyline. The path is stroked with round line joins and round end caps.
- The enclosing PDF graphics state sets a round line join; `drawSvgPath` supplies the round cap and the existing stroke opacity. This preserves a single transparency operation for the stroke and avoids dark overlap nodes, which is useful groundwork for a future translucent Highlighter.
- Single-point taps still export as filled circles.
- No smoothing/interpolation is added here. Raw stored points and on-screen completed-stroke rendering remain unchanged.

IMMEDIATE RETEST:
- Make several thick loops, S-curves, and tight turns with the 5.5 pt Pen, then export. Inspect the PDF at high zoom; the white wedges between sampled segments should be gone.
- Also verify thin/medium widths and all pen colors.
- Recheck at least one rotated/CropBox page if convenient, because coordinate mapping itself was intentionally left unchanged.
- The on-screen handwriting may still look somewhat polygonal. That is the separate planned interpolation/smoothing pass.

NEXT ANNOTATION WORK AFTER BASIC PEN CORRECTNESS:
1. Deliberate stroke smoothing/interpolation while preserving raw sample points.
2. Partial-stroke eraser built against the stable stroke geometry/data model.
3. Lasso/select with move + resize + delete + duplicate.
4. Highlighter with its own remembered palette/widths.

MILESTONE 5.0.1 — PRESENTATION TOOLBAR POSITIONING FIX
====================================================================

User-reported regression found immediately after the 5.0.0 annotation-toolbar change:
- In Presentation mode, roughly the left half of the top toolbar could be shifted outside the viewport. The screenshot showed only the right-hand Presentation controls while the annotation tools/colors/widths were off-screen.
- Root cause: a legacy CSS rule from the former centered floating Presentation palette still applied `transform: translateX(-50%)`. Milestone 5 changed the same element into a full-width bar anchored with left: 0, so that old transform translated the entire full-width bar left by half its own width.
- Fix: the Milestone 5 Presentation annotation-toolbar rule now explicitly sets `transform: none !important` (and top-left transform origin), keeping the unified strip edge-docked across the full viewport.
- This is intentionally a narrow UI bug fix. No ink model, export, Library, backup, history, or viewer-state logic was changed.

IMMEDIATE RETEST:
- Enter Presentation on the same device/layout that produced the screenshot. Confirm Hand, Pen, all five color dots, all three widths, Undo/Redo, and the appended Presentation controls are reachable across the top strip rather than starting off-screen.
- Check both Single and Split Presentation.
- Recheck View -> Presentation -> View anchoring after this CSS-only fix.

NEXT ANNOTATION PRIORITY AFTER THIS RETEST:
1. Partial-stroke eraser.
2. Lasso/select with move + resize + delete + duplicate.
3. Highlighter with its own remembered palette/widths.

====================================================================
MILESTONE 5.0.0 — FIRST ANNOTATION / INKING SLICE
====================================================================

Baseline:
- Milestone 4.2.2 is the preferred validated Milestone 4 baseline. Its Files UI was approved on Surface and iPad in wide and narrow layouts.
- Milestone 4.2.1 Library/export functionality was broadly validated on Surface and iPad: create/move/import/export, editable backup, full restore, backup import as subtree, Trash/Restore/Permanent Delete, and cross-platform backup portability.
- The former export-size defect is fixed: the test slide deck exports byte-for-byte when untouched and slightly smaller after structural edits. Do not regress unchanged-byte passthrough or batched source-page copy/resource sharing.

Annotation-control decisions:
- Use one thin edge-docked toolbar architecture, defaulting to the full top edge. Do not put annotation controls at the bottom because of palm/hand rejection.
- Do not permanently favor left- or right-handed users with a required side toolbar. Edge placement may become a setting later.
- The same annotation controls and ordering should appear in ordinary View and Presentation. Presentation's former floating control palette is folded into this top strip.
- Presentation 5.0.0 keeps the bar always visible so Pen/color/width changes are immediate. Add an Always visible / Auto-hide setting later, after core annotation behavior is validated.
- Normal View retains its existing app bar; the annotation strip sits directly beneath it. Presentation hides the app bar and docks this same strip at the absolute top.

5.0.0 implemented tool slice:
- Hand/View tool: stylus does not draw; finger navigation remains one-finger pan/scroll and two-finger pinch.
- Pen tool. Primary mouse drawing is also allowed for desktop testing.
- Direct Pen palette: black, blue, red, green, orange.
- Three direct Pen widths: 1.5 pt, 3 pt, 5.5 pt.
- Color and width are selected independently; selecting either activates Pen. Pen tool/color/width are remembered locally.
- Undo/Redo buttons are in the annotation strip and share the existing document history.
- Coalesced pointer samples are consumed where available. Draw-with-Finger remains off.

Ink data architecture:
- Workbench ink is non-destructive page-local vector stroke data stored with each page. Each stroke contains an id, tool/type, color, width, opacity, and page-local point list.
- Stored coordinates are in the Workbench page's unrotated base-canvas coordinate system (PDF points, top-left origin), not screen pixels. Rendering maps those coordinates through current Workbench page rotation and zoom. Export maps them back through the source PDF's inherited page rotation/crop box.
- Same-document split panes share document content but preserve independent view state. Live strokes are mirrored into another rendered instance of the same page.
- Page state cloning was made annotation-aware so history, persistence, templates, duplication, page copy, Combine, backup restore, and backup-as-subtree do not accidentally alias mutable stroke arrays.
- Duplicate + notes carries Workbench ink. Duplicate clean omits Workbench ink.
- Page-size fit/center applies a proportional fit/center transform to existing Workbench ink. Margin/crop changes shift ink with the page content; resetting edge adjustments shifts it back. Page rotation needs no data mutation because rendering/export applies rotation to the page-local coordinates.

Export:
- Any page containing Workbench ink prevents untouched-byte passthrough for that document. An unannotated untouched source PDF remains byte-for-byte passthrough.
- Structural export adds Workbench strokes as vector PDF line/circle drawing operations; pages are not rasterized just to export ink.
- Source PDFs still use one batched copyPages call per source, preserving shared resources.
- Generated pages and image pages also receive Workbench ink before final page rotation is applied.

NEXT ANNOTATION PRIORITIES (user-confirmed):
1. Partial-stroke eraser. The user definitely wants erasing portions of strokes eventually; whole-stroke-only erasing is not an acceptable final endpoint.
2. Lasso/select with move and RESIZE as a high priority. Resizing is frequently used. Include delete and copy/duplicate.
3. Highlighter with its own quick palette: yellow, pinkish, bluish/cyan, greenish, and its own three widths. Pen and Highlighter remember separate color/width state.
4. Recolor selected annotations is useful but low-frequency.
5. Do NOT prioritize changing selected-stroke width or opacity; the user says they never use those operations.
6. Images as annotation objects come after annotation tools are mature. Eventually images should be movable/resizable/copyable/deletable/rotatable.
7. Future toolbar options: Always visible vs Auto-hide; possibly Top/Left/Right edge placement. Do not make the toolbar freely draggable initially.
8. Future optional light pen colors/color picker for dark PDF backgrounds.
9. Future Presentation Laser Pointer and possible palm-safe handling/auto-hide of Full Page bottom navigation.

HIGH-PRIORITY 5.0.0 TESTS:
- Surface Pen and Apple Pencil strokes at Fit Width/Fit Page and multiple zoom levels.
- Finger pan/pinch while Pen is selected; touch must not draw.
- Pen UI controls must accept stylus taps.
- View <-> Presentation with annotated pages; logical page/scroll anchors must remain stable.
- Single/Split and same-document split with independent page/zoom/scroll state.
- Library persistence and cross-platform editable backup restore of annotations.
- PDF export placement/color/width on ordinary, rotated, mixed-size, CropBox, generated graph/blank, and image pages.
- Re-run the 44-page slide-deck size regression unannotated and with a small annotation.
- Pages thumbnails and Insert previews should display Workbench ink; Duplicate clean preview should omit it.

KNOWN INTENTIONAL LIMITS OF 5.0.0:
- No eraser yet.
- No lasso/select/resize yet.
- No highlighter yet.
- No pressure-sensitive width yet.
- No finger drawing.
- Presentation annotation strip is always visible; auto-hide setting is deferred.
- Hand/View prevents stylus ink but does not yet promise stylus-drag panning; finger navigation remains the established navigation path.

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 4.2.1

MILESTONE 4.2.1 — EXPORT EFFICIENCY + DIRECTORY WORKFLOWS
---------------------------------------------------------
Cross-platform 4.2.0 testing succeeded on desktop, Surface, Chromebook, and iPad. Editable backups exported on one device restored correctly on another, and whole-Library PDF ZIP export worked. iPad required a `.zip` extension for selecting backup files, so editable backups now use `.pwbbackup.zip`; Restore accepts ZIP files and validates the manifest rather than relying on a custom extension.

PDF EXPORT SIZE REGRESSION / FIX:
- Testing found that an imported PDF could become dramatically larger on export even with no meaningful content change.
- Concrete regression case: a 44-page slide deck stores one large background/grid image once and references it from 43 pages; the previous Workbench exporter called pdf-lib `copyPages()` separately for each output page, causing shared resources to be recopied repeatedly.
- 4.2.1 returns original source bytes unchanged when a document is a completely untouched, full, in-order single-source PDF.
- For edited PDFs, all page occurrences from each source PDF are now copied in one batch per source so pdf-lib uses one resource-copy map and preserves sharing of repeated images/fonts/resources.
- This common build path benefits Export, Extract, Split, Combine, Library PDF ZIP export, and Preserve compression. Rasterize compression is intentionally different.

DIRECTORY / LIBRARY WORKFLOWS ADDED:
- Import/Open while in Files stays in Files and stores imported files in the currently browsed Library folder.
- Local Library has an Import files entry plus Import PDF folder ZIP; the latter recreates ZIP directory/subdirectory paths beneath the current folder.
- Clicking/tapping a Library PDF thumbnail/title opens that document and switches to View.
- Folder rows support Export PDFs (ZIP preserving the selected folder hierarchy) and Trash.
- Trashing a folder moves its complete subtree together; Restore restores the subtree; Permanent Delete removes the subtree and can export changed documents as a ZIP first.
- Editable backup can now be imported non-destructively as a new Library subtree. All internal IDs/source IDs/page IDs are remapped; existing Library data is retained; imported templates receive duplicate-safe names.

BACKUP NAMING:
- Write editable backups as `.pwbbackup.zip`.
- Restore picker accepts `.zip`; validation is based on `manifest.json`. Bare `.pwbbackup` support is intentionally not carried forward.

PENDING BEFORE INKING:
- Audit preservation of existing PDF hyperlinks and outlines/bookmarks through edits/export.
- Final Files UI placement cleanup (New Folder / Refresh and other actions may move once the feature set is final).
- Broader schema migration/recovery testing.
- Favorites/search/sophisticated sorting remain lower priority.
- Then Milestone 5: Pen, Highlighter, Eraser, Select/Lasso transforms, presets/View-Hand mode, laser pointer, and later image annotations.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 4.2.0 CURRENT UPDATE
====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 4.2.1
Visible About version: 4.2.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m4.2.0-v1
IndexedDB database version: 2
Library schema version: 3
Editable backup format version: 1

MILESTONE 4.2.0 — LIBRARY BACKUP / PORTABILITY
----------------------------------------------
Milestone 4.1.1 was tested successfully on Surface and iPad, including migration of older Library data, folders/subfolders, rename, move, duplicate, thumbnails, and persistence. 4.2.0 builds on that validated Library.

New Files → Library backup & export section:
- Export Library as PDFs (.zip): exports every non-Trash Library document as a conventional PDF while preserving folder/subfolder paths inside the ZIP.
- Persistent templates are included as one-page PDFs in an `_Templates` folder.
- Empty Library folders are represented as ZIP directories where supported.
- This PDF archive is human-readable/software-independent and is NOT the editable restore format.

Editable Library backup:
- `Back up editable Library` creates a ZIP-based `.pwbbackup` file.
- Backup contains all Library document records INCLUDING Trash state, folder hierarchy, persistent templates, original PDF/image source bytes, page edits/geometry/generated pages, undo/redo page snapshots, and saved open/single/split view session state.
- Current small local preferences (scroll mode, fit mode, Library Grid/List choice) are included.
- Backup file contains `manifest.json` plus binary source payloads under `sources/`.

Editable restore:
- Restore validates the backup format/schema before touching the current Library.
- Future/unsupported backup/schema versions are refused rather than guessed.
- Every document/template source reference is checked against the manifest.
- Every binary payload is read successfully before replacement begins.
- User receives an explicit warning that restore REPLACES the current Local Library and should back up first if needed.
- After validation, documents/sources/folders/meta are replaced in one IndexedDB transaction, then supported preferences are restored and PDF Workbench reloads into the backup's saved session.
- Pagehide persistence is suppressed during the restore reload so the pre-restore in-memory session cannot overwrite the restored session.

Milestone 4 UI status:
- Open Documents and Local Library remain collapsed by default.
- Template Manager is available both from Files and Insert Page.
- New Folder/Refresh placement is intentionally not finalized; user said it can wait until the Files system is more complete.
- Favorites/search/sophisticated sorting remain lower priority.

NEXT MILESTONE 4 HARDENING
--------------------------
1. Test whole-Library PDF archive and editable backup/restore across Surface, iPad Safari/PWA, and Chromebook.
2. Continue schema migration/recovery hardening as schema changes become necessary.
3. Audit preservation of existing PDF hyperlinks and outlines/bookmarks through structural edits/exports.
4. Consider page bookmark/outline UI foundation.
5. Final Files/Library UI cleanup after functionality settles.
6. Then move to Milestone 5 inking/annotation.

- Highlighter as first-class tool.
- A small number of configurable pen presets.

--------------------------------------
Milestone 4.0.2 persistence was tested successfully on Surface, iPad Safari, and the installed iPad Home Screen PWA. Documents can be opened, closed/reopened, trashed/restored, and retained across repeated app closes/reopens. Persistent templates also survive. Existing Safari Library data survived application upgrades.

4.1.0 adds the first organization layer:
- Files → Local Library is collapsible and CLOSED by default.
- Files → Open Documents is collapsible and CLOSED by default.
- Local Library supports folders and nested subfolders.
- New folder creates inside the currently viewed folder.
- Breadcrumb navigation exposes Library root and ancestors.
- Folders can be renamed and moved to root/another folder. Folder moves cannot target the folder itself or one of its descendants.
- Documents can be renamed, duplicated, and moved between folders/root.
- Duplicate creates a separate editable Library record with fresh document/page IDs and no shared undo history. It intentionally reuses immutable source PDF/image data internally instead of duplicating source bytes.
- Documents retain the existing Open/Use/Close/Trash behavior.
- Library Grid and List views are available; Grid is the default and is remembered locally.
- First-page thumbnails are rendered lazily for Library documents, including closed documents, so the Library is visually recognizable without eagerly opening every source.
- Files now includes a Templates section linking to the persistent Template Manager.
- Manage templates remains in Insert Page; both entry points open the same manager.

PERSISTENCE / MIGRATION
-----------------------
- IndexedDB database version increased from 1 to 2 solely to add a `folders` object store.
- Library schema increased from 2 to 3 for folder-aware organization.
- Existing 4.0.x document records remain valid; documents with no folderId remain at Library root.
- Normal upgrades must preserve the Local Library. Do NOT make purge/reset part of ordinary upgrade flow.
- Service-worker/application cache remains separate from persistent Library data.

UI PRIORITIES CONFIRMED
-----------------------
- User prefers expanded page-operation rows in Pages on iPad rather than hiding page tools behind More.
- Favorites, search, and sophisticated sorting are lower priorities.
- First-page thumbnails are considered worthwhile; they motivated implementing Grid/List now.
- Folder/subfolder organization, rename, duplicate, move, close/reopen, export, backup, and eventual annotation persistence are primary Library goals.

MILESTONE 4 ROADMAP AFTER 4.1.0
-------------------------------
High priority remaining:
1. Whole-Library PDF export as a ZIP preserving folder/subfolder hierarchy.
2. Full editable PDF Workbench Library backup and restore, preserving sources, page edits, templates, annotations later, folder hierarchy, and relevant view/project state.
3. Stronger schema migration/recovery and storage cleanup tools.
4. Folder Trash/recovery semantics if needed; current document Trash remains working.
5. Page bookmarks/outlines foundation and audit preservation of existing PDF hyperlinks/outlines through structural editing/export.
6. Crash-safe incremental persistence, storage reporting/persistent-storage request remain permanent requirements.

Lower priority / can defer:
- Favorites.
- richer search and sorting controls.
- advanced bulk Library actions.
- future content/OCR/handwriting search index.

MILESTONE 5 INKING ROADMAP (PRESERVE)
-------------------------------------
- Pen, Eraser, Lasso/Select, Undo/Redo.
- Highlighter as first-class tool.
- A small number of configurable pen presets.
- Explicit View/Hand (non-writing) state.
- Pen/stylus inks on document content; one finger pans; two fingers pinch/zoom; pen can operate visible UI.
- Draw with Finger OFF by default.
- Auto-return from eraser option.
- Optional multi-finger undo/redo gestures.
- Laser Pointer for Presentation.
- Eventually annotated-pages filter in Pages.
- Full Page bottom navigation controls must be tested for palm interference once inking exists.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 4.1.1
-----------------------
- Upgrade an existing 4.0.2 Library without losing documents/templates.
- Nested folders on Surface, iPad Safari/PWA, Chromebook.
- Close/reopen documents after moving them into folders.
- Duplicate an edited document; verify copy is independent and exports correctly.
- First-page thumbnails for PDFs, scans/JBIG2, generated pages, and image documents.
- Grid/List responsiveness on iPad portrait/landscape and narrow Surface.
- Folder move + breadcrumb navigation.

PREVIOUS HANDOFF / FULL PROJECT HISTORY FOLLOWS

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — CURRENT PROJECT HANDOFF
==============================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 4.0.2
Visible About version: 4.0.2
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m4.0.2-v1
Library schema version: 2

WHY 4.0.2 EXISTS
----------------
Milestone 4.0.1 persistent Library testing succeeded on Surface and in iPad Safari, but the installed iPad Home Screen PWA could fail to initialize the Local Library: the Library stayed empty, documents disappeared after leaving/reopening the PWA, and templates were unavailable after relaunch. 4.0.2 is a focused WebKit/PWA persistence hardening release plus small Files UI cleanup.

PERSISTENCE HARDENING
---------------------
- Keep IndexedDB as the primary Local Library store and keep it separate from the service-worker/app-shell cache.
- On Apple WebKit, issue a harmless IndexedDB warm-up open before the real Library database. This targets a documented WebKit first-open/home-screen race where indexedDB.open() can remain pending.
- Use bounded database-open timeouts and automatic retries rather than allowing Library initialization to hang indefinitely.
- Reconnect and retry once if the IndexedDB server connection is lost during persistence.
- On returning to the foreground/pageshow, recheck/reconnect Local Library storage and commit current state.
- Files → Local Library → Refresh also acts as an explicit reconnect/retry control.
- Attach transaction completion handlers before issuing writes/deletes/clears to avoid WebKit transaction timing races.
- New PDF/image source payloads are stored as ArrayBuffer structured-clone data rather than Blob/File records. Blob records from 4.0.0/4.0.1 remain readable for backward compatibility.
- If the first PWA Library initialization fails, automatically retry shortly afterward and show a useful status instead of silently operating without persistence.

PERSISTENT TEMPLATES
--------------------
- Templates now persist through the same Local Library layer; they are no longer session-only.
- Template names/page records and any PDF/image source bytes they reference are persisted.
- Restored templates return in Insert Page, Manage Templates, and Files → New → From template after reopening the app.
- Rename/delete changes are persisted.
- Delete local Library / Factory reset removes persistent templates.

FILES UI
--------
- Local Library is now collapsible (open by default).
- Open Documents is now collapsible (open by default).
- Trash and all document-level operations remain separate collapsible sections.

CURRENT LIBRARY SEMANTICS
-------------------------
- Imported/created working documents persist locally.
- Close and More → Close all files affect only the active workspace and do not prompt for PDF export.
- Local Library supports Open/Use/Close and per-document Trash.
- Trash persists across app restarts and supports Restore and Delete permanently.
- Permanent deletion of a document with unexported changes offers Export PDF & delete, Delete permanently, or Cancel.
- Storage & reset includes request persistent storage, usage/quota reporting, Delete local Library, and Factory reset all local data.
- Library schema remains 2 (trashedAt). 4.0.2 extends meta/source storage compatibly without requiring a schema-number bump.

MILESTONE 4 ROADMAP
-------------------
4.1 — Library organization/daily management:
- folders and subfolders
- rename and duplicate documents
- move documents/folders
- Favorites
- filename/folder search
- sort by name/modified/created
- grid/list Library views
- bulk Library actions
- page bookmarks/document outlines foundation

4.2 — Backup/portability/storage hardening:
- export entire Library as PDFs in hierarchy-preserving ZIP
- full editable PDF Workbench Library backup/restore
- schema migrations/version checks
- backup-first reset flow
- hyperlink/outline preservation audit
- future PDF-text/OCR/handwriting search-index fields
- orphaned-source cleanup/storage hardening

MILESTONE 5 INKING ROADMAP
--------------------------
Pen/Eraser/Lasso/Undo/Redo plus Highlighter, a few configurable pen presets, explicit View/Hand mode, auto-return eraser, optional multi-finger undo/redo, Laser Pointer for Presentation, pressure/coalesced/predicted smooth ink, palm rejection, editable annotation objects, vector PDF export, and an annotated-pages filter later. Stylus writes; one finger pans; two fingers pinch/zoom; Draw with Finger defaults off.

PRESERVE / DO NOT REGRESS
-------------------------
Do not regress validated Milestone 3 features: PDF export/ZIP, Extract/Split/Combine, compression and Letter-canvas normalization, Images → PDF, page copy between documents, Page size and Crop/margins, blank/graph/template insertion, thumbnail template chooser/manager, Continuous/Page Snap/Full Page, Single/Split, Presentation, same-document split anchoring, finger/pen separation, and PDF.js JBIG2/WASM configuration.

TEST PRIORITY FOR 4.0.2
-----------------------
On the installed iPad Home Screen PWA itself: open a disposable PDF, verify it immediately appears in Local Library, save a template, fully leave/reopen the PWA, verify both document and template return, Close/Reopen the document from Local Library, and test Trash/Restore. If storage does not initialize, press Local Library → Refresh and record the exact status text.

RELEASE PRACTICE
----------------
Every distribution ZIP must include this handoff, README, and graph-paper-reference.png. About version and service-worker cache version must stay synchronized.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.9.1 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.9.1
Visible About version: 3.9.1
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.9.1-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Milestone 3.9.0 compression was tested successfully, especially explicit Rasterize mode on student scans. Already-optimized PDFs can remain the same size or become larger after raster rebuilding; that is acceptable and should not cause silent destructive behavior. The user's common compression workflow is standardizing student-submitted scans, which may have inconsistent page dimensions.

3.9.1 LETTER-CANVAS NORMALIZATION DURING COMPRESSION
-----------------------------------------------------
1. Add an optional Files → Compress checkbox: Normalize pages to Letter canvas.
2. The checkbox affects only the compressed output and does not modify the open workbench document.
3. Every output page becomes US Letter in the page's current orientation: 612×792 pt portrait or 792×612 pt landscape.
4. Oversized pages are scaled down proportionally to fit within Letter without cropping.
5. Smaller pages are NOT enlarged; their existing physical content size is retained and centered on the Letter canvas with white space around it.
6. Mixed portrait/landscape documents choose Letter orientation independently per page.
7. The option works with both Preserve and Rasterize compression. Preserve keeps page content structural/vector where the existing compressor does; Rasterize applies the normalized Letter canvas before rendering the final JPEG-backed pages.
8. Preserve target-size accounting and Raster target-size accounting use the normalized output when the checkbox is enabled.
9. Keep the option off by default so ordinary compression does not silently change page dimensions.

PRESERVE
--------
Do not regress validated 3.9.0 compression, 3.8 image assembly, 3.7 page geometry/crop/export, 3.6 page copy, 3.5 templates/Presentation/view state, split-pane independence, touch/pen separation, Files Export/Extract/Split/Combine, page insertion, or PDF.js JBIG2/WASM configuration.

TEST PRIORITIES
---------------
- Student-scan PDF with mixed scanner page sizes: Rasterize Medium + Letter normalization.
- Verify every output page reports 8.5×11 or 11×8.5 in in Adobe.
- Small page: verify centered and not enlarged.
- Oversized/Legal page: verify reduced proportionally and centered.
- Mixed portrait/landscape pages: verify per-page orientation choice.
- Preserve-mode selectable-text PDF + Letter normalization: verify text remains selectable.
- Checkbox off: verify 3.9.0 output behavior is unchanged.

NEXT ROADMAP
------------
After compression is stable, begin Milestone 4 persistent Files/Library: stored working documents/projects, close/reopen, Recent, folders, rename/move/delete, persistent templates, and backup/restore. Inking/annotations follows.

RELEASE PRACTICE
----------------

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.9.0 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.9.0
Visible About version: 3.9.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.9.0-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Milestone 3.8.1 Images → PDF is working. The next planned manipulation feature is compression/target file size. Compression must not silently rasterize selectable text merely to reduce file size.

3.9.0 COMPRESSION
-----------------
1. Add a Files → Compress expandable operation that uses the same checked-document selection model as Export and Combine.
2. One checked document saves one compressed PDF. Multiple checked documents are compressed individually and packaged into one ZIP for one save/share action, especially on iPad.
3. Methods:
   - Preserve text / vector content (default/recommended).
   - Rasterize pages (explicit destructive option).
4. Preserve mode keeps opened PDF source pages structural. It downscales/recompresses imported image pages as JPEG according to the selected level. In this first structure-preserving implementation, raster images already embedded inside an opened PDF are retained as-is; scan-heavy opened PDFs may therefore shrink little unless Rasterize is explicitly selected.
5. Rasterize mode renders each final edited page and rebuilds the PDF from JPEG page images. This can strongly compress scans but removes selectable/searchable text, live links/forms, and vector sharpness. Never invoke this path silently.
6. Levels: Light, Medium, Strong, and Maximum file size. Maximum size is interpreted per output PDF when multiple documents are selected.
7. Target-size Preserve mode progressively lowers imported-image settings. If the target still cannot be met, stop without downloading and tell the user to choose Rasterize explicitly.
8. Target-size Rasterize mode progressively lowers raster DPI/JPEG quality. If even the lowest safe setting cannot meet the target, stop rather than falsely claiming the maximum was achieved.
9. Raster work is page-at-a-time with bounded canvas pixel count to reduce iPad/Chromebook/Surface memory pressure.
10. Keep all processing local in the browser.

PRESERVE
--------
Do not regress the validated 3.8 image assembly and numbered names, 3.7 page geometry/crop/export, 3.6 page copy, 3.5 templates/Presentation/view state, split-pane independence, touch/pen separation, Files Export/Extract/Split/Combine, page insertion, or PDF.js JBIG2/WASM configuration.

TEST PRIORITIES
---------------
- Images → PDF document: compare Preserve Light/Medium/Strong size and quality.
- Ordinary selectable-text PDF in Preserve: verify text remains selectable.
- Scan-heavy PDF (including BaakeScan if available) with explicit Rasterize: verify size reduction and visual correctness.
- Strict target in Preserve that cannot be met: verify no silent rasterization/download.
- Strict target in Rasterize: verify output is <= target when feasible.
- Multi-document compression: verify one ZIP with one compressed PDF per checked document.
- iPad/Surface/Chromebook memory/progress behavior on raster compression.

NEXT ROADMAP
------------
After compression is stable, begin Milestone 4 persistent Files/Library: stored working documents/projects, close/reopen, Recent, folders, rename/move/delete, persistent templates, and backup/restore. Inking/annotations follows.

RELEASE PRACTICE
----------------
Every distribution ZIP must include this handoff, README, and graph-paper-reference.png and keep About/service-worker versions synchronized.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.8.0 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.8.0
Visible About version: 3.8.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.8.0-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Milestone 3.7.1 crop/margins was tested successfully, including multiple sizes/orientations and export verification in Adobe. The Pages interface also remains comfortable on iPad in both orientations, and the user prefers keeping its expanded rows of page-operation buttons. The next roadmap item is improved image-to-PDF assembly.

3.8.0 IMAGES → PDF
------------------
1. Add a dedicated Files → Images → PDF expandable section. Do not change the normal Open semantics: Open continues to treat separately selected images as separate documents.
2. The image-assembly picker accepts multiple browser-readable images and can be used repeatedly to append additional images before creation.
3. Show a thumbnail, filename, and pixel dimensions for every pending image.
4. Allow up/down ordering and removal before document creation; Clear images removes the pending list.
5. New document settings: document name; US Letter, A4, US Legal, or image-aspect page size; Auto/Portrait/Landscape orientation.
6. Auto orientation chooses portrait/landscape separately from each image's natural aspect. Forced orientation changes the target page canvas; it does not rotate/crop the source image.
7. Image-aspect pages preserve the source aspect ratio and bound the long page edge to 11 inches (792 pt), avoiding unbounded PDF pages based directly on camera pixel dimensions.
8. Imported images are fit proportionally and centered with no cropping. Existing Pages geometry tools can subsequently normalize/crop/add margins if desired.
9. Creating the assembly produces one normal editable working document and switches to Pages for further arrangement.
10. Each image remains a source object rather than being pre-rasterized into a whole PDF page. JPEG/PNG export can embed the original image bytes; other browser-readable formats may be converted to PNG at image-page export time.
11. Resulting image pages must continue to participate in reorder/rotate/duplicate/delete, Page size, Crop/margins, Copy to…, templates, Combine, Extract, Split, and Export.

PRESERVE
--------
Do not regress the validated 3.7.x geometry/export path, the expanded iPad Pages interface, 3.6 copy workflow, 3.5 viewer/Presentation/template behavior, independent split state, finger/pen separation, multi-document Files operations, or JBIG2/WASM support.

TEST PRIORITIES
---------------
- Multiple JPEG/PNG images selected in one picker action.
- Add a second batch before creating the document.
- Reorder/remove/clear pending images.
- Mixed portrait/landscape images with Auto orientation.
- Forced portrait/landscape, Letter/A4/Legal/image-aspect page sizes.
- Export/reopen in Adobe and verify page sizes/orientations/content.
- iPad/Surface/Chromebook multi-image picker and thumbnail-list layout.
- Resulting pages with normal Pages geometry/transfer/template/output operations.

NEXT ROADMAP
------------
After image assembly is stable, the next planned manipulation area is compression/target file size, followed by the persistent Files/Library system (stored documents, close/reopen, folders, recent items, persistent templates, backup/restore), then inking/annotations.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.7.1 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.7.1
Visible About version: 3.7.1
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.7.1-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
Milestone 3.7.0 page-size/orientation normalization was tested successfully on mixed-size, pre-rotated, and CropBox/MediaBox PDFs. Exports reopened correctly in Adobe with the expected properties. The next requested geometry feature is crop/margins, and the user prefers these relatively rare tools to remain confined to Pages.

3.7.1 CROP / MARGINS
---------------------
1. Add a Pages-only “Crop / margins…” command.
2. Scope can be selected pages or all pages.
3. Operations: Add margins / expand canvas, or Crop page edges.
4. Presets: 1/4 inch, 1/2 inch, 1 inch; Custom permits independent Top/Right/Bottom/Left values.
5. Edge names are visual: Top/Right/Bottom/Left mean the sides as currently viewed, even after Workbench rotation. Internally these are mapped back to the page's pre-Workbench base orientation so later rotation carries the crop/margins with the page.
6. Margins add white page canvas and do not scale current content. Crop moves the page boundary inward and clips content; it also does not scale content.
7. Repeated edge operations accumulate and participate in normal Pages Undo/Redo.
8. “Reset crop/margins” clears edge adjustments while preserving 3.7.0 Page-size normalization.
9. Page size establishes an exact new target canvas and clears prior edge adjustments. Apply Page size first, then Crop/margins, when both are desired.
10. Page instances carry edge adjustments through duplicate, templates, Copy to…, Combine, Extract, Split, and Export.
11. PDF export preserves vector/text structure: after any 3.7.0 normalization, page boxes are expanded/cropped structurally. Do not rasterize PDF pages for these operations.
12. Image and generated pages use the same final canvas/edge model; graph paper remains generated vector content in export.
13. Geometry changes invalidate stale raw viewer scroll offsets while retaining logical page identity.

PRESERVE
--------
Do not regress the validated 3.7.0 normalization/export path or the stable 3.5.x/3.6.x viewer, split, Presentation, template, transfer, Files, touch/pen, and JBIG2/WASM behavior.

TEST PRIORITIES
---------------
- Symmetric and one-sided margins on ordinary, rotated, and normalized pages.
- One-sided crop; verify clipping rather than scaling.
- Source-rotated pages and Workbench-rotated pages.
- CropBox/MediaBox PDFs.
- Graph paper and image/scan pages.
- Export/reopen in Adobe and verify final page dimensions.
- Undo/Redo and Reset crop/margins.
- Copy/template/Combine/Extract/Split with adjusted pages.

----------------

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.7.0 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.7.0
Visible About version: 3.7.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.7.0-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
3.6.1 page copying tested successfully. The next roadmap area is page geometry/normalization. The user noted these tools will be used relatively rarely, so they should remain confined to Pages rather than adding permanent controls to View/Presentation/Files.

3.7.0 PAGE SIZE / CANVAS NORMALIZATION
--------------------------------------
1. Add a Pages-only “Page size…” command.
2. Scope can be selected pages or all pages. If nothing is selected, all pages is the default.
3. Target size presets: US Letter, US Legal, A4, match first-page size, and custom dimensions in inches.
4. Orientation: preserve each page orientation, force portrait, or force landscape.
5. Placement in 3.7.0 is intentionally simple: preserve aspect ratio, fit proportionally, center, and do not crop.
6. Store the target canvas non-destructively on each page (`canvasWidth` / `canvasHeight`) rather than replacing source dimensions. This means Undo/Redo and later resizing can reuse original source geometry.
7. The target canvas follows page copies/templates/Combine because page instances are cloned structurally.
8. Viewer, thumbnails, split panes, and template previews use the normalized page canvas dimensions.
9. PDF export does not rasterize PDF source pages for normalization. It scales copied page content and annotations and adjusts page boxes to produce the target canvas. Images remain image content; generated graph paper remains vector generated content.
10. Crop, margin expansion, and more advanced placement controls remain for a later 3.7.x revision.

PRESERVE
--------
Do not regress the stable 3.5.x/3.6.x behavior: responsive toolbar, View↔Presentation anchoring, independent split panes, same-document structural-edit anchoring, visual template chooser/manager, session templates, pen/finger separation, Files operations, and JBIG2/WASM support.

PDF WORKBENCH PWA — MILESTONE 3.6.0 CURRENT UPDATE
=====================================================

CURRENT BUILD: Milestone 3.6.0
Visible About version: 3.6.0
Service-worker/cache version: pdf-workbench-m3.6.0-v1

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS
------------------------
After 3.5.3 was visually stable across Surface, iPad, and Chromebook, the next requested document-manipulation feature was copying selected pages from one open document to another. The user explicitly said cross-document drag-and-drop is not needed now and can be deferred.

3.6.0 PAGE COPY BETWEEN DOCUMENTS
---------------------------------
1. Pages now has a **Copy to…** action when one or more pages are selected and at least one other document is open.
2. The transfer dialog chooses another open destination document.
3. Destination placement options are:
   - beginning of document
   - end of document
   - after a specified destination page number
4. Selected pages are copied in their current source Pages order.
5. The source document is unchanged.
6. Copies preserve the page model: PDF/image/generated content reference, dimensions, base rotation, and user rotation. No rasterization is introduced.
7. Source data remains shared safely through the existing source-reference model, so closing the original document does not invalidate copied pages that remain in another open document.
8. Copy changes only the destination and is recorded as one destination-document history operation, so normal Undo/Redo remains coherent.
9. Stored destination view state retains its logical active page. If the copy is inserted before that page, stale raw scroll offsets are invalidated so a later render does not jump to the wrong logical content.

MOVE / DRAG POLICY
------------------
- Do NOT add cross-document thumbnail drag-and-drop unless later use shows a real need.
- A true Move-to-document command is also deferred for now. Move changes source and destination simultaneously; add it when the undo architecture can represent an atomic cross-document transaction rather than allowing one document to undo only half of a move.

CONFIRMED BEHAVIOR TO PRESERVE
------------------------------
- 3.5.3 responsive toolbar looks substantially better in iPad portrait and narrow Surface windows; Chromebook portrait/landscape and iPad landscape were already good.
- View ↔ Presentation preserves logical position in all three modes on Surface.
- Split View preserves both panes in both directions.
- Same-document split structural edits preserve the inactive pane.
- Pages/template source selection and visual thumbnails work.
- Saving a template in Presentation stays in Presentation.
- Finger pan/pinch and pen-reserved-for-ink behavior remain stable across tested devices.
- Export/Extract/Split/Combine and structural PDF export remain validated.

IMMEDIATE 3.6.0 TESTS
---------------------
1. Copy one page between two open documents to Beginning, End, and After page N.
2. Copy several nonconsecutive pages and confirm source-order preservation.
3. Switch to the destination and test Undo/Redo.
4. Copy rotated, mixed-size, graph-paper, scan, and JBIG2-backed pages and export the destination.

WHY THIS REVISION EXISTS

3.5.1 FIXES
-----------
1. VIEW ↔ PRESENTATION LOGICAL ANCHOR
- Before changing Presentation state, capture the logical page/content point at the center of each viewer.
- Do not transfer raw pre-layout scroll pixels across the viewport-size change.
- Rebuild the viewer, then restore the same page/content point after layout settles.
- Apply independently to left/right panes in split mode.
- Full Page preserves the same active page.

2. PRESENTATION-SAFE TEMPLATE NAMING
- Do not use window.prompt() for Save as Template or Rename Template.
- Use an in-app modal naming dialog instead.
- Keep the automatic generic Template 1, Template 2, ... name prefilled.
- This avoids the browser prompt/native-fullscreen interaction that can make Surface leave Presentation.

3. PAGES SELECTION IS AUTHORITATIVE
- When Insert/Save Template is invoked from Pages, derive the source/anchor from the actual current Pages selection before any retained View/split state.
- If exactly one selected page remains after another page is unchecked, that remaining page is authoritative.
- Do not synchronize an old split pane merely because split mode was last used when the current workspace is Pages.
- Do not fall back to stale View current-page state when a real Pages selection exists.

IMPORTANT PRESERVED TEST RESULTS
--------------------------------
- 3.4.4 same-document split structural-edit anchoring was reported working in Continuous, Page Snap, Full Page, and all corresponding Presentation variants.
